### Q1. MHT CET 2026 (20 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $\vec{a}, \vec{b}, \vec{c}$ are unit vectors satisfying $\vec{a} - (\sqrt{3})\vec{b} + \vec{c} = \vec{0}$, then the angle between $\vec{a}$ and $\vec{c}$ is...

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{\pi}{6}$
- **(B)** $\dfrac{\pi}{3}$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{\pi}{4}$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{\pi}{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\dfrac{\pi}{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $\vec{a} - \sqrt{3}\vec{b} + \vec{c} = \vec{0}$

$\vec{a} + \vec{c} = \sqrt{3}\vec{b}$

Squaring both sides, we get:

$|\vec{a} + \vec{c}|^2 = |\sqrt{3}\vec{b}|^2$

$|\vec{a}|^2 + |\vec{c}|^2 + 2\vec{a} \cdot \vec{c} = 3|\vec{b}|^2$

Since $\vec{a}, \vec{b}, \vec{c}$ are unit vectors, $|\vec{a}| = |\vec{b}| = |\vec{c}| = 1$.

$1 + 1 + 2|\vec{a}||\vec{c}|\cos\theta = 3(1)^2$

$2 + 2\cos\theta = 3$

$2\cos\theta = 1$

$\cos\theta = \dfrac{1}{2}$

$\theta = \dfrac{\pi}{3}$

Answer: $\dfrac{\pi}{3}$

</details>

### Q2. MHT CET 2026 (20 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

In $\triangle ABC$, $P$ is a point on side $BC$ such that $BP:PC = 4:1$ and $Q$ is a point on the segment $AP$ such that $AQ:QP = 3:2$. If $\vec{BQ}$ is expressed as $\vec{BQ} = x\vec{BA} + y\vec{BC}$, then the value of $x + y$ is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{22}{25}$
- **(B)** $\dfrac{3}{5}$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{17}{25}$
- **(D)** $1$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\dfrac{22}{25}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the position vector of $B$ be $\vec{0}$. Then the position vectors of $A$ and $C$ with respect to $B$ are $\vec{BA}$ and $\vec{BC}$ respectively.

Since $P$ divides $BC$ in the ratio $4:1$, the position vector of $P$ is given by:
$\vec{BP} = \dfrac{1 \cdot \vec{0} + 4 \cdot \vec{BC}}{4 + 1} = \dfrac{4}{5}\vec{BC}$

Since $Q$ divides $AP$ in the ratio $3:2$, the position vector of $Q$ is given by:
$\vec{BQ} = \dfrac{2 \cdot \vec{BA} + 3 \cdot \vec{BP}}{3 + 2} = \dfrac{2}{5}\vec{BA} + \dfrac{3}{5}\vec{BP}$

Substituting $\vec{BP} = \dfrac{4}{5}\vec{BC}$ into the equation for $\vec{BQ}$:
$\vec{BQ} = \dfrac{2}{5}\vec{BA} + \dfrac{3}{5} \left( \dfrac{4}{5}\vec{BC} \right) = \dfrac{2}{5}\vec{BA} + \dfrac{12}{25}\vec{BC}$

Comparing this with the given expression $\vec{BQ} = x\vec{BA} + y\vec{BC}$, we get:
$x = \dfrac{2}{5}$ and $y = \dfrac{12}{25}$

Therefore, the value of $x + y$ is:
$x + y = \dfrac{2}{5} + \dfrac{12}{25} = \dfrac{10}{25} + \dfrac{12}{25} = \dfrac{22}{25}$

Answer: $\dfrac{22}{25}$

</details>

### Q3. MHT CET 2026 (20 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $8\overline{AB} - 3\overline{AC} - 5\overline{AD} = \overline{O}$, then the ratio in which the point $B$ divides segment $CD$ is ....

**Options:**

- **(A)** $8:3$
- **(B)** $8:5$
- **(C)** $5:3$
- **(D)** $3:4$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $5:3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $8\overline{AB} - 3\overline{AC} - 5\overline{AD} = \overline{O}$

Let the position vectors of points $A, B, C, D$ be $\vec{a}, \vec{b}, \vec{c}, \vec{d}$ respectively.

Substituting the position vectors:

$8(\vec{b} - \vec{a}) - 3(\vec{c} - \vec{a}) - 5(\vec{d} - \vec{a}) = \vec{0}$

$8\vec{b} - 8\vec{a} - 3\vec{c} + 3\vec{a} - 5\vec{d} + 5\vec{a} = \vec{0}$

$8\vec{b} - 3\vec{c} - 5\vec{d} = \vec{0}$

$8\vec{b} = 3\vec{c} + 5\vec{d}$

$\vec{b} = \dfrac{5\vec{d} + 3\vec{c}}{5 + 3}$

By the section formula, the position vector of a point dividing the line segment joining $C(\vec{c})$ and $D(\vec{d})$ in the ratio $m:n$ is given by $\dfrac{m\vec{d} + n\vec{c}}{m + n}$.

Comparing the expressions, $m = 5$ and $n = 3$.

Therefore, the point $B$ divides the segment $CD$ in the ratio $5:3$.

Answer: $5:3$

</details>

### Q4. MHT CET 2026 (20 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If a parallelogram is constructed on vectors $\vec{a} = 3\vec{p} - \vec{q}$, $\vec{b} = \vec{p} + 3\vec{q}$ and $|\vec{p}| = |\vec{q}| = 2$ and the angle between $\vec{p}$ and $\vec{q}$ is $\dfrac{\pi}{3}$, then $|\vec{a}| : |\vec{b}| = \ldots$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{5} : \sqrt{13}$
- **(B)** $\sqrt{7} : \sqrt{13}$
- **(C)** $\sqrt{13} : \sqrt{7}$
- **(D)** $\sqrt{13} : \sqrt{5}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\sqrt{7} : \sqrt{13}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $|\vec{p}| = |\vec{q}| = 2$ and the angle between them is $\dfrac{\pi}{3}$.

The dot product is $\vec{p} \cdot \vec{q} = |\vec{p}||\vec{q}|\cos\left(\dfrac{\pi}{3}\right) = 2(2)\left(\dfrac{1}{2}\right) = 2$.

Now, $|\vec{a}|^2 = |3\vec{p} - \vec{q}|^2 = 9|\vec{p}|^2 + |\vec{q}|^2 - 6(\vec{p} \cdot \vec{q})$.

Substituting the values, $|\vec{a}|^2 = 9(4) + 4 - 6(2) = 36 + 4 - 12 = 28$.

Therefore, $|\vec{a}| = \sqrt{28} = 2\sqrt{7}$.

Similarly, $|\vec{b}|^2 = |\vec{p} + 3\vec{q}|^2 = |\vec{p}|^2 + 9|\vec{q}|^2 + 6(\vec{p} \cdot \vec{q})$.

Substituting the values, $|\vec{b}|^2 = 4 + 9(4) + 6(2) = 4 + 36 + 12 = 52$.

Therefore, $|\vec{b}| = \sqrt{52} = 2\sqrt{13}$.

The ratio is $|\vec{a}| : |\vec{b}| = 2\sqrt{7} : 2\sqrt{13} = \sqrt{7} : \sqrt{13}$.

Answer: $\sqrt{7} : \sqrt{13}$

</details>

### Q5. MHT CET 2026 (19 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

In a triangle PQR, L and M are the points on QR and PR respectively such that $QL = 2 LR$ and $PM = 3 MR$. Let S be the point of intersection of PL and QM; then S divides segment QM internally in the ratio ...

**Options:**

- **(A)** $12 : 5$
- **(B)** $8 : 3$
- **(C)** $5 : 3$
- **(D)** $16 : 9$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $8 : 3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the position vector of $R$ be $\vec{0}$. Let the position vectors of $P$ and $Q$ be $\vec{p}$ and $\vec{q}$ respectively.

Since $L$ divides $QR$ in the ratio $2 : 1$, the position vector of $L$ is:
$\vec{L} = \dfrac{1 \cdot \vec{q} + 2 \cdot \vec{0}}{3} = \dfrac{\vec{q}}{3}$

Since $M$ divides $PR$ in the ratio $3 : 1$, the position vector of $M$ is:
$\vec{M} = \dfrac{1 \cdot \vec{p} + 3 \cdot \vec{0}}{4} = \dfrac{\vec{p}}{4}$

The equation of line $PL$ is given by:
$\vec{r} = (1-t)\vec{p} + t\vec{L} = (1-t)\vec{p} + \dfrac{t}{3}\vec{q}$

The equation of line $QM$ is given by:
$\vec{r} = (1-s)\vec{q} + s\vec{M} = (1-s)\vec{q} + \dfrac{s}{4}\vec{p}$

At the point of intersection $S$, the position vectors must be equal. Equating the coefficients of $\vec{p}$ and $\vec{q}$:
$1 - t = \dfrac{s}{4}$
$\dfrac{t}{3} = 1 - s \Rightarrow t = 3 - 3s$

Substituting $t$ into the first equation:
$1 - (3 - 3s) = \dfrac{s}{4}$
$-2 + 3s = \dfrac{s}{4}$
$\dfrac{11s}{4} = 2 \Rightarrow s = \dfrac{8}{11}$

Substitute $s = \dfrac{8}{11}$ into the equation for line $QM$ to find the position vector of $S$:
$\vec{S} = \left(1 - \dfrac{8}{11}\right)\vec{q} + \dfrac{8}{11}\vec{M} = \dfrac{3\vec{q} + 8\vec{M}}{11}$

This shows that $S$ divides the segment $QM$ internally in the ratio $8 : 3$.

Answer: $8 : 3$

</details>

### Q6. MHT CET 2026 (19 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $\vec{a} = \hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}, \vec{b} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} + 4\hat{k}, \vec{c} = 3\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$ are the position vectors of the vertices $A$, $B$ and $C$ respectively of $\triangle ABC$ and the bisector of $\angle A$ meets side $BC$ at $D$ where $B - D - C$, then $|\vec{AD}|$ is...

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{2\sqrt{30}}{5}$
- **(B)** $\dfrac{2}{3}$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{\sqrt{13}}{5}$
- **(D)** $1$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\dfrac{2\sqrt{30}}{5}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The position vectors of the vertices are given as $\vec{a} = \hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$, $\vec{b} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} + 4\hat{k}$, and $\vec{c} = 3\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$.

The vectors representing the sides $AB$ and $AC$ are:
$\vec{AB} = \vec{b} - \vec{a} = (3-1)\hat{i} + (4-2)\hat{j} + (4-3)\hat{k} = 2\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + \hat{k}$
$\vec{AC} = \vec{c} - \vec{a} = (3-1)\hat{i} + (2-2)\hat{j} + (3-3)\hat{k} = 2\hat{i}$

The lengths of these sides are:
$|\vec{AB}| = \sqrt{2^2 + 2^2 + 1^2} = \sqrt{9} = 3$
$|\vec{AC}| = \sqrt{2^2 + 0^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{4} = 2$

By the angle bisector theorem, the point $D$ divides the side $BC$ internally in the ratio of the adjacent sides, which is $|\vec{AB}| : |\vec{AC}| = 3 : 2$.

Using the section formula, the position vector of $D$ is:
$\vec{d} = \dfrac{2\vec{b} + 3\vec{c}}{3 + 2} = \dfrac{2\vec{b} + 3\vec{c}}{5}$

The vector $\vec{AD}$ is given by:
$\vec{AD} = \vec{d} - \vec{a} = \dfrac{2\vec{b} + 3\vec{c}}{5} - \vec{a} = \dfrac{2(\vec{b} - \vec{a}) + 3(\vec{c} - \vec{a})}{5}$

Substituting $\vec{AB}$ and $\vec{AC}$:
$\vec{AD} = \dfrac{2\vec{AB} + 3\vec{AC}}{5} = \dfrac{2(2\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + \hat{k}) + 3(2\hat{i})}{5}$

$\vec{AD} = \dfrac{4\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} + 2\hat{k} + 6\hat{i}}{5} = \dfrac{10\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}}{5}$

The length of the angle bisector $AD$ is the magnitude of $\vec{AD}$:
$|\vec{AD}| = \dfrac{1}{5} \sqrt{10^2 + 4^2 + 2^2} = \dfrac{1}{5} \sqrt{100 + 16 + 4} = \dfrac{\sqrt{120}}{5}$

$|\vec{AD}| = \dfrac{2\sqrt{30}}{5}$

Answer: $\dfrac{2\sqrt{30}}{5}$

</details>

### Q7. MHT CET 2026 (19 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $8\vec{AB} - 3\vec{AC} - 5\vec{AD} = \vec{0}$, then the point $C$ divides $BD$ in the ratio ...

**Options:**

- **(A)** $8 : 5$ internally
- **(B)** $3 : 4$ externally
- **(C)** $4 : 3$ internally
- **(D)** $5 : 8$ externally

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $5 : 8$ externally**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the position vectors of points $A, B, C, D$ be $\vec{a}, \vec{b}, \vec{c}, \vec{d}$ respectively.

Given equation:
$8\vec{AB} - 3\vec{AC} - 5\vec{AD} = \vec{0}$

Expressing the vectors in terms of position vectors:
$8(\vec{b} - \vec{a}) - 3(\vec{c} - \vec{a}) - 5(\vec{d} - \vec{a}) = \vec{0}$

$8\vec{b} - 8\vec{a} - 3\vec{c} + 3\vec{a} - 5\vec{d} + 5\vec{a} = \vec{0}$

$8\vec{b} - 3\vec{c} - 5\vec{d} + (-8 + 3 + 5)\vec{a} = \vec{0}$

$8\vec{b} - 3\vec{c} - 5\vec{d} = \vec{0}$

Rearranging to find the position vector of $C$:
$3\vec{c} = 8\vec{b} - 5\vec{d}$

$\vec{c} = \dfrac{8\vec{b} - 5\vec{d}}{3}$

$\vec{c} = \dfrac{8\vec{b} - 5\vec{d}}{8 - 5}$

This is of the form $\vec{c} = \dfrac{m\vec{b} - n\vec{d}}{m - n}$, which represents the section formula for external division.

To find the exact ratio in which $C$ divides $BD$, we compare it with the standard section formula $\vec{c} = \dfrac{m\vec{d} - n\vec{b}}{m - n}$ for external division:
$\vec{c} = \dfrac{5\vec{d} - 8\vec{b}}{5 - 8}$

Thus, $C$ divides the line segment $BD$ externally in the ratio $5 : 8$.

Answer: $5 : 8$ externally

</details>

---

### Q8. MHT CET 2026 (19 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

$A(1,2)$, $B(1,0)$ and $C(0,2)$ are vertices of $\Delta ABC$. The position vector of the point in which the bisector of angle $A$ cuts $BC$ is .....

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{1}{3}\hat{i} + \dfrac{4}{3}\hat{j}$
- **(B)** $\dfrac{2}{3}\hat{i} + \dfrac{4}{3}\hat{j}$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{1}{3}\hat{i} + \dfrac{2}{3}\hat{j}$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{2}{3}\hat{i} + \dfrac{5}{3}\hat{j}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\dfrac{1}{3}\hat{i} + \dfrac{4}{3}\hat{j}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The vertices of $\Delta ABC$ are given as $A(1,2)$, $B(1,0)$, and $C(0,2)$.

The lengths of the sides containing angle $A$ are:
$AB = \sqrt{(1-1)^2 + (0-2)^2} = 2$
$AC = \sqrt{(0-1)^2 + (2-2)^2} = 1$

Let $D$ be the point where the internal bisector of angle $A$ intersects the side $BC$. By the angle bisector theorem, the point $D$ divides the segment $BC$ internally in the ratio of the adjacent sides.

$\dfrac{BD}{DC} = \dfrac{AB}{AC} = \dfrac{2}{1}$

The position vectors of $B$ and $C$ are $\vec{r}_B = \hat{i}$ and $\vec{r}_C = 2\hat{j}$.

Using the section formula, the position vector of $D$ is:
$\vec{r}_D = \dfrac{2\vec{r}_C + 1\vec{r}_B}{2 + 1}$

$\vec{r}_D = \dfrac{2(2\hat{j}) + 1(\hat{i})}{3} = \dfrac{1}{3}\hat{i} + \dfrac{4}{3}\hat{j}$

Answer: $\dfrac{1}{3}\hat{i} + \dfrac{4}{3}\hat{j}$

</details>

---

### Q9. MHT CET 2026 (15 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $\bar{a} = 2\hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k}$, $\bar{b} = \hat{i} + 3\hat{j} - \hat{k}$, $\bar{c} = -2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 3\hat{k}$ and $\bar{d} = 3\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 5\hat{k}$, then the value of $x^2 + z^2 - y$ is $\cdots$, where $\bar{d} = x\bar{a} + y\bar{b} + z\bar{c}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{35}{4}$
- **(B)** $\dfrac{17}{2}$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{33}{4}$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{15}{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\dfrac{15}{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $\bar{d} = x\bar{a} + y\bar{b} + z\bar{c}$, we can substitute the given vectors:

$3\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 5\hat{k} = x(2\hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k}) + y(\hat{i} + 3\hat{j} - \hat{k}) + z(-2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 3\hat{k})$

Equating the coefficients of $\hat{i}$, $\hat{j}$, and $\hat{k}$ on both sides, we get the following system of linear equations:

$2x + y - 2z = 3$  (Equation 1)

$-x + 3y + z = 2$  (Equation 2)

$x - y - 3z = 5$  (Equation 3)

Adding Equation 2 and Equation 3, we get:

$(-x + 3y + z) + (x - y - 3z) = 2 + 5$

$2y - 2z = 7$  (Equation 4)

Multiplying Equation 2 by 2 and adding it to Equation 1, we get:

$(2x + y - 2z) + 2(-x + 3y + z) = 3 + 4$

$7y = 7 \Rightarrow y = 1$

Substituting $y = 1$ into Equation 4:

$2(1) - 2z = 7 \Rightarrow 2z = -5 \Rightarrow z = -\dfrac{5}{2}$

Substituting $y = 1$ and $z = -\dfrac{5}{2}$ into Equation 3:

$x - 1 - 3\left(-\dfrac{5}{2}\right) = 5$

$x - 1 + \dfrac{15}{2} = 5 \Rightarrow x = 6 - \dfrac{15}{2} = -\dfrac{3}{2}$

Now, we need to find the value of $x^2 + z^2 - y$:

$x^2 + z^2 - y = \left(-\dfrac{3}{2}\right)^2 + \left(-\dfrac{5}{2}\right)^2 - 1$

$= \dfrac{9}{4} + \dfrac{25}{4} - 1$

$= \dfrac{34}{4} - 1 = \dfrac{17}{2} - 1 = \dfrac{15}{2}$

Answer: $\dfrac{15}{2}$

</details>

---

### Q10. MHT CET 2026 (15 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $\bar{a} = 2\hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k}$ and $\bar{b} = \hat{i} + 2\hat{j} - 2\hat{k}$. If a third vector $\bar{c} = x\hat{i} + y\hat{j} + z\hat{k}$ is coplanar with $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$, is perpendicular to $\bar{a}$, and satisfies $|\bar{c}| = \sqrt{45}$, then the sum of all possible values of $y$ is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0$
- **(B)** $2$
- **(C)** $5$
- **(D)** $-5$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Since $\bar{c}$ is coplanar with $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$, it must be perpendicular to their cross product. We first find $\bar{a} \times \bar{b}$:

$\bar{a} \times \bar{b} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 2 & -1 & 1 \\ 1 & 2 & -2 \end{vmatrix} = \hat{i}(2 - 2) - \hat{j}(-4 - 1) + \hat{k}(4 - (-1)) = 5\hat{j} + 5\hat{k}$

For $\bar{c} = x\hat{i} + y\hat{j} + z\hat{k}$ to be coplanar with $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$, we have $\bar{c} \cdot (\bar{a} \times \bar{b}) = 0$:

$(x\hat{i} + y\hat{j} + z\hat{k}) \cdot (5\hat{j} + 5\hat{k}) = 0 \Rightarrow 5y + 5z = 0 \Rightarrow z = -y$

We are also given that $\bar{c}$ is perpendicular to $\bar{a}$, so $\bar{c} \cdot \bar{a} = 0$:

$(x\hat{i} + y\hat{j} + z\hat{k}) \cdot (2\hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k}) = 0 \Rightarrow 2x - y + z = 0$

Substituting $z = -y$ into the above equation:

$2x - y - y = 0 \Rightarrow 2x = 2y \Rightarrow x = y$

Thus, the vector $\bar{c}$ can be written as $\bar{c} = y\hat{i} + y\hat{j} - y\hat{k}$.

The magnitude of $\bar{c}$ is given as $\sqrt{45}$:

$|\bar{c}| = \sqrt{x^2 + y^2 + z^2} = \sqrt{y^2 + y^2 + (-y)^2} = \sqrt{3y^2}$

Equating the magnitudes:

$\sqrt{3y^2} = \sqrt{45} \Rightarrow 3y^2 = 45 \Rightarrow y^2 = 15$

Solving for $y$, we get $y = \pm \sqrt{15}$.

The possible values of $y$ are $\sqrt{15}$ and $-\sqrt{15}$. The sum of all possible values of $y$ is:

$\sqrt{15} + (-\sqrt{15}) = 0$

Answer: $0$

</details>

---

### Q11. MHT CET 2026 (15 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A unit vector $\bar{u}$ makes an angle of $45^\circ$ with the X- axis. If $\bar{u} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}$ is also a unit vector, then the sum of the projections of $\bar{u}$ on the coordinate axes is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{2} - 1$
- **(B)** $\sqrt{2} + 1$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}} - 1$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}} + 1$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}} - 1$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\bar{u} = x \hat{i} + y \hat{j} + z \hat{k}$.

Since $\bar{u}$ is a unit vector, $x^2 + y^2 + z^2 = 1$.

Given that $\bar{u}$ makes an angle of $45^\circ$ with the X-axis, its projection on the X-axis is $x = \cos 45^\circ = \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}$.

Thus, $y^2 + z^2 = 1 - x^2 = 1 - \dfrac{1}{2} = \dfrac{1}{2}$.

It is also given that $\bar{u} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}$ is a unit vector. Therefore, its magnitude squared is $1$:

$|x \hat{i} + (y + 1) \hat{j} + (z + 1) \hat{k}|^2 = 1$

$x^2 + (y + 1)^2 + (z + 1)^2 = 1$

$x^2 + y^2 + 2y + 1 + z^2 + 2z + 1 = 1$

Rearranging the terms, we get:

$(x^2 + y^2 + z^2) + 2(y + z) + 2 = 1$

Substituting $x^2 + y^2 + z^2 = 1$:

$1 + 2(y + z) + 2 = 1$

$2(y + z) = -2 \Rightarrow y + z = -1$

The sum of the projections of $\bar{u}$ on the coordinate axes is $x + y + z$.

Substituting the values of $x$ and $y + z$:

$x + y + z = \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}} - 1$

Answer: $\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}} - 1$

</details>

---

### Q12. MHT CET 2026 (15 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The position vectors of vertices $A$, $B$ and $C$ of triangle $ABC$ are $\hat{i} - \hat{j} - 3\hat{k}$, $2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 2\hat{k}$, and $-5\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} - 6\hat{k}$ respectively. The length of the bisector $AD$ of angle $BAC$, where $D$ is on the line segment $BC$, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $15/2$
- **(B)** $11/2$
- **(C)** $1/4$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{3}{4}\sqrt{10}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\dfrac{3}{4}\sqrt{10}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The position vectors of the vertices are $\vec{A} = \hat{i} - \hat{j} - 3\hat{k}$, $\vec{B} = 2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 2\hat{k}$, and $\vec{C} = -5\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} - 6\hat{k}$.

The vectors representing the sides $AB$ and $AC$ are:
$\overrightarrow{AB} = \vec{B} - \vec{A} = (2 - 1)\hat{i} + (1 - (-1))\hat{j} + (-2 - (-3))\hat{k} = \hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + \hat{k}$
$\overrightarrow{AC} = \vec{C} - \vec{A} = (-5 - 1)\hat{i} + (2 - (-1))\hat{j} + (-6 - (-3))\hat{k} = -6\hat{i} + 3\hat{j} - 3\hat{k}$

The lengths of these sides are:
$|\overrightarrow{AB}| = \sqrt{1^2 + 2^2 + 1^2} = \sqrt{6}$
$|\overrightarrow{AC}| = \sqrt{(-6)^2 + 3^2 + (-3)^2} = \sqrt{54} = 3\sqrt{6}$

By the angle bisector theorem, the point $D$ divides the line segment $BC$ internally in the ratio of the adjacent sides:
$\dfrac{BD}{DC} = \dfrac{|\overrightarrow{AB}|}{|\overrightarrow{AC}|} = \dfrac{\sqrt{6}}{3\sqrt{6}} = \dfrac{1}{3}$

Using the section formula, the position vector of $D$ is:
$\vec{D} = \dfrac{3\vec{B} + 1\vec{C}}{1 + 3} = \dfrac{3(2\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 2\hat{k}) + (-5\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} - 6\hat{k})}{4} = \dfrac{\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} - 12\hat{k}}{4}$

The vector $\overrightarrow{AD}$ is:
$\overrightarrow{AD} = \vec{D} - \vec{A} = \dfrac{\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} - 12\hat{k}}{4} - (\hat{i} - \hat{j} - 3\hat{k}) = \dfrac{-3\hat{i} + 9\hat{j}}{4}$

The length of the bisector $AD$ is:
$|\overrightarrow{AD}| = \sqrt{\left(\dfrac{-3}{4}\right)^2 + \left(\dfrac{9}{4}\right)^2} = \sqrt(\dfrac{9}{16} + \dfrac{81}{16}) = \sqrt{\dfrac{90}{16}} = \dfrac{3}{4}\sqrt{10}$

Answer: $\dfrac{3}{4}\sqrt{10}$

</details>

---

### Q13. MHT CET 2026 (15 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $|\bar{a}| = 24$, $|\bar{b}| = 7$, $\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b} = 0$, $\bar{a} + \bar{b} + \bar{c} = \bar{0}$, then $|\bar{c}| = \ldots$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $31$
- **(B)** $17$
- **(C)** $40$
- **(D)** $25$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $25$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $\bar{a} + \bar{b} + \bar{c} = \bar{0}$

$\bar{c} = -(\bar{a} + \bar{b})$

Squaring both sides, we get

$|\bar{c}|^2 = |\bar{a} + \bar{b}|^2$

$|\bar{c}|^2 = |\bar{a}|^2 + |\bar{b}|^2 + 2\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b}$

Substituting the given values $|\bar{a}| = 24$, $|\bar{b}| = 7$, and $\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b} = 0$

$|\bar{c}|^2 = (24)^2 + (7)^2 + 2(0)$

$|\bar{c}|^2 = 576 + 49 = 625$

$|\bar{c}| = 25$

Answer: $25$

</details>

### Q14. MHT CET 2026 (14 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $\vec{a}$ and $\vec{b}$ be two unit vectors such that their sum is also a unit vector. If $\vec{u} = \vec{a} + 2\vec{b}$ and $\vec{v} = 2\vec{a} + \vec{b}$, then the angle between $\vec{u}$ and $\vec{v}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0$
- **(B)** $\dfrac{\pi}{6}$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{\pi}{3}$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{\pi}{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\dfrac{\pi}{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $|\vec{a}| = 1$, $|\vec{b}| = 1$, and $|\vec{a} + \vec{b}| = 1$.

Squaring $|\vec{a} + \vec{b}| = 1$, we get:

$|\vec{a}|^2 + |\vec{b}|^2 + 2\vec{a} \cdot \vec{b} = 1$

$1 + 1 + 2\vec{a} \cdot \vec{b} = 1 \Rightarrow \vec{a} \cdot \vec{b} = -\dfrac{1}{2}$

Now, $\vec{u} = \vec{a} + 2\vec{b}$ and $\vec{v} = 2\vec{a} + \vec{b}$.

The dot product of $\vec{u}$ and $\vec{v}$ is:

$\vec{u} \cdot \vec{v} = (\vec{a} + 2\vec{b}) \cdot (2\vec{a} + \vec{b}) = 2|\vec{a}|^2 + 5\vec{a} \cdot \vec{b} + 2|\vec{b}|^2$

$\vec{u} \cdot \vec{v} = 2(1) + 5\left(-\dfrac{1}{2}\right) + 2(1) = 4 - \dfrac{5}{2} = \dfrac{3}{2}$

The magnitudes of $\vec{u}$ and $\vec{v}$ are:

$|\vec{u}|^2 = |\vec{a} + 2\vec{b}|^2 = |\vec{a}|^2 + 4|\vec{b}|^2 + 4\vec{a} \cdot \vec{b} = 1 + 4(1) + 4\left(-\dfrac{1}{2}\right) = 3 \Rightarrow |\vec{u}| = \sqrt{3}$

$|\vec{v}|^2 = |2\vec{a} + \vec{b}|^2 = 4|\vec{a}|^2 + |\vec{b}|^2 + 4\vec{a} \cdot \vec{b} = 4(1) + 1 + 4\left(-\dfrac{1}{2}\right) = 3 \Rightarrow |\vec{v}| = \sqrt{3}$

Let $\theta$ be the angle between $\vec{u}$ and $\vec{v}$. Then:

$\cos \theta = \dfrac{\vec{u} \cdot \vec{v}}{|\vec{u}||\vec{v}|} = \dfrac{\dfrac{3}{2}}{\sqrt{3} \times \sqrt{3}} = \dfrac{\dfrac{3}{2}}{3} = \dfrac{1}{2}$

$\theta = \dfrac{\pi}{3}$

Answer: $\dfrac{\pi}{3}$

</details>

---

### Q15. MHT CET 2026 (14 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $\bar{a} = 4\hat{i} - 10\hat{j} + (1 - p)\hat{k}$ and $\bar{b} = 12\hat{i} + (q - 10)\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$ satisfy $\bar{a} \times \bar{b} = \bar{0}$, then the values of $p$ and $q$ are respectively

**Options:**

- **(A)** $9, 7$
- **(B)** $0, -20$
- **(C)** $1, -30$
- **(D)** $10, 5$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $0, -20$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $\bar{a} = 4\hat{i} - 10\hat{j} + (1 - p)\hat{k}$ and $\bar{b} = 12\hat{i} + (q - 10)\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$.

Since $\bar{a} \times \bar{b} = \bar{0}$, the vectors $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$ are collinear. Their corresponding components must be proportional.

$\dfrac{4}{12} = \dfrac{-10}{q - 10} = \dfrac{1 - p}{3}$

From the first and second terms:

$\dfrac{1}{3} = \dfrac{-10}{q - 10}$

$q - 10 = -30 \Rightarrow q = -20$

From the first and third terms:

$\dfrac{1}{3} = \dfrac{1 - p}{3}$

$1 - p = 1 \Rightarrow p = 0$

The values of $p$ and $q$ are $0$ and $-20$ respectively.

Answer: $0, -20$

</details>

---

### Q16. MHT CET 2026 (13 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the vector $\vec{a} = x\hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k}$ and $\vec{b} = \hat{i} + y\hat{j} + z\hat{k}$ are collinear, then a possible unit vector parallel to the vector $\hat{i} + y\hat{j} + z\hat{k}$ is...

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(\hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k})$
- **(B)** $\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(\hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k})$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(\hat{i} - \hat{j} - \hat{k})$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(-\hat{i} - \hat{j} - \hat{k})$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(\hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k})$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Since the vectors $\vec{a} = x\hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k}$ and $\vec{b} = \hat{i} + y\hat{j} + z\hat{k}$ are collinear, their direction ratios are proportional.

$\dfrac{x}{1} = \dfrac{-1}{y} = \dfrac{1}{z} = \lambda$

From this, we get $y = -\dfrac{1}{\lambda}$ and $z = \dfrac{1}{\lambda}$.

The required vector is $\vec{b} = \hat{i} - \dfrac{1}{\lambda}\hat{j} + \dfrac{1}{\lambda}\hat{k}$.

Observing the given options, the magnitudes of the $\hat{i}$, $\hat{j}$, and $\hat{k}$ components of the unit vector are equal. This means the magnitudes of the components of $\vec{b}$ must also be equal.

$\left|-\dfrac{1}{\lambda}\right| = \left|\dfrac{1}{\lambda}\right| = 1 \Rightarrow \lambda = \pm 1$

If we take $\lambda = 1$, the vector becomes $\hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k}$.

The unit vector parallel to this vector is:

$\hat{n} = \pm \dfrac{\hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k}}{\sqrt{1^2 + (-1)^2 + 1^2}} = \pm \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(\hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k})$

Taking the positive sign, we get $\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(\hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k})$, which matches the first option.

</details>

---

### Q17. MHT CET 2026 (12 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $\vec{a}, \vec{b}, \vec{c}$ are three vectors such that $\vec{a} \cdot (\vec{b} + \vec{c}) + \vec{b} \cdot (\vec{c} + \vec{a}) + \vec{c} \cdot (\vec{a} + \vec{b}) = 0$ and $|\vec{a}| = 8, |\vec{b}| = 1, |\vec{c}| = 4$, then $|\vec{a} + \vec{b} + \vec{c}| = ...$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $5$
- **(B)** $9$
- **(C)** $13$
- **(D)** $18$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $9$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $\vec{a} \cdot (\vec{b} + \vec{c}) + \vec{b} \cdot (\vec{c} + \vec{a}) + \vec{c} \cdot (\vec{a} + \vec{b}) = 0$

$\vec{a} \cdot \vec{b} + \vec{a} \cdot \vec{c} + \vec{b} \cdot \vec{c} + \vec{b} \cdot \vec{a} + \vec{c} \cdot \vec{a} + \vec{c} \cdot \vec{b} = 0$

$2(\vec{a} \cdot \vec{b} + \vec{b} \cdot \vec{c} + \vec{c} \cdot \vec{a}) = 0$

Using the identity for the magnitude of the sum of three vectors:

$|\vec{a} + \vec{b} + \vec{c}|^2 = |\vec{a}|^2 + |\vec{b}|^2 + |\vec{c}|^2 + 2(\vec{a} \cdot \vec{b} + \vec{b} \cdot \vec{c} + \vec{c} \cdot \vec{a})$

Substituting the given values $|\vec{a}| = 8$, $|\vec{b}| = 1$, and $|\vec{c}| = 4$:

$|\vec{a} + \vec{b} + \vec{c}|^2 = 8^2 + 1^2 + 4^2 + 0$

$|\vec{a} + \vec{b} + \vec{c}|^2 = 64 + 1 + 16 = 81$

$|\vec{a} + \vec{b} + \vec{c}| = 9$

Answer: $9$

</details>

---

### Q18. MHT CET 2026 (12 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the sum of two unit vectors is a unit vector, then the magnitude of their difference is...

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{2}$
- **(B)** $0$
- **(C)** $\sqrt{5}$
- **(D)** $\sqrt{3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\sqrt{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\vec{a}$ and $\vec{b}$ be the two unit vectors.

Given $|\vec{a}| = 1$, $|\vec{b}| = 1$, and $|\vec{a} + \vec{b}| = 1$.

Using the identity for vectors:

$|\vec{a} + \vec{b}|^2 + |\vec{a} - \vec{b}|^2 = 2(|\vec{a}|^2 + |\vec{b}|^2)$

Substituting the given values:

$1^2 + |\vec{a} - \vec{b}|^2 = 2(1^2 + 1^2)$

$1 + |\vec{a} - \vec{b}|^2 = 4$

$|\vec{a} - \vec{b}|^2 = 3$

$|\vec{a} - \vec{b}| = \sqrt{3}$

Answer: $\sqrt{3}$

</details>

### Q19. MHT CET 2026 (12 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $\bar{a}=\hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k}$ and $\bar{b}=\hat{i}-\hat{j}+2\hat{k}$. If a vector $\bar{c}$ is coplanar with $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$ such that $\bar{c}\cdot\bar{a}=1$ and $\bar{c}$ is perpendicular to $\bar{b}$, then $\bar{c}$ is equal to ...

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{1}{3}(2\hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k})$
- **(B)** $\dfrac{1}{5}(2\hat{i}-\hat{j}+4\hat{k})$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{1}{7}(2\hat{i}+4\hat{j}+\hat{k})$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{1}{7}(2\hat{i}-4\hat{j}-\hat{k})$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\dfrac{1}{7}(2\hat{i}+4\hat{j}+\hat{k})$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Since $\bar{c}$ is coplanar with $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$, we can express $\bar{c}$ as a linear combination of $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$:

$\bar{c} = x\bar{a} + y\bar{b}$

Given that $\bar{c}$ is perpendicular to $\bar{b}$, we have $\bar{c}\cdot\bar{b} = 0$.

$(x\bar{a} + y\bar{b})\cdot\bar{b} = 0$

$x(\bar{a}\cdot\bar{b}) + y|\bar{b}|^2 = 0$

Calculating the dot product and magnitude:

$\bar{a}\cdot\bar{b} = (1)(1) + (1)(-1) + (1)(2) = 2$

$|\bar{b}|^2 = (1)^2 + (-1)^2 + (2)^2 = 6$

Substituting these values:

$2x + 6y = 0 \Rightarrow x = -3y$

We are also given $\bar{c}\cdot\bar{a} = 1$.

$(x\bar{a} + y\bar{b})\cdot\bar{a} = 1$

$x|\bar{a}|^2 + y(\bar{a}\cdot\bar{b}) = 1$

Calculating $|\bar{a}|^2$:

$|\bar{a}|^2 = (1)^2 + (1)^2 + (1)^2 = 3$

Substituting the values:

$3x + 2y = 1$

Using $x = -3y$ in the above equation:

$3(-3y) + 2y = 1$

$-9y + 2y = 1 \Rightarrow -7y = 1 \Rightarrow y = -\dfrac{1}{7}$

This gives $x = \dfrac{3}{7}$.

Therefore, the vector $\bar{c}$ is:

$\bar{c} = \dfrac{3}{7}\bar{a} - \dfrac{1}{7}\bar{b} = \dfrac{1}{7}(3\bar{a} - \bar{b})$

$\bar{c} = \dfrac{1}{7}(3(\hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k}) - (\hat{i}-\hat{j}+2\hat{k}))$

$\bar{c} = \dfrac{1}{7}(2\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} + \hat{k})$

Answer: $\dfrac{1}{7}(2\hat{i}+4\hat{j}+\hat{k})$

</details>

---

### Q20. MHT CET 2026 (12 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $4\hat{i}+7\hat{j}+8\hat{k}, 2\hat{i}+3\hat{j}+4\hat{k}$ and $2\hat{i}+5\hat{j}+7\hat{k}$ are the position vectors of the vertices A, B and C respectively of the triangle ABC, then the largest angle of the triangle is (are) ...

**Options:**

- **(A)** A
- **(B)** C
- **(C)** A and C
- **(D)** B and C

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) C**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the position vectors of the vertices be $\vec{a} = 4\hat{i} + 7\hat{j} + 8\hat{k}$, $\vec{b} = 2\hat{i} + 3\hat{j} + 4\hat{k}$, and $\vec{c} = 2\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} + 7\hat{k}$.

The vectors representing the sides of the triangle are:
$\vec{AB} = \vec{b} - \vec{a} = -2\hat{i} - 4\hat{j} - 4\hat{k}$
$\vec{BC} = \vec{c} - \vec{b} = 0\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$
$\vec{CA} = \vec{a} - \vec{c} = 2\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + \hat{k}$

The lengths of the sides are the magnitudes of these vectors:
$|\vec{AB}| = \sqrt{(-2)^2 + (-4)^2 + (-4)^2} = \sqrt{4 + 16 + 16} = \sqrt{36} = 6$
$|\vec{BC}| = \sqrt{0^2 + 2^2 + 3^2} = \sqrt{0 + 4 + 9} = \sqrt{13}$
$|\vec{CA}| = \sqrt{2^2 + 2^2 + 1^2} = \sqrt{4 + 4 + 1} = \sqrt{9} = 3$

Comparing the lengths of the sides, we have $6 > \sqrt{13} > 3$, which means $|\vec{AB}| > |\vec{BC}| > |\vec{CA}|$.

Since the largest angle in a triangle is always opposite to its longest side, the largest angle is opposite to side $AB$. Therefore, the largest angle is $C$.

Answer: C

</details>

---

### Q21. MHT CET 2026 (12 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $O$ be the origin in 3D space. If the vertices $A(2, p, -3), B(q, -2, 5)$, and $C(-5, 1, r)$ of a triangle satisfy the vector relation $\overline{PA}+\overline{PB}+\overline{PC}=3\overline{PO}$ for any point $P$ in space, then the values of $(p, q, r)$ respectively are ...

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(1, 3, 2)$
- **(B)** $(1, 3, -2)$
- **(C)** $(1, 2, 3)$
- **(D)** $(1, -2, 3)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $(1, 3, -2)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the position vectors of $A, B, C$, and $P$ with respect to the origin $O$ be $\vec{a}, \vec{b}, \vec{c}$, and $\vec{p}$ respectively.

The given relation is $\overline{PA} + \overline{PB} + \overline{PC} = 3\overline{PO}$.

Expressing the vectors in terms of position vectors, we get:

$(\vec{a} - \vec{p}) + (\vec{b} - \vec{p}) + (\vec{c} - \vec{p}) = 3(\vec{0} - \vec{p})$

$\vec{a} + \vec{b} + \vec{c} - 3\vec{p} = -3\vec{p}$

$\vec{a} + \vec{b} + \vec{c} = \vec{0}$

This implies that the centroid of the triangle $ABC$ is at the origin $O(0, 0, 0)$.

Given the coordinates of the vertices $A(2, p, -3), B(q, -2, 5)$, and $C(-5, 1, r)$, the sum of their respective coordinates must be zero.

For the x-coordinates:
$2 + q - 5 = 0 \Rightarrow q = 3$

For the y-coordinates:
$p - 2 + 1 = 0 \Rightarrow p = 1$

For the z-coordinates:
$-3 + 5 + r = 0 \Rightarrow r = -2$

Therefore, the values of $(p, q, r)$ are $(1, 3, -2)$.

Answer: $(1, 3, -2)$

</details>

---

### Q22. MHT CET 2026 (12 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $\bar{a}=\lambda\hat{i}+4\hat{j}$ and $\bar{b}=\hat{i}+\lambda\hat{j}$ where $\lambda\in Z$ such that $|\bar{a}|<6$. The number of possible values of $\lambda$ for which $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$ are non-collinear is ___

**Options:**

- **(A)** $7$
- **(B)** $9$
- **(C)** $3$
- **(D)** $5$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $7$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $\bar{a} = \lambda\hat{i} + 4\hat{j}$ and $\bar{b} = \hat{i} + \lambda\hat{j}$.

We are given $|\bar{a}| < 6$.

$\sqrt{\lambda^2 + 16} < 6$

$\lambda^2 + 16 < 36$

$\lambda^2 < 20$

Since $\lambda \in Z$, the possible values of $\lambda$ are $-4, -3, -2, -1, 0, 1, 2, 3, 4$.

Total number of possible values of $\lambda$ is $9$.

For $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$ to be collinear, their components must be proportional:

$\dfrac{\lambda}{1} = \dfrac{4}{\lambda}$

$\lambda^2 = 4 \Rightarrow \lambda = \pm 2$

Thus, for $\lambda = 2$ and $\lambda = -2$, the vectors are collinear.

The number of values of $\lambda$ for which $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$ are non-collinear is $9 - 2 = 7$.

Answer: $7$

</details>

---

### Q23. MHT CET 2026 (20 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $\vec{a} = 2\hat{i} + \hat{k}, \vec{b} = \hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}$, and $\vec{c} = 4\hat{i} - 3\hat{j} + 7\hat{k}$. If $\vec{r}$ is a vector such that $\vec{r} \times \vec{b} = \vec{c} \times \vec{b}$ and $\vec{r} \cdot \vec{a} = 0$, Then $\vec{r} \cdot \vec{c} =$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-14$
- **(B)** $34$
- **(C)** $-7$
- **(D)** $20$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $34$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $\vec{r} \times \vec{b} = \vec{c} \times \vec{b}$

$(\vec{r} - \vec{c}) \times \vec{b} = 0$

$\vec{r} - \vec{c} = \lambda \vec{b}$

$\vec{r} = \vec{c} + \lambda \vec{b}$

Taking the dot product with $\vec{a}$:

$\vec{r} \cdot \vec{a} = \vec{c} \cdot \vec{a} + \lambda (\vec{b} \cdot \vec{a}) = 0$

Calculating the dot products:

$\vec{c} \cdot \vec{a} = (4)(2) + (-3)(0) + (7)(1) = 15$

$\vec{b} \cdot \vec{a} = (1)(2) + (1)(0) + (1)(1) = 3$

Substituting these values:

$15 + 3\lambda = 0 \Rightarrow \lambda = -5$

Thus, $\vec{r} = \vec{c} - 5\vec{b}$

$\vec{r} = (4\hat{i} - 3\hat{j} + 7\hat{k}) - 5(\hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}) = -\hat{i} - 8\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$

Now, calculating $\vec{r} \cdot \vec{c}$:

$\vec{r} \cdot \vec{c} = (-\hat{i} - 8\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}) \cdot (4\hat{i} - 3\hat{j} + 7\hat{k})$

$\vec{r} \cdot \vec{c} = -4 + 24 + 14 = 34$

Answer: $34$

</details>

---

### Q24. MHT CET 2026 (20 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $\bar{a} = \hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}$, $\bar{b} = \hat{i} - 3\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$ and $\bar{c} = 3\hat{i} - 2\hat{k}$. If a vector $\bar{p}$ satisfies the conditions $\bar{p} \cdot \bar{c} = 0$ and $\bar{p} \times \bar{a} = \bar{b} \times \bar{a}$, then the value of $|\bar{p}| = $...

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{13}$
- **(B)** $\sqrt{14}$
- **(C)** $\sqrt{17}$
- **(D)** $\sqrt{19}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\sqrt{17}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $\bar{p} \times \bar{a} = \bar{b} \times \bar{a}$

$\Rightarrow (\bar{p} - \bar{b}) \times \bar{a} = \bar{0}$

$\Rightarrow \bar{p} - \bar{b} = \lambda \bar{a}$

$\Rightarrow \bar{p} = \bar{b} + \lambda \bar{a}$

Also given $\bar{p} \cdot \bar{c} = 0$

$\Rightarrow (\bar{b} + \lambda \bar{a}) \cdot \bar{c} = 0$

$\Rightarrow \bar{b} \cdot \bar{c} + \lambda (\bar{a} \cdot \bar{c}) = 0$

Calculating the dot products:

$\bar{b} \cdot \bar{c} = (\hat{i} - 3\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}) \cdot (3\hat{i} - 2\hat{k}) = 3 - 4 = -1$

$\bar{a} \cdot \bar{c} = (\hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}) \cdot (3\hat{i} - 2\hat{k}) = 3 - 2 = 1$

Substituting these values:

$-1 + \lambda(1) = 0 \Rightarrow \lambda = 1$

Therefore, $\bar{p} = \bar{b} + \bar{a}$

$\bar{p} = (\hat{i} - 3\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}) + (\hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}) = 2\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$

Magnitude of $\bar{p}$:

$|\bar{p}| = \sqrt{2^2 + (-2)^2 + 3^2} = \sqrt{4 + 4 + 9} = \sqrt{17}$

Answer: $\sqrt{17}$

</details>

---

### Q25. MHT CET 2026 (20 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $\overline{OD} = \hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 6\hat{k}$, $\overline{CB} = -3\hat{i} - 2\hat{k}$ be the diagonals of the parallelogram OBDC and $\overline{OA} = \hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$ be another vector. Then the volume of a parallelopiped determined by vectors $\overline{OA}$, $\overline{OB}$, and $\overline{OC}$ (in cubic units), is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3$
- **(B)** $6$
- **(C)** $9$
- **(D)** $12$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $9$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\overline{OB} = \vec{b}$ and $\overline{OC} = \vec{c}$.

In the parallelogram OBDC, the diagonals are given by the sum and difference of the adjacent sides:
$\overline{OD} = \vec{b} + \vec{c} = \hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 6\hat{k}$
$\overline{CB} = \vec{b} - \vec{c} = -3\hat{i} - 2\hat{k}$

The volume of the parallelopiped determined by vectors $\overline{OA} = \vec{a}$, $\overline{OB} = \vec{b}$, and $\overline{OC} = \vec{c}$ is given by the scalar triple product $V = |\vec{a} \cdot (\vec{b} \times \vec{c})|$.

Taking the cross product of the diagonals:
$(\vec{b} + \vec{c}) \times (\vec{b} - \vec{c}) = \vec{b} \times \vec{b} - \vec{b} \times \vec{c} + \vec{c} \times \vec{b} - \vec{c} \times \vec{c}$

Since $\vec{b} \times \vec{b} = \vec{c} \times \vec{c} = \vec{0}$ and $\vec{c} \times \vec{b} = -\vec{b} \times \vec{c}$, we get:
$(\vec{b} + \vec{c}) \times (\vec{b} - \vec{c}) = -2(\vec{b} \times \vec{c})$

Now, computing the cross product of $\overline{OD}$ and $\overline{CB}$:
$\overline{OD} \times \overline{CB} = \left| \begin{matrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1 & 2 & 6 \\ -3 & 0 & -2 \end{matrix} \right|$

$\overline{OD} \times \overline{CB} = \hat{i}(-4 - 0) - \hat{j}(-2 - (-18)) + \hat{k}(0 - (-6))$
$\overline{OD} \times \overline{CB} = -4\hat{i} - 16\hat{j} + 6\hat{k}$

Substituting this back to find $\vec{b} \times \vec{c}$:
$-2(\vec{b} \times \vec{c}) = -4\hat{i} - 16\hat{j} + 6\hat{k}$
$\vec{b} \times \vec{c} = 2\hat{i} + 8\hat{j} - 3\hat{k}$

Given $\overline{OA} = \vec{a} = \hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$, the volume is:
$V = |\vec{a} \cdot (\vec{b} \times \vec{c})|$
$V = |(\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}) \cdot (2\hat{i} + 8\hat{j} - 3\hat{k})|$
$V = |(1)(2) + (2)(8) + (3)(-3)|$
$V = |2 + 16 - 9| = 9$

The volume of the parallelopiped is $9$ cubic units.

Answer: $9$

</details>

---

### Q26. MHT CET 2026 (20 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $\bar{a}$, $\bar{b}$ and $\bar{c}$ are three vectors such that $|\bar{a} + \bar{b} + \bar{c}| = 1$, $\bar{c} = \lambda(\bar{a} \times \bar{b})$ and $|\bar{a}| = \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{3}}$, $|\bar{b}| = \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}$, $|\bar{c}| = \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{6}}$, then the angle between $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{\pi^c}{6}$
- **(B)** $\dfrac{\pi^c}{4}$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{\pi^c}{3}$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{\pi^c}{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\dfrac{\pi^c}{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $|\bar{a} + \bar{b} + \bar{c}| = 1$, squaring both sides gives:

$|\bar{a}|^2 + |\bar{b}|^2 + |\bar{c}|^2 + 2(\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b} + \bar{b} \cdot \bar{c} + \bar{c} \cdot \bar{a}) = 1$

Since $\bar{c} = \lambda(\bar{a} \times \bar{b})$, the vector $\bar{c}$ is perpendicular to both $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$.

$\bar{a} \cdot \bar{c} = 0$ and $\bar{b} \cdot \bar{c} = 0$

Substituting the given magnitudes $|\bar{a}| = \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{3}}$, $|\bar{b}| = \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}$, and $|\bar{c}| = \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{6}}$:

$\left(\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\right)^2 + \left(\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\right)^2 + \left(\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{6}}\right)^2 + 2(\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b} + 0 + 0) = 1$

$\dfrac{1}{3} + \dfrac{1}{2} + \dfrac{1}{6} + 2(\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b}) = 1$

$\dfrac{2 + 3 + 1}{6} + 2(\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b}) = 1$

$1 + 2(\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b}) = 1$

$2(\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b}) = 0$

$\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b} = 0$

$|\bar{a}| |\bar{b}| \cos \theta = 0$

Since $|\bar{a}| \neq 0$ and $|\bar{b}| \neq 0$, we have $\cos \theta = 0$.

$\theta = \dfrac{\pi^c}{2}$

Answer: $\dfrac{\pi^c}{2}$

</details>

---

### Q27. MHT CET 2026 (19 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $\vec{a}, \vec{b}, \vec{c}$ are three vectors such that $\vec{a} \perp (\vec{b} + \vec{c}), \vec{b} \perp (\vec{c} + \vec{a}),$ and $\vec{c} \perp (\vec{a} + \vec{b})$ and $|\vec{a}| = 1, |\vec{b}| = 2, |\vec{c}| = 3$, then $|\vec{a} + \vec{b} + \vec{c}|$ is...

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{8}$
- **(B)** $8$
- **(C)** $14$
- **(D)** $\sqrt{14}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\sqrt{14}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $\vec{a} \perp (\vec{b} + \vec{c})$, we have $\vec{a} \cdot (\vec{b} + \vec{c}) = 0 \Rightarrow \vec{a} \cdot \vec{b} + \vec{a} \cdot \vec{c} = 0$

Similarly, $\vec{b} \perp (\vec{c} + \vec{a}) \Rightarrow \vec{b} \cdot (\vec{c} + \vec{a}) = 0 \Rightarrow \vec{b} \cdot \vec{c} + \vec{b} \cdot \vec{a} = 0$

And $\vec{c} \perp (\vec{a} + \vec{b}) \Rightarrow \vec{c} \cdot (\vec{a} + \vec{b}) = 0 \Rightarrow \vec{c} \cdot \vec{a} + \vec{c} \cdot \vec{b} = 0$

Adding the above three equations, we get:

$2(\vec{a} \cdot \vec{b} + \vec{b} \cdot \vec{c} + \vec{c} \cdot \vec{a}) = 0$

Using the identity for the square of the sum of three vectors:

$|\vec{a} + \vec{b} + \vec{c}|^2 = |\vec{a}|^2 + |\vec{b}|^2 + |\vec{c}|^2 + 2(\vec{a} \cdot \vec{b} + \vec{b} \cdot \vec{c} + \vec{c} \cdot \vec{a})$

Substituting the given values $|\vec{a}| = 1$, $|\vec{b}| = 2$, and $|\vec{c}| = 3$:

$|\vec{a} + \vec{b} + \vec{c}|^2 = 1^2 + 2^2 + 3^2 + 0$

$|\vec{a} + \vec{b} + \vec{c}|^2 = 1 + 4 + 9 = 14$

$|\vec{a} + \vec{b} + \vec{c}| = \sqrt{14}$

Answer: $\sqrt{14}$

</details>

---

### Q28. MHT CET 2026 (19 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let O$(0, 0)$, A$(-1, 2)$ and B$(1, 3)$ be the vertices of $\triangle$OAB. The bisector of angle O intersects side AB at point D. The value of $\vec{OD} \cdot \vec{AB}$ is equal to...

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-5(\sqrt{2} + 1)$
- **(B)** $5(1 - \sqrt{2})$
- **(C)** $5(\sqrt{2} + 1)$
- **(D)** $5(\sqrt{2} - 1)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $5(\sqrt{2} - 1)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Position vectors of the vertices are $\vec{OA} = -\hat{i} + 2\hat{j}$ and $\vec{OB} = \hat{i} + 3\hat{j}$.

The lengths of the sides OA and OB are:

$|\vec{OA}| = \sqrt{(-1)^2 + 2^2} = \sqrt{5}$

$|\vec{OB}| = \sqrt{1^2 + 3^2} = \sqrt{10}$

By the angle bisector theorem, the point D divides the segment AB in the ratio of the adjacent sides:

$\dfrac{AD}{DB} = \dfrac{|\vec{OA}|}{|\vec{OB}|} = \dfrac{\sqrt{5}}{\sqrt{10}} = \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}$

Using the section formula, the position vector of D is:

$\vec{OD} = \dfrac{\sqrt{2}\vec{OA} + 1\vec{OB}}{\sqrt{2} + 1} = \dfrac{\sqrt{2}(-\hat{i} + 2\hat{j}) + (\hat{i} + 3\hat{j})}{\sqrt{2} + 1} = \dfrac{(1 - \sqrt{2})\hat{i} + (2\sqrt{2} + 3)\hat{j}}{\sqrt{2} + 1}$

The vector $\vec{AB}$ is given by:

$\vec{AB} = \vec{OB} - \vec{OA} = (\hat{i} + 3\hat{j}) - (-\hat{i} + 2\hat{j}) = 2\hat{i} + \hat{j}$

Now, calculating the dot product $\vec{OD} \cdot \vec{AB}$:

$\vec{OD} \cdot \vec{AB} = \left( \dfrac{1 - \sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{2} + 1} \right)(2) + \left( \dfrac{2\sqrt{2} + 3}{\sqrt{2} + 1} \right)(1)$

$= \dfrac{2 - 2\sqrt{2} + 2\sqrt{2} + 3}{\sqrt{2} + 1} = \dfrac{5}{\sqrt{2} + 1}$

Rationalizing the denominator:

$\vec{OD} \cdot \vec{AB} = \dfrac{5(\sqrt{2} - 1)}{(\sqrt{2} + 1)(\sqrt{2} - 1)} = 5(\sqrt{2} - 1)$

Answer: $5(\sqrt{2} - 1)$

</details>

---

### Q29. MHT CET 2026 (19 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $A(\vec{a})$, $B(\vec{b})$ and $C(\vec{c})$ are vertices of $\triangle ABC$. Point D divides segment BC internally in the ratio $2 : 1$. Point E divides segment AD internally in the ratio $1 : 2$, then the position vector of E is ____

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{3\vec{a} + 4\vec{b} + 2\vec{c}}{9}$
- **(B)** $\dfrac{6\vec{a} + 2\vec{b} + \vec{c}}{9}$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{3\vec{a} + 2\vec{b} + 4\vec{c}}{9}$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{6\vec{a} + \vec{b} + 2\vec{c}}{9}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\dfrac{6\vec{a} + \vec{b} + 2\vec{c}}{9}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Point D divides the segment BC internally in the ratio $2 : 1$. Using the section formula, the position vector of D is given by:

$\vec{d} = \dfrac{2\vec{c} + 1\vec{b}}{2 + 1} = \dfrac{\vec{b} + 2\vec{c}}{3}$

Point E divides the segment AD internally in the ratio $1 : 2$. The position vector of E is given by:

$\vec{e} = \dfrac{1\vec{d} + 2\vec{a}}{1 + 2} = \dfrac{2\vec{a} + \vec{d}}{3}$

Substituting the value of $\vec{d}$ into the equation for $\vec{e}$:

$\vec{e} = \dfrac{2\vec{a} + \dfrac{\vec{b} + 2\vec{c}}{3}}{3}$

$\vec{e} = \dfrac{\dfrac{6\vec{a} + \vec{b} + 2\vec{c}}{3}}{3}$

$\vec{e} = \dfrac{6\vec{a} + \vec{b} + 2\vec{c}}{9}$

Answer: $\dfrac{6\vec{a} + \vec{b} + 2\vec{c}}{9}$

</details>

---

### Q30. MHT CET 2026 (19 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $|\vec{a}| = 3$, $|\vec{b}| = 4$, $|\vec{c}| = 5$ such that each vector is perpendicular to the sum of the other two, then $|\vec{a} + \vec{b} + \vec{c}|$ is equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** $5\sqrt{3}$
- **(B)** $10\sqrt{2}$
- **(C)** $5\sqrt{2}$
- **(D)** $4\sqrt{3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $5\sqrt{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given that each vector is perpendicular to the sum of the other two, we have:

$\vec{a} \cdot (\vec{b} + \vec{c}) = 0 \Rightarrow \vec{a} \cdot \vec{b} + \vec{a} \cdot \vec{c} = 0$

$\vec{b} \cdot (\vec{c} + \vec{a}) = 0 \Rightarrow \vec{b} \cdot \vec{c} + \vec{b} \cdot \vec{a} = 0$

$\vec{c} \cdot (\vec{a} + \vec{b}) = 0 \Rightarrow \vec{c} \cdot \vec{a} + \vec{c} \cdot \vec{b} = 0$

Adding the above three equations, we get:

$2(\vec{a} \cdot \vec{b} + \vec{b} \cdot \vec{c} + \vec{c} \cdot \vec{a}) = 0$

Using the identity for the square of the sum of three vectors:

$|\vec{a} + \vec{b} + \vec{c}|^2 = |\vec{a}|^2 + |\vec{b}|^2 + |\vec{c}|^2 + 2(\vec{a} \cdot \vec{b} + \vec{b} \cdot \vec{c} + \vec{c} \cdot \vec{a})$

Substituting the given magnitudes $|\vec{a}| = 3$, $|\vec{b}| = 4$, and $|\vec{c}| = 5$:

$|\vec{a} + \vec{b} + \vec{c}|^2 = 3^2 + 4^2 + 5^2 + 0$

$|\vec{a} + \vec{b} + \vec{c}|^2 = 9 + 16 + 25 = 50$

$|\vec{a} + \vec{b} + \vec{c}| = \sqrt{50} = 5\sqrt{2}$

Answer: $5\sqrt{2}$

</details>

---

### Q31. MHT CET 2026 (19 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $ABC$ is a right-angled triangle in which $BC$ is the longest side and the position vector of $B$ and $C$ are respectively $3\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + \hat{k}$ and $5\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 3\hat{k}$, then the value of $\overline{AB} \cdot \overline{AC} + \overline{BA} \cdot \overline{BC} + \overline{CA} \cdot \overline{CB}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $25$
- **(B)** $27$
- **(C)** $29$
- **(D)** $31$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $29$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Since $ABC$ is a right-angled triangle and $BC$ is the longest side, the right angle must be at $A$.

Therefore, $\overline{AB} \cdot \overline{AC} = 0$.

The given expression is $\overline{AB} \cdot \overline{AC} + \overline{BA} \cdot \overline{BC} + \overline{CA} \cdot \overline{CB}$.

Substituting $\overline{AB} \cdot \overline{AC} = 0$, we get:

$0 + \overline{BA} \cdot (\overline{BA} + \overline{AC}) + \overline{CA} \cdot (\overline{CA} + \overline{AB})$

$= |\overline{BA}|^2 + \overline{BA} \cdot \overline{AC} + |\overline{CA}|^2 + \overline{CA} \cdot \overline{AB}$

Since $\overline{BA} \cdot \overline{AC} = 0$ and $\overline{CA} \cdot \overline{AB} = 0$, the expression simplifies to:

$|\overline{BA}|^2 + |\overline{CA}|^2$

By Pythagoras theorem, $|\overline{BA}|^2 + |\overline{CA}|^2 = |\overline{BC}|^2$.

Given the position vectors of $B$ and $C$:

$\vec{B} = 3\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + \hat{k}$

$\vec{C} = 5\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 3\hat{k}$

$\overline{BC} = \vec{C} - \vec{B} = (5 - 3)\hat{i} + (1 - (-2))\hat{j} + (-3 - 1)\hat{k} = 2\hat{i} + 3\hat{j} - 4\hat{k}$

$|\overline{BC}|^2 = (2)^2 + (3)^2 + (-4)^2 = 4 + 9 + 16 = 29$

Thus, the value of the given expression is $29$.

Answer: $29$

</details>

---

### Q32. MHT CET 2026 (18 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $\bar{a}, \bar{b}, \bar{c}$ be three vectors of equal magnitude such that the angle between $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$ is $\alpha$, $\bar{b}$ and $\bar{c}$ is $\beta$, $\bar{c}$ and $\bar{a}$ is $\gamma$.
Then the minimum value of $\cos\alpha + \cos\beta + \cos\gamma$ is ...

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{1}{2}$
- **(B)** $-\dfrac{1}{2}$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{3}{2}$
- **(D)** $-\dfrac{3}{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $-\dfrac{3}{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the magnitude of the three vectors be $|\vec{a}| = |\vec{b}| = |\vec{c}| = x$.

The square of the magnitude of the sum of the three vectors is given by:

$|\vec{a} + \vec{b} + \vec{c}|^2 = |\vec{a}|^2 + |\vec{b}|^2 + |\vec{c}|^2 + 2(\vec{a} \cdot \vec{b} + \vec{b} \cdot \vec{c} + \vec{c} \cdot \vec{a})$

Since the square of the magnitude of any vector is always non-negative:

$|\vec{a} + \vec{b} + \vec{c}|^2 \ge 0$

$|\vec{a}|^2 + |\vec{b}|^2 + |\vec{c}|^2 + 2(|\vec{a}||\vec{b}|\cos\alpha + |\vec{b}||\vec{c}|\cos\beta + |\vec{c}||\vec{a}|\cos\gamma) \ge 0$

Substituting the magnitudes:

$x^2 + x^2 + x^2 + 2(x^2\cos\alpha + x^2\cos\beta + x^2\cos\gamma) \ge 0$

$3x^2 + 2x^2(\cos\alpha + \cos\beta + \cos\gamma) \ge 0$

Since $x^2 > 0$ (assuming non-zero vectors), dividing the entire inequality by $x^2$ gives:

$3 + 2(\cos\alpha + \cos\beta + \cos\gamma) \ge 0$

$2(\cos\alpha + \cos\beta + \cos\gamma) \ge -3$

$\cos\alpha + \cos\beta + \cos\gamma \ge -\dfrac{3}{2}$

Therefore, the minimum value of $\cos\alpha + \cos\beta + \cos\gamma$ is $-\dfrac{3}{2}$.

</details>

---

### Q33. MHT CET 2026 (18 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A vector which is orthogonal to the vector $\bar{a} = \hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$ and coplanar with the vectors $\bar{b} = 3\hat{i} + 2\hat{j}$ and $\bar{c} = 2\hat{i} + \hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $25\hat{i} + 19\hat{j} - 21\hat{k}$
- **(B)** $-25\hat{i} + 19\hat{j} - 21\hat{k}$
- **(C)** $-25\hat{i} + 19\hat{j} + 21\hat{k}$
- **(D)** $25\hat{i} + 19\hat{j} + 21\hat{k}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $25\hat{i} + 19\hat{j} - 21\hat{k}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the required vector be $\bar{v}$.

Since $\bar{v}$ is coplanar with $\bar{b}$ and $\bar{c}$, and orthogonal to $\bar{a}$, it is parallel to the vector triple product $\bar{a} \times (\bar{b} \times \bar{c})$.

Using the vector triple product expansion:

$\bar{v} = \bar{a} \times (\bar{b} \times \bar{c}) = (\bar{a} \cdot \bar{c})\bar{b} - (\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b})\bar{c}$

Given vectors are:

$\bar{a} = \hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$

$\bar{b} = 3\hat{i} + 2\hat{j}$

$\bar{c} = 2\hat{i} + \hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$

Calculating the dot products:

$\bar{a} \cdot \bar{c} = (1)(2) + (2)(1) + (3)(3) = 2 + 2 + 9 = 13$

$\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b} = (1)(3) + (2)(2) + (3)(0) = 3 + 4 + 0 = 7$

Substituting these values into the expansion:

$\bar{v} = 13(3\hat{i} + 2\hat{j}) - 7(2\hat{i} + \hat{j} + 3\hat{k})$

$\bar{v} = (39\hat{i} + 26\hat{j}) - (14\hat{i} + 7\hat{j} + 21\hat{k})$

$\bar{v} = (39 - 14)\hat{i} + (26 - 7)\hat{j} - 21\hat{k}$

$\bar{v} = 25\hat{i} + 19\hat{j} - 21\hat{k}$

Answer: $25\hat{i} + 19\hat{j} - 21\hat{k}$

</details>

---

### Q34. MHT CET 2026 (17 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $\vec{a}$ and $\vec{b}$ be linearly independent vectors such that
$|\vec{a}| = \sqrt{3}, |\vec{b}| = 3$ and $|\vec{a} - \vec{b}| = 4$.
If $\vec{a} \times (2\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} - \hat{k}) = (2\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} - \hat{k}) \times \vec{b}$ and $|(\vec{a} + \vec{b}) \cdot (3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} + 2\hat{k})| = \sqrt{\lambda}$, then $\lambda = \ldots$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $32$
- **(B)** $64$
- **(C)** $256$
- **(D)** $128$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $128$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $|\vec{a} - \vec{b}| = 4$, squaring both sides gives:

$|\vec{a}|^2 + |\vec{b}|^2 - 2\vec{a} \cdot \vec{b} = 16$

Substituting $|\vec{a}| = \sqrt{3}$ and $|\vec{b}| = 3$:

$3 + 9 - 2\vec{a} \cdot \vec{b} = 16 \Rightarrow 2\vec{a} \cdot \vec{b} = -4$

Now, calculating $|\vec{a} + \vec{b}|^2$:

$|\vec{a} + \vec{b}|^2 = |\vec{a}|^2 + |\vec{b}|^2 + 2\vec{a} \cdot \vec{b} = 3 + 9 - 4 = 8$

Let $\vec{c} = 2\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} - \hat{k}$. The given cross product relation is:

$\vec{a} \times \vec{c} = \vec{c} \times \vec{b}$

$\vec{a} \times \vec{c} = -\vec{b} \times \vec{c}$

$(\vec{a} + \vec{b}) \times \vec{c} = \vec{0}$

This implies that $\vec{a} + \vec{b}$ is parallel to $\vec{c}$. Thus, $\vec{a} + \vec{b} = t\vec{c}$ for some scalar $t$.

Taking the magnitude on both sides:

$|\vec{a} + \vec{b}| = |t| |\vec{c}|$

Since $|\vec{c}| = \sqrt{2^2 + 2^2 + (-1)^2} = 3$ and $|\vec{a} + \vec{b}| = \sqrt{8} = 2\sqrt{2}$, we get:

$2\sqrt{2} = |t| (3) \Rightarrow |t| = \dfrac{2\sqrt{2}}{3}$

We need to evaluate $|(\vec{a} + \vec{b}) \cdot (3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} + 2\hat{k})|$. Let $\vec{d} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$.

$|(\vec{a} + \vec{b}) \cdot \vec{d}| = |t\vec{c} \cdot \vec{d}| = |t| |\vec{c} \cdot \vec{d}|$

Calculating the dot product $\vec{c} \cdot \vec{d}$:

$\vec{c} \cdot \vec{d} = (2)(3) + (2)(4) + (-1)(2) = 6 + 8 - 2 = 12$

Substituting the values:

$|(\vec{a} + \vec{b}) \cdot \vec{d}| = \dfrac{2\sqrt{2}}{3} \times 12 = 8\sqrt{2}$

Given that this value is $\sqrt{\lambda}$:

$\sqrt{\lambda} = 8\sqrt{2} = \sqrt{128}$

$\lambda = 128$

Answer: $128$

</details>

---

### Q35. MHT CET 2026 (17 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The value of $b$ such that the scalar product of the vector $\hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}$ with the unit vector parallel to the sum of the vectors $2\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} - 5\hat{k}$ and $b\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$ is one, is...

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-2$
- **(B)** $-1$
- **(C)** $0$
- **(D)** $1$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $1$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the sum of the given vectors be $\vec{v}$.

$\vec{v} = (2\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} - 5\hat{k}) + (b\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}) = (b+2)\hat{i} + 6\hat{j} - 2\hat{k}$

The unit vector parallel to $\vec{v}$ is $\hat{v} = \dfrac{(b+2)\hat{i} + 6\hat{j} - 2\hat{k}}{\sqrt{(b+2)^2 + 6^2 + (-2)^2}}$

The scalar product of $\hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}$ with $\hat{v}$ is $1$.

$(\hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}) \cdot \dfrac{(b+2)\hat{i} + 6\hat{j} - 2\hat{k}}{\sqrt{(b+2)^2 + 40}} = 1$

$\dfrac{(b+2) + 6 - 2}{\sqrt{(b+2)^2 + 40}} = 1$

$b + 6 = \sqrt{(b+2)^2 + 40}$

Squaring both sides:

$(b+6)^2 = (b+2)^2 + 40$

$b^2 + 12b + 36 = b^2 + 4b + 4 + 40$

$12b + 36 = 4b + 44$

$8b = 8$

$b = 1$

Answer: $1$

</details>

---

### Q36. MHT CET 2026 (17 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Let $\vec{a} = \lambda\hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}, \vec{b} = 2\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} + 4\hat{k}, \vec{c} = \hat{i} + \mu\hat{j} + \hat{k}$
If $\vec{a}$ is parallel to $\vec{b}$ and $\vec{b}$ is perpendicular to $\vec{c}$ then $\lambda - \mu = \ldots$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-2$
- **(B)** $-1$
- **(C)** $2$
- **(D)** $1$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $\vec{a} = \lambda\hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}$ and $\vec{b} = 2\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} + 4\hat{k}$.

Since $\vec{a}$ is parallel to $\vec{b}$, their corresponding components are proportional:

$\dfrac{\lambda}{2} = \dfrac{1}{4} = \dfrac{1}{4}$

$\Rightarrow \lambda = \dfrac{1}{2}$

Given $\vec{c} = \hat{i} + \mu\hat{j} + \hat{k}$.

Since $\vec{b}$ is perpendicular to $\vec{c}$, their dot product is zero:

$\vec{b} \cdot \vec{c} = 0$

$(2)(1) + (4)(\mu) + (4)(1) = 0$

$2 + 4\mu + 4 = 0$

$4\mu = -6$

$\Rightarrow \mu = -\dfrac{3}{2}$

Calculating $\lambda - \mu$:

$\lambda - \mu = \dfrac{1}{2} - \left(-\dfrac{3}{2}\right) = \dfrac{1}{2} + \dfrac{3}{2} = 2$

Answer: $2$

</details>

---

### Q37. MHT CET 2026 (16 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $\bar{a} = 4\hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}$, $\bar{b} = 2\hat{i} + \hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$ and $\bar{c} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} + 5\hat{k}$, then $(\bar{a} + \bar{b}) \cdot (\bar{b} + \bar{c}) = $

**Options:**

- **(A)** $30$
- **(B)** $21$
- **(C)** $61$
- **(D)** $10$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $61$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given vectors are $\bar{a} = 4\hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}$, $\bar{b} = 2\hat{i} + \hat{j} + 2\hat{k}$ and $\bar{c} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} + 5\hat{k}$.

Calculating $\bar{a} + \bar{b}$:

$\bar{a} + \bar{b} = (4+2)\hat{i} + (1+1)\hat{j} + (1+2)\hat{k} = 6\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$

Calculating $\bar{b} + \bar{c}$:

$\bar{b} + \bar{c} = (2+3)\hat{i} + (1+4)\hat{j} + (2+5)\hat{k} = 5\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} + 7\hat{k}$

Taking the dot product:

$(\bar{a} + \bar{b}) \cdot (\bar{b} + \bar{c}) = (6\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}) \cdot (5\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} + 7\hat{k})$

$= (6 \times 5) + (2 \times 5) + (3 \times 7)$

$= 30 + 10 + 21 = 61$

Answer: $61$

</details>

---

### Q38. MHT CET 2026 (16 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If a vector $3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} - 5\hat{k}$ is rotated through a certain angle about the origin in the anti-clockwise direction, then the components of the new vector are $a + 1, -3, 5$ . The possible values of $a$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 5 or 3
- **(B)** 5 or -3
- **(C)** 4 or -2
- **(D)** -5 or 3

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) -5 or 3**

**Detailed Solution:**

When a vector is rotated about the origin, its magnitude remains unchanged.

Let the initial vector be $\vec{v}_1 = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} - 5\hat{k}$.

Magnitude of $\vec{v}_1$ is $|\vec{v}_1| = \sqrt{3^2 + 4^2 + (-5)^2} = \sqrt{9 + 16 + 25} = \sqrt{50}$.

Let the new vector be $\vec{v}_2 = (a+1)\hat{i} - 3\hat{j} + 5\hat{k}$.

Magnitude of $\vec{v}_2$ is $|\vec{v}_2| = \sqrt{(a+1)^2 + (-3)^2 + 5^2} = \sqrt{(a+1)^2 + 9 + 25} = \sqrt{(a+1)^2 + 34}$.

Since $|\vec{v}_1| = |\vec{v}_2|$, we have:

$\sqrt{50} = \sqrt{(a+1)^2 + 34}$

Squaring both sides:

$50 = (a+1)^2 + 34$

$(a+1)^2 = 16$

$a+1 = \pm 4$

$a = 3$ or $a = -5$

Answer: -5 or 3

</details>

---

### Q39. MHT CET 2026 (16 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let A, B, C, D be the points in the plane with position vectors $-2\hat{i} - \hat{j}$, $4\hat{i}$, $3\hat{i} + 3\hat{j}$ and $-3\hat{i} + 2\hat{j}$ respectively, then $\square$ABCD is

**Options:**

- **(A)** a parallelogram which is neither a rhombus nor a reactangle
- **(B)** a square
- **(C)** a rectangle but not a square
- **(D)** a rhombus but not a square

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) a parallelogram which is neither a rhombus nor a reactangle**

**Detailed Solution:**

The position vectors of the vertices are given as $\vec{A} = -2\hat{i} - \hat{j}$, $\vec{B} = 4\hat{i}$, $\vec{C} = 3\hat{i} + 3\hat{j}$, and $\vec{D} = -3\hat{i} + 2\hat{j}$.

The vectors representing the sides of the quadrilateral are:
$\vec{AB} = \vec{B} - \vec{A} = (4 - (-2))\hat{i} + (0 - (-1))\hat{j} = 6\hat{i} + \hat{j}$
$\vec{BC} = \vec{C} - \vec{B} = (3 - 4)\hat{i} + (3 - 0)\hat{j} = -\hat{i} + 3\hat{j}$
$\vec{CD} = \vec{D} - \vec{C} = (-3 - 3)\hat{i} + (2 - 3)\hat{j} = -6\hat{i} - \hat{j}$
$\vec{DA} = \vec{A} - \vec{D} = (-2 - (-3))\hat{i} + (-1 - 2)\hat{j} = \hat{i} - 3\hat{j}$

Since $\vec{AB} = -\vec{CD}$ and $\vec{BC} = -\vec{DA}$, the opposite sides are equal and parallel. Thus, ABCD is a parallelogram.

The lengths of adjacent sides are:
$|\vec{AB}| = \sqrt{6^2 + 1^2} = \sqrt{37}$
$|\vec{BC}| = \sqrt{(-1)^2 + 3^2} = \sqrt{10}$

Since $|\vec{AB}| \neq |\vec{BC}|$, the adjacent sides are not equal in length, so ABCD is not a rhombus.

The dot product of adjacent side vectors is:
$\vec{AB} \cdot \vec{BC} = (6)(-1) + (1)(3) = -6 + 3 = -3$

Since $\vec{AB} \cdot \vec{BC} \neq 0$, the angle between adjacent sides is not $90^{\circ}$, so ABCD is not a rectangle.

Therefore, ABCD is a parallelogram which is neither a rhombus nor a rectangle.

Answer: a parallelogram which is neither a rhombus nor a reactangle

</details>

---

### Q40. MHT CET 2026 (16 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A parallelogram is constructed on $5\bar{a} + 2\bar{b}$ and $\bar{a} - 3\bar{b}$ as its adjacent sides, with $|\bar{a}| = 2\sqrt{2}, |\bar{b}| = 3$ . The angle between $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$ is $\dfrac{\pi}{4}$ . Then the length of the diagonals of the parallelogram are

**Options:**

- **(A)** $15, \sqrt{593}$
- **(B)** $15,\ 593$
- **(C)** $225,\ 593$
- **(D)** $20,\ 593$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $15, \sqrt{593}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the adjacent sides of the parallelogram be $\vec{u} = 5\bar{a} + 2\bar{b}$ and $\vec{v} = \bar{a} - 3\bar{b}$.

The diagonals of the parallelogram are given by the sum and difference of the adjacent sides:

$\vec{d}_1 = \vec{u} + \vec{v} = (5\bar{a} + 2\bar{b}) + (\bar{a} - 3\bar{b}) = 6\bar{a} - \bar{b}$

$\vec{d}_2 = \vec{u} - \vec{v} = (5\bar{a} + 2\bar{b}) - (\bar{a} - 3\bar{b}) = 4\bar{a} + 5\bar{b}$

Given $|\bar{a}| = 2\sqrt{2}$, $|\bar{b}| = 3$, and the angle between $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$ is $\dfrac{\pi}{4}$.

The dot product of $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$ is:

$\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b} = |\bar{a}| |\bar{b}| \cos\left(\dfrac{\pi}{4}\right) = (2\sqrt{2})(3)\left(\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\right) = 6$

Now, finding the length of the first diagonal:

$|\vec{d}_1|^2 = |6\bar{a} - \bar{b}|^2 = 36|\bar{a}|^2 - 12(\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b}) + |\bar{b}|^2$

$|\vec{d}_1|^2 = 36(8) - 12(6) + 9 = 288 - 72 + 9 = 225$

$|\vec{d}_1| = \sqrt{225} = 15$

Finding the length of the second diagonal:

$|\vec{d}_2|^2 = |4\bar{a} + 5\bar{b}|^2 = 16|\bar{a}|^2 + 40(\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b}) + 25|\bar{b}|^2$

$|\vec{d}_2|^2 = 16(8) + 40(6) + 25(9) = 128 + 240 + 225 = 593$

$|\vec{d}_2| = \sqrt{593}$

The lengths of the diagonals are $15$ and $\sqrt{593}$.

Answer: $15, \sqrt{593}$

</details>

---

### Q41. MHT CET 2026 (15 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $\vec{a} + \vec{b} + \vec{c} = \vec{0}$, $|\vec{a}| = |\vec{b}| = |\vec{c}| = 3$ and $\theta$ is the angle between $\vec{b}$ and $\vec{c}$ then $\tan^2\theta + \cot^2\theta =$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{2}{3}$
- **(B)** $\dfrac{5}{3}$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{8}{3}$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{10}{3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\dfrac{10}{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $\vec{a} + \vec{b} + \vec{c} = \vec{0}$

$\vec{b} + \vec{c} = -\vec{a}$

Squaring both sides, we get

$|\vec{b}|^2 + |\vec{c}|^2 + 2\vec{b}\cdot\vec{c} = |\vec{a}|^2$

Substituting $|\vec{a}| = |\vec{b}| = |\vec{c}| = 3$,

$3^2 + 3^2 + 2|\vec{b}||\vec{c}|\cos\theta = 3^2$

$9 + 9 + 2(3)(3)\cos\theta = 9$

$18 + 18\cos\theta = 9$

$18\cos\theta = -9 \Rightarrow \cos\theta = -\dfrac{1}{2}$

This gives $\theta = 120^\circ$

Now, $\tan^2\theta + \cot^2\theta = \tan^2(120^\circ) + \cot^2(120^\circ)$

$= (-\sqrt{3})^2 + \left(-\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\right)^2$

$= 3 + \dfrac{1}{3} = \dfrac{10}{3}$

Answer: $\dfrac{10}{3}$

</details>

---

### Q42. MHT CET 2026 (15 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If ABCDEF is a regular hexagon and $\overline{AB} + \overline{AC} + \overline{AD} + \overline{AE} + \overline{AF} = p\overline{AD} = q\overline{AO}$, where O is the center of the hexagon, then the values of $p$ and $q$ respectively are

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2, 3$
- **(B)** $4, 6$
- **(C)** $3, 6$
- **(D)** $3, 5$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $3, 6$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $O$ be the origin. The position vectors of the vertices $A, B, C, D, E, F$ are $\vec{a}, \vec{b}, \vec{c}, \vec{d}, \vec{e}, \vec{f}$ respectively.

Since $ABCDEF$ is a regular hexagon with center $O$, the position vectors of opposite vertices are negatives of each other. Thus, $\vec{d} = -\vec{a}$, $\vec{e} = -\vec{b}$, and $\vec{f} = -\vec{c}$.

The given sum of vectors is:

$\overrightarrow{AB} + \overrightarrow{AC} + \overrightarrow{AD} + \overrightarrow{AE} + \overrightarrow{AF}$

$= (\vec{b} - \vec{a}) + (\vec{c} - \vec{a}) + (\vec{d} - \vec{a}) + (\vec{e} - \vec{a}) + (\vec{f} - \vec{a})$

$= \vec{b} + \vec{c} + \vec{d} + \vec{e} + \vec{f} - 5\vec{a}$

Substituting $\vec{d} = -\vec{a}$, $\vec{e} = -\vec{b}$, and $\vec{f} = -\vec{c}$:

$= \vec{b} + \vec{c} - \vec{a} - \vec{b} - \vec{c} - 5\vec{a}$

$= -6\vec{a}$

The vector $\overrightarrow{AO}$ is given by $\vec{0} - \vec{a} = -\vec{a}$.

Therefore, the sum is $6(-\vec{a}) = 6\overrightarrow{AO}$.

Also, the vector $\overrightarrow{AD}$ is given by $\vec{d} - \vec{a} = -\vec{a} - \vec{a} = -2\vec{a} = 2\overrightarrow{AO}$.

Thus, the sum can be written as $3(-2\vec{a}) = 3\overrightarrow{AD}$.

Comparing this with $p\overrightarrow{AD} = q\overrightarrow{AO}$, we get $p = 3$ and $q = 6$.

Answer: $3, 6$

</details>

---

### Q43. MHT CET 2026 (15 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The vector $\bar{r}$ whose magnitude is $3\sqrt{2}$ units and which makes angles of $\dfrac{\pi}{4}$ and $\dfrac{\pi}{2}$ with the positive y- and z-axes respectively is....

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\hat{i} \pm 3\hat{j}$
- **(B)** $\hat{i} \pm \hat{j}$
- **(C)** $-\hat{i} \pm \hat{j}$
- **(D)** $\pm 3\hat{i} + 3\hat{j}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\pm 3\hat{i} + 3\hat{j}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the direction cosines of the vector $\bar{r}$ be $l, m, n$.

Given that the vector makes angles $\beta = \dfrac{\pi}{4}$ and $\gamma = \dfrac{\pi}{2}$ with the positive y- and z-axes respectively.

$m = \cos\left(\dfrac{\pi}{4}\right) = \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}$

$n = \cos\left(\dfrac{\pi}{2}\right) = 0$

Using the relation $l^2 + m^2 + n^2 = 1$:

$l^2 + \left(\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\right)^2 + 0 = 1$

$l^2 + \dfrac{1}{2} = 1 \Rightarrow l = \pm\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}$

The vector $\bar{r}$ is given by $|\bar{r}|(l\hat{i} + m\hat{j} + n\hat{k})$.

Substituting the values:

$\bar{r} = 3\sqrt{2} \left( \pm\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\hat{i} + \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\hat{j} + 0\hat{k} \right)$

$\bar{r} = \pm 3\hat{i} + 3\hat{j}$

Answer: $\pm 3\hat{i} + 3\hat{j}$

</details>

---

### Q44. MHT CET 2026 (15 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $(\bar{p} \wedge \bar{q})$ denote the angle between $\bar{p}$ and $\bar{q}$. If $\bar{a} + \bar{b} + \bar{c} = \bar{0}, |\bar{a}| = 7, |\bar{b}| = 5$ and $|\bar{c}| = 3$ then (take $\pi = \dfrac{22}{7}$)

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sin(\bar{b} \wedge \bar{c}) = \dfrac{1}{2}$
- **(B)** $\cos(\bar{a} \wedge \bar{c}) = -\dfrac{\pi}{4}$
- **(C)** $\cos(\bar{b} \wedge \bar{c}) = -\dfrac{1}{2}$
- **(D)** $\sin(\bar{a} \wedge \bar{c}) = \dfrac{\pi}{4}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\cos(\bar{a} \wedge \bar{c}) = -\dfrac{\pi}{4}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $\bar{a} + \bar{b} + \bar{c} = \bar{0}$, we can write $\bar{a} + \bar{c} = -\bar{b}$.

Squaring both sides, we get:

$|\bar{a}|^2 + |\bar{c}|^2 + 2|\bar{a}||\bar{c}|\cos(\bar{a} \wedge \bar{c}) = |\bar{b}|^2$

Substituting the given values $|\bar{a}| = 7$, $|\bar{b}| = 5$, and $|\bar{c}| = 3$:

$7^2 + 3^2 + 2(7)(3)\cos(\bar{a} \wedge \bar{c}) = 5^2$

$49 + 9 + 42\cos(\bar{a} \wedge \bar{c}) = 25$

$58 + 42\cos(\bar{a} \wedge \bar{c}) = 25$

$42\cos(\bar{a} \wedge \bar{c}) = -33$

$\cos(\bar{a} \wedge \bar{c}) = -\dfrac{33}{42} = -\dfrac{11}{14}$

Given $\pi = \dfrac{22}{7}$, we can rewrite the expression as:

$-\dfrac{11}{14} = -\dfrac{1}{4} \left(\dfrac{22}{7}\right) = -\dfrac{\pi}{4}$

Therefore, $\cos(\bar{a} \wedge \bar{c}) = -\dfrac{\pi}{4}$.

Answer: $\cos(\bar{a} \wedge \bar{c}) = -\dfrac{\pi}{4}$

</details>

---

### Q45. MHT CET 2026 (13 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $\bar{a} = \hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}, \bar{b} = \hat{i}, \bar{c} = c_1\hat{i} + c_2\hat{j} + c_3\hat{k}$ with $c_1 = 1$, $c_2 = 2$, then value of $c_3$ such that $\bar{a}, \bar{b}, \bar{c}$ are coplanar is ____

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2$
- **(B)** $-1$
- **(C)** $0$
- **(D)** $-2$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given vectors are $\bar{a} = \hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}$, $\bar{b} = \hat{i}$, and $\bar{c} = \hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + c_3\hat{k}$.

For the vectors to be coplanar, their scalar triple product must be zero. This means the determinant of their components is zero:

$\begin{vmatrix} 1 & 1 & 1 \\ 1 & 0 & 0 \\ 1 & 2 & c_3 \end{vmatrix} = 0$

Expanding the determinant along the second row:

$-1(c_3 - 2) = 0$

$c_3 = 2$

Answer: $2$

</details>

---

### Q46. MHT CET 2026 (13 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $\bar{a}, \bar{b}$ and $\bar{c}$ are non-coplanar unit vectors such that the angle between any two of them is $60^\circ$, and the vector $\bar{d} = x\bar{a} + y\bar{b} + z\bar{c}$ is perpendicular to both $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$, then the value of $\dfrac{(x+y)}{z}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-\dfrac{1}{2}$
- **(B)** $-\dfrac{2}{3}$
- **(C)** $-1$
- **(D)** $0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $-\dfrac{2}{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $|\bar{a}| = |\bar{b}| = |\bar{c}| = 1$ and the angle between any two vectors is $60^\circ$.

$\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b} = \bar{b} \cdot \bar{c} = \bar{c} \cdot \bar{a} = 1 \times 1 \times \cos 60^\circ = \dfrac{1}{2}$

Since $\bar{d}$ is perpendicular to $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$, we have $\bar{d} \cdot \bar{a} = 0$ and $\bar{d} \cdot \bar{b} = 0$.

Substituting $\bar{d} = x\bar{a} + y\bar{b} + z\bar{c}$ into $\bar{d} \cdot \bar{a} = 0$:

$(x\bar{a} + y\bar{b} + z\bar{c}) \cdot \bar{a} = 0$

$x|\bar{a}|^2 + y(\bar{b} \cdot \bar{a}) + z(\bar{c} \cdot \bar{a}) = 0$

$x(1) + y\left(\dfrac{1}{2}\right) + z\left(\dfrac{1}{2}\right) = 0$

$2x + y + z = 0$

Similarly, substituting $\bar{d}$ into $\bar{d} \cdot \bar{b} = 0$:

$(x\bar{a} + y\bar{b} + z\bar{c}) \cdot \bar{b} = 0$

$x(\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b}) + y|\bar{b}|^2 + z(\bar{c} \cdot \bar{b}) = 0$

$x\left(\dfrac{1}{2}\right) + y(1) + z\left(\dfrac{1}{2}\right) = 0$

$x + 2y + z = 0$

Adding the two equations, we get:

$(2x + y + z) + (x + 2y + z) = 0$

$3x + 3y + 2z = 0$

$3(x + y) = -2z$

$\dfrac{x + y}{z} = -\dfrac{2}{3}$

Answer: $-\dfrac{2}{3}$

</details>

---

### Q47. MHT CET 2026 (13 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $\bar{a}, \bar{b}, \bar{c}$ be unit vectors such that $\bar{a}$ is perpendicular to the plane of $\bar{b}$ and $\bar{c}$. If the angle between $\bar{b}$ and $\bar{c}$ is $\dfrac{\pi}{3}$, then $|\bar{a} + \bar{b} + \bar{c}| =$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1$
- **(B)** $2$
- **(C)** $4$
- **(D)** $16$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $|\bar{a}| = |\bar{b}| = |\bar{c}| = 1$

Since $\bar{a}$ is perpendicular to the plane of $\bar{b}$ and $\bar{c}$, $\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b} = 0$ and $\bar{a} \cdot \bar{c} = 0$

The angle between $\bar{b}$ and $\bar{c}$ is $\dfrac{\pi}{3}$, so $\bar{b} \cdot \bar{c} = |\bar{b}||\bar{c}|\cos\left(\dfrac{\pi}{3}\right) = \dfrac{1}{2}$

We know that $|\bar{a} + \bar{b} + \bar{c}|^2 = |\bar{a}|^2 + |\bar{b}|^2 + |\bar{c}|^2 + 2(\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b} + \bar{b} \cdot \bar{c} + \bar{c} \cdot \bar{a})$

Substituting the values:

$|\bar{a} + \bar{b} + \bar{c}|^2 = 1 + 1 + 1 + 2\left(0 + \dfrac{1}{2} + 0\right)$

$|\bar{a} + \bar{b} + \bar{c}|^2 = 3 + 1 = 4$

$|\bar{a} + \bar{b} + \bar{c}| = 2$

Answer: $2$

</details>

---

### Q48. MHT CET 2026 (13 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If a parallelogram is constructed on the vectors $\bar{a} = 3\bar{p} - \bar{q}, \bar{b} = \bar{p} + 3\bar{q}$ and $|\bar{p}| = 3, |\bar{q}| = 2$ and angle between $\bar{p}$ and $\bar{q}$ is $\dfrac{\pi}{3}$, then the ratio of the lengths of adjacent sides $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$ of the parallelogram is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{57} : \sqrt{54}$
- **(B)** $\sqrt{67} : \sqrt{63}$
- **(C)** $\sqrt{63} : \sqrt{47}$
- **(D)** $\sqrt{57} : \sqrt{52}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\sqrt{67} : \sqrt{63}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $\bar{a} = 3\bar{p} - \bar{q}$ and $\bar{b} = \bar{p} + 3\bar{q}$.

We are given $|\bar{p}| = 3$, $|\bar{q}| = 2$, and the angle between $\bar{p}$ and $\bar{q}$ is $\dfrac{\pi}{3}$.

The dot product of $\bar{p}$ and $\bar{q}$ is:

$\bar{p} \cdot \bar{q} = |\bar{p}| |\bar{q}| \cos\left(\dfrac{\pi}{3}\right) = 3 \times 2 \times \dfrac{1}{2} = 3$

The length of side $\bar{a}$ is given by:

$|\bar{a}|^2 = |3\bar{p} - \bar{q}|^2 = 9|\bar{p}|^2 + |\bar{q}|^2 - 6(\bar{p} \cdot \bar{q})$

$|\bar{a}|^2 = 9(3)^2 + (2)^2 - 6(3) = 81 + 4 - 18 = 67$

$\Rightarrow |\bar{a}| = \sqrt{67}$

The length of side $\bar{b}$ is given by:

$|\bar{b}|^2 = |\bar{p} + 3\bar{q}|^2 = |\bar{p}|^2 + 9|\bar{q}|^2 + 6(\bar{p} \cdot \bar{q})$

$|\bar{b}|^2 = (3)^2 + 9(2)^2 + 6(3) = 9 + 36 + 18 = 63$

$\Rightarrow |\bar{b}| = \sqrt{63}$

The ratio of the lengths of adjacent sides $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$ is $|\bar{a}| : |\bar{b}| = \sqrt{67} : \sqrt{63}$.

Answer: $\sqrt{67} : \sqrt{63}$

</details>

---

### Q49. MHT CET 2026 (13 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A vector $\bar{r}$ of magnitude $3\sqrt{2}$ units which makes angles of $\dfrac{\pi}{4}$ and $\dfrac{\pi}{2}$ respectively with Y and Z axes is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\bar{r} = \pm 3\hat{i} + 3\hat{j}$
- **(B)** $\bar{r} = \hat{i} + \hat{j}$
- **(C)** $\bar{r} = \pm 2\hat{i} + 3\hat{j}$
- **(D)** $\bar{r} = \pm 5\hat{i} + \hat{j}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\bar{r} = \pm 3\hat{i} + 3\hat{j}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the direction cosines of the vector $\bar{r}$ be $l, m, n$.

Given that the vector makes angles of $\dfrac{\pi}{4}$ and $\dfrac{\pi}{2}$ with the Y and Z axes respectively, we have:
$m = \cos\left(\dfrac{\pi}{4}\right) = \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}$
$n = \cos\left(\dfrac{\pi}{2}\right) = 0$

Using the relation $l^2 + m^2 + n^2 = 1$:
$l^2 + \left(\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\right)^2 + 0^2 = 1$
$l^2 + \dfrac{1}{2} = 1$
$l^2 = \dfrac{1}{2} \Rightarrow l = \pm \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}$

The vector $\bar{r}$ is given by:
$\bar{r} = |\bar{r}|(l\hat{i} + m\hat{j} + n\hat{k})$

Substituting the given magnitude $|\bar{r}| = 3\sqrt{2}$ and the direction cosines:
$\bar{r} = 3\sqrt{2}\left(\pm \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\hat{i} + \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\hat{j} + 0\hat{k}\right)$
$\bar{r} = \pm 3\hat{i} + 3\hat{j}$

Answer: $\bar{r} = \pm 3\hat{i} + 3\hat{j}$

</details>

---

### Q50. MHT CET 2026 (11 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The vector $\bar{a} + 3\bar{b}$ is perpendicular to $7\bar{a} - 5\bar{b}$ and the vector $\bar{a} - 4\bar{b}$ is perpendicular to $7\bar{a} - 2\bar{b}$. Then the angle between $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{\pi}{2}$
- **(B)** $\dfrac{\pi}{4}$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{\pi}{6}$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{\pi}{3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\dfrac{\pi}{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given that the vectors are perpendicular, their dot products are zero:

$(\bar{a} + 3\bar{b}) \cdot (7\bar{a} - 5\bar{b}) = 0$

$7|\bar{a}|^2 + 16(\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b}) - 15|\bar{b}|^2 = 0$

Also,

$(\bar{a} - 4\bar{b}) \cdot (7\bar{a} - 2\bar{b}) = 0$

$7|\bar{a}|^2 - 30(\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b}) + 8|\bar{b}|^2 = 0$

Subtracting the second equation from the first, we get:

$46(\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b}) - 23|\bar{b}|^2 = 0$

$\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b} = \dfrac{1}{2}|\bar{b}|^2$
