Substituting the given vectors:

$\vec{a} \cdot \vec{c} = (\alpha \hat{i} + 3 \hat{j} - \hat{k}) \cdot (\hat{i} + 2 \hat{j} - 2 \hat{k}) = \alpha + 6 + 2 = \alpha + 8$

$|\vec{c}| = \sqrt{1^2 + 2^2 + (-2)^2} = \sqrt{9} = 3$

Equating the projection to $\dfrac{10}{3}$:

$\dfrac{\alpha + 8}{3} = \dfrac{10}{3} \Rightarrow \alpha = 2$

Now, calculating the cross product $\vec{b} \times \vec{c}$:

$\vec{b} \times \vec{c} = (3 \hat{i} - \beta \hat{j} + 4 \hat{k}) \times (\hat{i} + 2 \hat{j} - 2 \hat{k})$

$\vec{b} \times \vec{c} = (2\beta - 8)\hat{i} - (-6 - 4)\hat{j} + (6 + \beta)\hat{k}$

$\vec{b} \times \vec{c} = (2\beta - 8)\hat{i} + 10\hat{j} + (\beta + 6)\hat{k}$

It is given that $\vec{b} \times \vec{c} = -6 \hat{i} + 10 \hat{j} + 7 \hat{k}$. Comparing the coefficients:

$2\beta - 8 = -6 \Rightarrow 2\beta = 2 \Rightarrow \beta = 1$

$\beta + 6 = 7 \Rightarrow \beta = 1$

Therefore, the value of $2\alpha + \beta$ is:

$2(2) + 1 = 5$

Answer: 5

</details>

---

### Q93. MHT CET 2024 (03 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $\overline{\mathrm{a}}=\hat{\mathrm{j}}-\hat{\mathrm{k}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{c}}=\hat{\mathrm{i}}-\hat{\mathrm{j}}-\hat{\mathrm{k}}$. Then the vector $\overline{\mathrm{b}}$ satisfying $\overline{\mathrm{a}} \times \overline{\mathrm{b}}+\overline{\mathrm{c}}=\overline{0}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{b}}=3$, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-\hat{i}+\hat{j}-2 \hat{k}$
- **(B)** $2 \hat{i}-\hat{j}+2 \hat{k}$
- **(C)** $\hat{i}-\hat{j}-2 \hat{k}$
- **(D)** $\hat{i}+\hat{j}-2 \hat{k}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $-\hat{i}+\hat{j}-2 \hat{k}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $\overline{a} \times \overline{b} + \overline{c} = \overline{0}$

$\Rightarrow \overline{a} \times \overline{b} = -\overline{c}$

Taking the cross product with $\overline{a}$ on both sides:

$\overline{a} \times (\overline{a} \times \overline{b}) = -\overline{a} \times \overline{c}$

Using the vector triple product expansion:

$(\overline{a} \cdot \overline{b})\overline{a} - (\overline{a} \cdot \overline{a})\overline{b} = -\overline{a} \times \overline{c}$

We are given $\overline{a} \cdot \overline{b} = 3$. Also, $\overline{a} \cdot \overline{a} = |\overline{a}|^2 = (1)^2 + (-1)^2 = 2$.

Substituting these values:

$3\overline{a} - 2\overline{b} = -\overline{a} \times \overline{c}$

$\Rightarrow 2\overline{b} = 3\overline{a} + \overline{a} \times \overline{c}$

Now, calculating $\overline{a} \times \overline{c}$:

$\overline{a} \times \overline{c} = (\hat{j} - \hat{k}) \times (\hat{i} - \hat{j} - \hat{k}) = -2\hat{i} - \hat{j} - \hat{k}$

Substituting $\overline{a}$ and $\overline{a} \times \overline{c}$ into the equation for $2\overline{b}$:

$2\overline{b} = 3(\hat{j} - \hat{k}) + (-2\hat{i} - \hat{j} - \hat{k})$

$2\overline{b} = -2\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} - 4\hat{k}$

$\Rightarrow \overline{b} = -\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 2\hat{k}$

Answer: $-\hat{i}+\hat{j}-2 \hat{k}$

</details>

---

### Q94. MHT CET 2024 (03 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the vectors $\overline{\mathrm{AB}}=3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+4 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{AC}}=5 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+4 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$ are the sides of the triangle $A B C$, then the length of the median through A is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{45}$ units
- **(B)** $\sqrt{18}$ units
- **(C)** $\sqrt{72}$ units
- **(D)** $\sqrt{33}$ units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\sqrt{33}$ units**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\overline{AD}$ be the median through the vertex $A$ of the triangle $ABC$.

The vector $\overline{AD}$ is given by $\dfrac{\overline{AB} + \overline{AC}}{2}$.

Substituting the given vectors:

$\overline{AD} = \dfrac{(3\hat{i} + 4\hat{k}) + (5\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + 4\hat{k})}{2}$

$\overline{AD} = \dfrac{8\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} + 8\hat{k}}{2} = 4\hat{i} - \hat{j} + 4\hat{k}$

The length of the median is the magnitude of $\overline{AD}$:

$|\overline{AD}| = \sqrt{4^2 + (-1)^2 + 4^2} = \sqrt{16 + 1 + 16} = \sqrt{33}$ units.

Answer: $\sqrt{33}$ units

</details>

---

### Q95. MHT CET 2024 (03 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $\mathrm{L}_1$ $\dfrac{x+1}{3}=\dfrac{y+2}{2}=\dfrac{z+1}{1}$ and $L_2: \dfrac{x-2}{2}=\dfrac{y+2}{1}=\dfrac{z-3}{3}$ be the given lines. Then the unit vector perpendicular to $L_1$ and $L_2$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{-5 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{k}}}{\sqrt{78}}$
- **(B)** $\dfrac{5 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}}{5 \sqrt{3}}$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{5 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-\hat{\mathrm{k}}}{5 \sqrt{3}}$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{5 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-\hat{\mathrm{k}}}{5 \sqrt{3}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\dfrac{5 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-\hat{\mathrm{k}}}{5 \sqrt{3}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The direction vector of line $L_1$ is $\vec{b}_1 = 3\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + \hat{k}$

The direction vector of line $L_2$ is $\vec{b}_2 = 2\hat{i} + \hat{j} + 3\hat{k}$

A vector perpendicular to both lines is given by their cross product:

$\vec{b}_1 \times \vec{b}_2 = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 3 & 2 & 1 \\ 2 & 1 & 3 \end{vmatrix}$

$= \hat{i}(6 - 1) - \hat{j}(9 - 2) + \hat{k}(3 - 4)$

$= 5\hat{i} - 7\hat{j} - \hat{k}$

The magnitude of this vector is:

$|\vec{b}_1 \times \vec{b}_2| = \sqrt{5^2 + (-7)^2 + (-1)^2} = \sqrt{25 + 49 + 1} = \sqrt{75} = 5\sqrt{3}$

The unit vector perpendicular to both lines is:

$\dfrac{\vec{b}_1 \times \vec{b}_2}{|\vec{b}_1 \times \vec{b}_2|} = \dfrac{5\hat{i} - 7\hat{j} - \hat{k}}{5\sqrt{3}}$

Answer: $\dfrac{5 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-7 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-\hat{\mathrm{k}}}{5 \sqrt{3}}$

</details>

---

### Q96. MHT CET 2024 (03 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

If \(\bar{a}=a_1 \hat{i}+a_2 \hat{j}+a_3 \hat{k}, \bar{b}=b_1 \hat{i}+b_2 \hat{j}+b_3 \hat{k} \quad\) and \(\bar{c}=c_1 \hat{i}+c_2 \hat{j}+c_3 \hat{k}\) are non-zero noncoplanar vectors and \(m\) is non-zero scalar such that \([\mathrm{m} \overline{\mathrm{a}}+\overline{\mathrm{b}} \quad \mathrm{m} \overline{\mathrm{b}}+\overline{\mathrm{c}} \mathrm{m} \overline{\mathrm{c}}+\overline{\mathrm{a}}]=28[\overline{\mathrm{a}} \overline{\mathrm{b}} \overline{\mathrm{c}}]\), then the value of \(m\) is equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2
- **(B)** 3
- **(C)** 4
- **(D)** 7

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 3**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given scalar triple product is $[m\bar{a}+\bar{b} \quad m\bar{b}+\bar{c} \quad m\bar{c}+\bar{a}]$.

This can be written using the determinant of the coefficients of $\bar{a}, \bar{b}, \bar{c}$ as:

$[m\bar{a}+\bar{b} \quad m\bar{b}+\bar{c} \quad m\bar{c}+\bar{a}] = \begin{vmatrix} m & 1 & 0 \\ 0 & m & 1 \\ 1 & 0 & m \end{vmatrix} [\bar{a} \quad \bar{b} \quad \bar{c}]$

Expanding the determinant, we get:

$m(m^2 - 0) - 1(0 - 1) + 0 = m^3 + 1$

Thus, $[m\bar{a}+\bar{b} \quad m\bar{b}+\bar{c} \quad m\bar{c}+\bar{a}] = (m^3 + 1) [\bar{a} \quad \bar{b} \quad \bar{c}]$

Given that $[m\bar{a}+\bar{b} \quad m\bar{b}+\bar{c} \quad m\bar{c}+\bar{a}] = 28 [\bar{a} \quad \bar{b} \quad \bar{c}]$

Equating the two expressions, we get:

$(m^3 + 1) [\bar{a} \quad \bar{b} \quad \bar{c}] = 28 [\bar{a} \quad \bar{b} \quad \bar{c}]$

Since $\bar{a}, \bar{b}, \bar{c}$ are non-coplanar, $[\bar{a} \quad \bar{b} \quad \bar{c}] \neq 0$. Dividing both sides by $[\bar{a} \quad \bar{b} \quad \bar{c}]$ gives:

$m^3 + 1 = 28$

$m^3 = 27$

$m = 3$

Answer: 3

</details>

---

### Q97. MHT CET 2024 (03 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The vectors $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$ are not perpendicular and $\overline{\mathrm{c}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{d}}$ are two vectors satisfying $\overline{\mathrm{b}} \times \overline{\mathrm{c}}=\overline{\mathrm{b}} \times \overline{\mathrm{d}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{d}}=0$, then the vector $\overline{\mathrm{d}}$ is equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\bar{b}+\left(\dfrac{\bar{b} \cdot \bar{c}}{\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b}}\right) \bar{c}$
- **(B)** $\overline{\mathrm{c}}-\left(\dfrac{\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{c}}}{\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{b}}}\right) \overline{\mathrm{b}}$
- **(C)** $\bar{b}-\left(\dfrac{\bar{b} \cdot \bar{c}}{\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b}}\right) \bar{c}$
- **(D)** $\overline{\mathrm{c}}+\left(\dfrac{\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{c}}}{\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{b}}}\right) \overline{\mathrm{b}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\overline{\mathrm{c}}-\left(\dfrac{\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{c}}}{\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{b}}}\right) \overline{\mathrm{b}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $\bar{b} \times \bar{c} = \bar{b} \times \bar{d}$

$\bar{b} \times \bar{c} - \bar{b} \times \bar{d} = \bar{0}$

$\bar{b} \times (\bar{c} - \bar{d}) = \bar{0}$

This implies that the vector $(\bar{c} - \bar{d})$ is parallel to $\bar{b}$.

$\bar{c} - \bar{d} = \lambda \bar{b}$ for some scalar $\lambda$

$\bar{d} = \bar{c} - \lambda \bar{b}$

Taking the dot product with $\bar{a}$ on both sides:

$\bar{a} \cdot \bar{d} = \bar{a} \cdot (\bar{c} - \lambda \bar{b})$

Since $\bar{a} \cdot \bar{d} = 0$, we have:

$0 = \bar{a} \cdot \bar{c} - \lambda (\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b})$

$\lambda = \dfrac{\bar{a} \cdot \bar{c}}{\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b}}$

Substituting the value of $\lambda$ back into the expression for $\bar{d}$:

$\bar{d} = \bar{c} - \left(\dfrac{\bar{a} \cdot \bar{c}}{\bar{a} \cdot \bar{b}}\right) \bar{b}$

Answer: $\overline{\mathrm{c}}-\left(\dfrac{\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{c}}}{\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{b}}}\right) \overline{\mathrm{b}}$

</details>

---

### Q98. MHT CET 2024 (03 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $\overline{\mathrm{a}}, \overline{\mathrm{b}}, \overline{\mathrm{c}}$ be three non-coplanar vectors and $\overline{\mathrm{p}}, \overline{\mathrm{q}}, \overline{\mathrm{r}}$ defined by the relations
$\overline{\mathrm{p}}=\dfrac{\overline{\mathrm{b}} \times \overline{\mathrm{c}}}{[\overline{\mathrm{a}} \overline{\mathrm{~b}} \overline{\mathrm{c}}]}, \overline{\mathrm{q}}=\dfrac{\overline{\mathrm{c}} \times \overline{\mathrm{a}}}{[\overline{\mathrm{a}} \overline{\mathrm{~b}} \overline{\mathrm{c}}]}, \overline{\mathrm{r}}=\dfrac{\overline{\mathrm{a}} \times \overline{\mathrm{b}}}{[\overline{\mathrm{a}} \overline{\mathrm{~b}} \overline{\mathrm{c}}]}$
then the value of the expression $(\bar{a}+\bar{b}) \cdot \bar{p}+(\bar{b}+\bar{c}) \cdot \bar{q}+(\bar{c}+\bar{a}) \cdot \bar{r}$ is equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0
- **(B)** 1
- **(C)** 2
- **(D)** 3

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 3**

**Detailed Solution:**

The vectors $\bar{p}, \bar{q}, \bar{r}$ form a reciprocal system of vectors to $\bar{a}, \bar{b}, \bar{c}$.

By the definition of reciprocal vectors, we have the following dot products:
$\bar{a} \cdot \bar{p} = \dfrac{\bar{a} \cdot (\bar{b} \times \bar{c})}{[\bar{a} \bar{b} \bar{c}]} = \dfrac{[\bar{a} \bar{b} \bar{c}]}{[\bar{a} \bar{b} \bar{c}]} = 1$
$\bar{b} \cdot \bar{p} = \dfrac{\bar{b} \cdot (\bar{b} \times \bar{c})}{[\bar{a} \bar{b} \bar{c}]} = 0$

Similarly, for the other vectors:
$\bar{b} \cdot \bar{q} = 1, \quad \bar{c} \cdot \bar{q} = 0$
$\bar{c} \cdot \bar{r} = 1, \quad \bar{a} \cdot \bar{r} = 0$

Expanding the given expression:
$(\bar{a}+\bar{b}) \cdot \bar{p} + (\bar{b}+\bar{c}) \cdot \bar{q} + (\bar{c}+\bar{a}) \cdot \bar{r}$

$= (\bar{a} \cdot \bar{p} + \bar{b} \cdot \bar{p}) + (\bar{b} \cdot \bar{q} + \bar{c} \cdot \bar{q}) + (\bar{c} \cdot \bar{r} + \bar{a} \cdot \bar{r})$

Substituting the values of the dot products:
$= (1 + 0) + (1 + 0) + (1 + 0)$

$= 1 + 1 + 1 = 3$

Answer: 3

</details>

---

### Q99. MHT CET 2024 (03 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $\mathrm{A} \equiv(1,-1,0), \mathrm{B} \equiv(0,1,-1)$ and $\mathrm{C} \equiv(-1,0,1)$, then the unit vector $\overline{\mathrm{d}}$ such that $\overline{\mathrm{a}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{d}}$ are perpendiculars and $\overline{\mathrm{b}}, \overline{\mathrm{c}}, \overline{\mathrm{d}}$ are coplanar is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $+\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(1,1,1)$
- **(B)** $+\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(-1,-1,1)$
- **(C)** $+\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{6}}(1,1,-2)$
- **(D)** $+\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(1,1,0)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $+\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{6}}(1,1,-2)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the position vectors be $\overline{\mathrm{a}} = \hat{i} - \hat{j}$, $\overline{\mathrm{b}} = \hat{j} - \hat{k}$, and $\overline{\mathrm{c}} = -\hat{i} + \hat{k}$.

Let the required unit vector be $\overline{\mathrm{d}} = x\hat{i} + y\hat{j} + z\hat{k}$.

Since $\overline{\mathrm{d}}$ is perpendicular to $\overline{\mathrm{a}}$, their dot product is zero:

$\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{d}} = 0$

$(1)(x) + (-1)(y) + (0)(z) = 0 \Rightarrow x = y$

Since $\overline{\mathrm{b}}, \overline{\mathrm{c}}, \overline{\mathrm{d}}$ are coplanar, their scalar triple product is zero:

$\begin{vmatrix} 0 & 1 & -1 \\ -1 & 0 & 1 \\ x & y & z \end{vmatrix} = 0$

Expanding the determinant, we get:

$0(0 - y) - 1(-z - x) - 1(-y - 0) = 0$

$x + y + z = 0$

Substituting $y = x$ into the equation, we obtain:

$2x + z = 0 \Rightarrow z = -2x$

Thus, the vector $\overline{\mathrm{d}}$ is of the form $x(\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 2\hat{k})$.

Since $\overline{\mathrm{d}}$ is a unit vector, its magnitude is $1$:

$|x|\sqrt{1^2 + 1^2 + (-2)^2} = 1$

$|x|\sqrt{6} = 1 \Rightarrow x = \pm \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{6}}$

Therefore, the required unit vector is $\overline{\mathrm{d}} = \pm \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{6}}(\hat{i} + \hat{j} - 2\hat{k})$, which can be written as $\pm \dfrac{1}{\sqrt{6}}(1, 1, -2)$.

Answer: $+\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{6}}(1,1,-2)$

</details>

---

### Q100. MHT CET 2024 (02 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The unit vector which is orthogonal to the vector $5 \hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+6 \hat{k}$ and is coplanar with the vectors $2 \hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k}$ and $\hat{i}-\hat{j}+\hat{k}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\dfrac{2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-6 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}}{\sqrt{41}}$
- **(B)** $\dfrac{2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-5 \hat{\mathrm{j}}}{\sqrt{29}}$
- **(C)** $\dfrac{-3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}}{\sqrt{10}}$
- **(D)** $\dfrac{2 \hat{i}-8 \hat{j}+\hat{k}}{69}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\dfrac{-3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}}{\sqrt{10}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the required vector be $\vec{v} = x \hat{i} + y \hat{j} + z \hat{k}$.

Since $\vec{v}$ is coplanar with $\vec{b} = 2 \hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}$ and $\vec{c} = \hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k}$, it must be perpendicular to their cross product $\vec{b} \times \vec{c}$.

$\vec{b} \times \vec{c} = (2 \hat{i} + \hat{j} + \hat{k}) \times (\hat{i} - \hat{j} + \hat{k}) = 2 \hat{i} - \hat{j} - 3 \hat{k}$

Therefore, $\vec{v} \cdot (\vec{b} \times \vec{c}) = 0 \Rightarrow 2x - y - 3z = 0$.

Since $\vec{v}$ is orthogonal to $\vec{a} = 5 \hat{i} + 2 \hat{j} + 6 \hat{k}$, we have $\vec{v} \cdot \vec{a} = 0 \Rightarrow 5x + 2y + 6z = 0$.

Solving the two equations $2x - y - 3z = 0$ and $5x + 2y + 6z = 0$:

From the first equation, $y = 2x - 3z$.

Substituting into the second equation:

$5x + 2(2x - 3z) + 6z = 0$

$9x = 0 \Rightarrow x = 0$

Substituting $x = 0$ back gives $y = -3z$.

Thus, the vector $\vec{v}$ is of the form $z(-3 \hat{j} + \hat{k})$.

The magnitude of $-3 \hat{j} + \hat{k}$ is $\sqrt{(-3)^2 + 1^2} = \sqrt{10}$.

The required unit vector is $\pm \dfrac{-3 \hat{j} + \hat{k}}{\sqrt{10}}$.

Answer: $\dfrac{-3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}}{\sqrt{10}}$

</details>

---

### Q101. MHT CET 2024 (02 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The vector equation of a line whose Cartesian equations are $y=2,4 x-3 z+5=0$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=(3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+4 \mathrm{k})+\lambda\left(2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\dfrac{5}{3} \hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)$
- **(B)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=(3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+4 \mathrm{k})+\lambda\left(2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-\dfrac{5}{3} \hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)$
- **(C)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\left(2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\dfrac{5}{3} \hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)+\lambda(3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+4 \mathrm{k})$
- **(D)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\left(2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-\dfrac{5}{3} \hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)+\lambda(3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+4 \mathrm{k})$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\left(2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\dfrac{5}{3} \hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)+\lambda(3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+4 \mathrm{k})$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given Cartesian equations of the line are $y=2$ and $4x-3z+5=0$.

We can rewrite the second equation as:
$4x = 3z - 5$
$\Rightarrow \dfrac{x}{3} = \dfrac{z - \dfrac{5}{3}}{4}$

Since $y = 2$, we can write the symmetric form of the line as:
$\dfrac{x - 0}{3} = \dfrac{y - 2}{0} = \dfrac{z - \dfrac{5}{3}}{4}$

From this symmetric form, the line passes through the point $(0, 2, \dfrac{5}{3})$ and has direction ratios proportional to $(3, 0, 4)$.

The position vector of the point on the line is $\vec{a} = 2\hat{j} + \dfrac{5}{3}\hat{k}$.
The direction vector of the line is $\vec{b} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{k}$.

The vector equation of a line is given by $\vec{r} = \vec{a} + \lambda\vec{b}$.

Substituting $\vec{a}$ and $\vec{b}$, we get:
$\vec{r} = \left(2\hat{j} + \dfrac{5}{3}\hat{k}\right) + \lambda(3\hat{i} + 4\hat{k})$

Answer: $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\left(2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\dfrac{5}{3} \hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)+\lambda(3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+4 \mathrm{k})$

</details>

### Q102. MHT CET 2023 (14 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $\overline{\mathrm{u}}, \overline{\mathrm{v}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{w}}$ be the vectors such that $|\overline{\mathrm{u}}|=1$; $|\overline{\mathrm{v}}|=2 ;|\overline{\mathrm{w}}|=3$. If the projection of $\overline{\mathrm{v}}$ along $\overline{\mathrm{u}}$ is equal to that of $\bar{w}$ along $\bar{u}$ and $\bar{v}, \bar{w}$ are perpendicular to each other, then $|\bar{u}-\bar{v}+\bar{w}|$ is equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2$
- **(B)** $\sqrt 7$
- **(C)** $\sqrt {14}$
- **(D)** $14$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\sqrt {14}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Projection of $\overline{\mathrm{v}}$ along $\overline{\mathrm{u}}=$ Projection of $\overline{\mathrm{w}}$ along $\overline{\mathrm{u}}$
$\begin{aligned}
& \Rightarrow \frac{\overline{\mathrm{v}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{u}}}{|\overline{\mathrm{u}}|}=\frac{\overline{\mathrm{w}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{u}}}{|\overline{\mathrm{u}}|} \\
& \Rightarrow \overline{\mathrm{v}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{u}}=\overline{\mathrm{w}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{u}}... (i)
\end{aligned}$
Also, $\bar{v}$ and $\bar{w}$ are perpendicular to each other.
$\therefore \quad \overline{\mathrm{v}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{w}}=0... (ii)$
Now, $|\overline{\mathrm{u}}-\overline{\mathrm{v}}+\overline{\mathrm{w}}|^2=|\overline{\mathrm{u}}|^2+|\overline{\mathrm{v}}|^2+|\overline{\mathrm{w}}|^2-2(\overline{\mathrm{u}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{v}})$
$-2(\overline{\mathrm{v}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{w}})+2(\overline{\mathrm{u}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{w}})$
$\Rightarrow|\overline{\mathrm{u}}-\overline{\mathrm{v}}+\overline{\mathrm{w}}|^2=1+4+9$
$\Rightarrow|\bar{u}-\bar{v}+\bar{w}|=\sqrt{14}$

</details>

---

### Q103. MHT CET 2023 (14 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $|\overline{\mathrm{a}}|=2,|\overline{\mathrm{b}}|=3,|\overline{\mathrm{c}}|=5$ and each of the angles between the vectors $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}, \bar{b}$ and $\bar{c}$, $\overline{\mathrm{c}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{a}}$ is $60^{\circ}$, then the value of $|\overline{\mathrm{a}}+\overline{\mathrm{b}}+\overline{\mathrm{c}}|$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt {69}$
- **(B)** $\sqrt {70}$
- **(C)** $\sqrt {80}$
- **(D)** $\sqrt {39}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\sqrt {69}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} \overline{\mathrm{a} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{b}}} & =|\overrightarrow{\mathrm{a}}||\overrightarrow{\mathrm{b}}| \cos 60^{\circ} \\ & =(2)(3)\left(\frac{1}{2}\right) \\ & =3 \\ \overline{\mathrm{b}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{c}} & =|\overline{\mathrm{b}}||\overline{\mathrm{c}}| \cos 60^{\circ} \\ & =(3)(5)\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)=\frac{15}{2}\end{aligned}$
$\begin{aligned} \overline{\mathrm{a} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{c}}} & =|\overrightarrow{\mathrm{a}}||\overrightarrow{\mathrm{c}}| \cos 60^{\circ} \\ & =(2)(5)\left(\frac{1}{2}\right) \\ & =5 \\ \therefore \quad \mid \overline{\mathrm{a}}+\overline{\mathrm{b}}+\overline{\mathrm{c}}^2 & =\left.\left|\overline{\mathrm{a}}^2+\right| \overrightarrow{\mathrm{b}}\right|^2+|\overline{\mathrm{c}}|^2+2(\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{b}}+\overline{\mathrm{b}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{c}}+\overline{\mathrm{c}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{a}}) \\ & =2^2+3^2+5^2+2\left(3+\frac{15}{2}+5\right) \\ & =4+9+25+31 \\ & =69 \\ \therefore \quad|\overline{\mathrm{a}}+\overline{\mathrm{b}}+\overline{\mathrm{c}}| & =\sqrt{69}\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q104. MHT CET 2023 (14 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

If $\overline{\mathrm{a}}, \overline{\mathrm{b}}, \overline{\mathrm{c}}$ are three vectors such that $|\overline{\mathrm{a}}+\overline{\mathrm{b}}+\overline{\mathrm{c}}|=1$, $\overline{\mathrm{c}}=\lambda(\overline{\mathrm{a}} \times \overline{\mathrm{b}})$ and $|\overline{\mathrm{a}}|=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}},|\overline{\mathrm{b}}|=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}},|\overline{\mathrm{c}}|=\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}$, then the angle between $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{\pi}{6}$
- **(B)** $\frac{\pi}{4}$
- **(C)** $\frac{\pi}{3}$
- **(D)** $\frac{\pi}{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\frac{\pi}{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\theta$ be the angle between $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$.
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \text { Since } \overline{\mathrm{c}}=\lambda(\overline{\mathrm{a}} \times \overline{\mathrm{b}}) \\
& \Rightarrow \overline{\mathrm{c}} \perp \overline{\mathrm{a}}, \overline{\mathrm{c}} \perp \overline{\mathrm{b}} \\
& \Rightarrow \overline{\mathrm{c}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{a}}=\overline{\mathrm{c}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{b}}=0
\end{aligned}
$$

Now,
$$
\begin{aligned}
& |\overline{\mathrm{a}}+\overline{\mathrm{b}}+\overline{\mathrm{c}}|=1 \\
& \Rightarrow|\overline{\mathrm{a}}+\overline{\mathrm{b}}+\overline{\mathrm{c}}|^2=1 \\
& \Rightarrow|\overline{\mathrm{a}}|^2+|\overline{\mathrm{b}}|^2+|\overline{\mathrm{c}}|^2+2(\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{b}}+\overline{\mathrm{b}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{c}}+\overline{\mathrm{c}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{a}})=1 \\
& \Rightarrow \frac{1}{3}+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{6}+2\{|\overline{\mathrm{a}}||\overline{\mathrm{b}}| \cos \theta\}=1 \\
& \Rightarrow \cos \theta=0 \\
& \Rightarrow \theta=\frac{\pi}{2}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q105. MHT CET 2023 (13 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the vectors $p \hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k}, \hat{i}+q \hat{j}+\hat{k}$ and $\hat{i}+\hat{j}+r \hat{k}$ $(\mathrm{p} \neq \mathrm{q} \neq \mathrm{r} \neq 1)$ are coplanar, then the value of $\mathrm{pq} \mathrm{r}-(\mathrm{p}+\mathrm{q}+\mathrm{r})$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-2$
- **(B)** 2
- **(C)** 0
- **(D)** $-1$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $-2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Since $\mathrm{pi}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}, \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\mathrm{q} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}$ and $\hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\mathrm{rk}$ are coplanar,
$\left|\begin{array}{lll}p & 1 & 1 \\ 1 & q & 1 \\ 1 & 1 & r\end{array}\right|=0$
$\begin{aligned} & \Rightarrow p(q r-1)-1(r-1)+1(1-q)=0 \\ & \Rightarrow p q r-p-q-r+2=0 \\ & \Rightarrow p q r-(p+q+r)=-2\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q106. MHT CET 2023 (13 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $\overline{\mathrm{a}}, \overline{\mathrm{b}}, \overline{\mathrm{c}}$ are non-coplanar unit vectors such that $\overline{\mathrm{a}} \times(\overline{\mathrm{b}} \times \overline{\mathrm{c}})=\frac{\overline{\mathrm{b}} \times \overline{\mathrm{c}}}{\sqrt{2}}$, then the angle between $\overline{\mathrm{a}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{b}}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{3 \pi}{4}$
- **(B)** $\frac{\pi}{4}$
- **(C)** $\frac{\pi}{2}$
- **(D)** $\pi$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{3 \pi}{4}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\overline{\mathrm{a}} \times(\overline{\mathrm{b}} \times \overline{\mathrm{c}})=\frac{\overline{\mathrm{b}}+\overline{\mathrm{c}}}{\sqrt{2}}$
$\begin{aligned} & \Rightarrow(\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{c}}) \overline{\mathrm{b}}-(\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{b}}) \overline{\mathrm{c}}=\frac{\overline{\mathrm{b}}+\overline{\mathrm{c}}}{\sqrt{2}} \\ & \Rightarrow\left(\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{c}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\right) \overline{\mathrm{b}}-\left(\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{b}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\right) \overline{\mathrm{c}}=0\end{aligned}$
Since $\overline{\mathrm{a}}, \overline{\mathrm{b}}, \overline{\mathrm{c}}$ are non-coplanar unit vectors,
$\begin{aligned} & \bar{a} \cdot \bar{b}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}=0 \\ & \Rightarrow \bar{a} \cdot \bar{b}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}} \\ & \Rightarrow|\bar{a}||\bar{b}| \cos \theta=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}} \\ & \Rightarrow \cos \theta=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}} \\ & \Rightarrow \theta=\frac{3 \pi}{4}\end{aligned}$
[Note: In the question, $\frac{\overline{\mathrm{b}} \times \overline{\mathrm{c}}}{\sqrt{2}}$ is changed to $\frac{\overline{\mathrm{b}}+\overline{\mathrm{c}}}{\sqrt{2}}$ to apply appropriate textual concepts.]

</details>

### Q107. MHT CET 2023 (13 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A vector $\overline{\mathrm{a}}$ has components 1 and $2 \mathrm{p}$ with respect to a rectangular Cartesian system. This system is rotated through a certain angle about origin in the counter clock wise sense. If, with respect to the new system, $\bar{a}$ has components 1 and $(p+1)$, then

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{p}=1$ or $\mathrm{p}=\frac{1}{3}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{p}=-1$ or $\mathrm{p}=\frac{-1}{3}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{p}=\frac{-1}{3}$ or $\mathrm{p}=1$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{p}=\frac{1}{3}$ or $\mathrm{p}=-1$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\mathrm{p}=\frac{-1}{3}$ or $\mathrm{p}=1$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
\bar{a} & =1 \cdot \hat{i}+2 p \hat{j} \\
& =\hat{i}+2 p \hat{j}
\end{aligned}
$$
Let $\bar{b}$ be the vector obtained on rotation with components 1 and $(p+1)$. Then,
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \overline{\mathrm{b}}=\hat{\mathrm{i}}+(\mathrm{p}+1) \hat{\mathrm{j}} \\
& |\overline{\mathrm{a}}|=|\overline{\mathrm{b}}|
\end{aligned}
$$
...[Magnitude remains unchanged after rotation]
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \Rightarrow|\vec{a}|^2=|\bar{b}|^2 \\
& \Rightarrow 1+(2 p)^2=1+(p+1)^2 \\
& \Rightarrow 4 p^2=p^2+2 p+1 \\
& \Rightarrow 3 p^2-2 p-1=0 \\
& \Rightarrow(3 p+1)(p-1)=0 \\
& \Rightarrow p=-\frac{1}{3} \text { or } p=1
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q108. MHT CET 2023 (13 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $\overline{\mathrm{a}}=2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-4 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{b}}=\hat{\mathrm{i}}-\hat{\mathrm{j}}-\hat{\mathrm{k}}$, then the projection of $\bar{b}$ in the direction of $\bar{a}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{1}{\sqrt{29}}$
- **(B)** $\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}$
- **(C)** $\frac{5}{\sqrt{3}}$
- **(D)** $\frac{3}{\sqrt{29}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\frac{3}{\sqrt{29}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Projection of $\overline{\mathrm{b}}$ in the direction of $\overline{\mathrm{a}}=\frac{\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{b}}}{|\overline{\mathrm{a}}|}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& =\frac{(2 \hat{i}+3 \hat{j}-4 \hat{k}) \cdot(\hat{i}-\hat{j}-\hat{k})}{\sqrt{2^2+3^2+(-4)^2}} \\
& =\frac{2-3+4}{\sqrt{4+9+16}}=\frac{3}{\sqrt{29}}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q109. MHT CET 2023 (12 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $\bar{a}, \bar{b}, \bar{c}$ are three non-zero vectors, no two of them are collinear, $\bar{a}+2 \bar{b}$ is collinear with $\overline{\mathrm{c}}, \overline{\mathrm{b}}+3 \overline{\mathrm{c}}$ is collinear with $\overline{\mathrm{a}}$, then $\overline{\mathrm{a}}+2 \overline{\mathrm{b}}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $6 \bar{c}_c$
- **(B)** $-6 \bar{c}$
- **(C)** $\overline{\mathrm{c}}$
- **(D)** $2 \bar{c}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $-6 \bar{c}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{array}{ll} & \overline{\mathrm{a}}+2 \overline{\mathrm{b}} \text { is collinear with } \overline{\mathrm{c}} \\ \therefore \quad & \overline{\mathrm{a}}+2 \overline{\mathrm{b}}=\mathrm{n} \overline{\mathrm{c}} \\ & \text { Similarly } \overline{\mathrm{b}}+3 \overline{\mathrm{c}}=\mathrm{ma} \\ & \mathrm{m} \text { and } \mathrm{n} \text { are non-zero scalars. } \\ \therefore \quad & \text { (i) } \Rightarrow \overline{\mathrm{a}}+2 \overline{\mathrm{b}}+6 \overline{\mathrm{c}}=(\mathrm{n}+6) \overline{\mathrm{c}} \\ & \text { (ii) } \Rightarrow \overline{\mathrm{a}}+2 \overline{\mathrm{b}}+6 \overline{\mathrm{c}}=(2 \mathrm{~m}+1) \overline{\mathrm{a}} \\ \Rightarrow \mathrm{n}+6=0 \text { and } 2 \mathrm{~m}+1=0 \\ \Rightarrow \mathrm{n}=-6 \text { and } \mathrm{m}=\frac{-1}{2} \\ \therefore \quad & \text { (i) } \Rightarrow \mathrm{a}+2 \mathrm{~b}=-6 \overline{\mathrm{c}}\end{array}$

</details>

### Q110. MHT CET 2023 (12 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The unit vector which is orthogonal to the vector $3 \hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+6 \hat{k}$ and coplanar with the vectors $2 \hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k}$ and $\hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{8 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}}}{\sqrt{82}}$
- **(B)** $\frac{-8 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}}}{\sqrt{82}}$
- **(C)** $\frac{-8 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}}}{\sqrt{82}}$
- **(D)** $-\frac{8 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}}}{\sqrt{82}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\frac{-8 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}}}{\sqrt{82}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Consider option (C)
$$
(3 \hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+6 \hat{k}) \cdot\left(\frac{-8 \hat{i}+3 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k}}{\sqrt{82}}\right)=0
$$

This is valid for only option (C)
$\therefore \quad$ Option (C) is correct.

</details>

### Q111. MHT CET 2023 (12 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Two adjacent of sides parallelogram $\mathrm{ABCD}$ are given by $\overline{\mathrm{AB}}=2 \hat{i}+10 \hat{j}+11 \hat{k}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{AD}}=-\hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$. The side $\mathrm{AD}$ is rotated by angle $\alpha$ in plane of parallelogram so that $\mathrm{AD}$ becomes $\mathrm{AD}^{\prime}$. If $\mathrm{AD}^{\prime}$ makes a right angle with the side $A B$, then the cosine of the angle $\alpha$ is given by

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{8}{9}$
- **(B)** $\frac{1}{9}$
- **(C)** $\frac{\sqrt{17}}{9}$
- **(D)** $\frac{4 \sqrt{5}}{9}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\frac{\sqrt{17}}{9}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\theta$ be the angle between $\overline{\mathrm{AB}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{AD}}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
\therefore \quad \cos \theta & =\frac{\overline{\mathrm{AB}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{AD}}}{|\overline{\mathrm{AB}}||\overline{\mathrm{AD}}|} \\
& =\frac{(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+10 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+11 \hat{\mathrm{k}}) \cdot(-\hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{k}})}{\sqrt{4+100+121} \sqrt{1+4+4}} \\
& =\frac{-2+20+22}{\sqrt{225} \sqrt{9}} \\
& =\frac{40}{45} \\
& =\frac{8}{9} \\
\therefore \quad \sin \theta & =\sqrt{1-\left(\frac{8}{9}\right)^2}=\frac{\sqrt{17}}{9}
\end{aligned}
$$
$\alpha$ is the angle of rotation of $A D$
$\therefore \quad$ The angle between side $\mathrm{AB}$ and $\mathrm{AD}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& =\alpha+\theta \\
& =90^{\circ}
\end{aligned}
$$
$$
\begin{array}{ll}
\therefore & \cos (\alpha+\theta)=\cos \left(90^{\circ}\right) \\
\therefore & \cos \alpha \cos \theta-\sin \alpha \sin \theta=0 \\
\therefore & 8 \cos \alpha=\sqrt{17} \sin \alpha \\
\therefore & 64 \cos ^2 \alpha=17\left(1-\cos ^2 \alpha\right) \\
\therefore & 81 \cos ^2 \alpha=17 \\
\therefore & \cos \alpha=\frac{\sqrt{17}}{9}
\end{array}
$$

</details>

---

### Q112. MHT CET 2023 (12 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

$\overline{\mathrm{u}}, \overline{\mathrm{v}}, \overline{\mathrm{w}}$ are three vectors such that $|\overline{\mathrm{u}}|=1$, $|\vec{v}|=2,|\bar{w}|=3$. If the projection of $\bar{v}$ along $\bar{u}$ is equal to projection of $\overline{\mathrm{w}}$ along $\overline{\mathrm{u}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{v}}, \overline{\mathrm{w}}$ are perpendicular to each other, then $|\overline{\mathrm{u}}-\overline{\mathrm{v}}+\overline{\mathrm{w}}|=$

**Options:**

- **(A)** 4
- **(B)** $\sqrt{7}$
- **(C)** $\sqrt{14}$
- **(D)** 2

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\sqrt{14}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
|\overline{\mathrm{u}}|=1,|\overline{\mathrm{v}}|=2,|\overline{\mathrm{w}}|=3
$$
According to the given condition,
(Projection of $\bar{v}$ along $\bar{u}$ )
$$
=(\text { Projection of } \bar{w} \text { along } \bar{u})
$$
$$
\begin{array}{ll}
\therefore & \frac{\overline{\mathrm{v}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{u}}}{|\overline{\mathrm{u}}|}=\frac{\overline{\mathrm{w}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{u}}}{|\overline{\mathrm{u}}|} \\
\therefore & \overline{\mathrm{v}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{u}}=\overline{\mathrm{w}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{u}} \\
\therefore & (\overline{\mathrm{w}}-\overline{\mathrm{v}}) \cdot \overline{\mathrm{u}}=0
\end{array}
$$
Now consider, $|\overline{\mathrm{u}}-\overline{\mathrm{v}}+\overline{\mathrm{w}}|=\sqrt{|\overline{\mathrm{u}}+\overline{\mathrm{w}}-\overline{\mathrm{v}}|^2}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& =\sqrt{|\overline{\mathrm{u}}|^2+|\overline{\mathrm{w}}-\overline{\mathrm{v}}|^2+2 \overline{\mathrm{u}} \cdot(\overline{\mathrm{w}}-\overline{\mathrm{v}})} \\
& =\sqrt{(1)^2+|\overline{\mathrm{w}}-\overline{\mathrm{v}}|^2+0} \\
& =\sqrt{1+|\overline{\mathrm{w}}|^2+|\overline{\mathrm{v}}|^2-2(\overline{\mathrm{w}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{v}})} \\
& =\sqrt{1+9+4+0}
\end{aligned}
$$
$\ldots[\because \overline{\mathrm{w}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{v}}$ are perpendicular $]$
$$
=\sqrt{14}
$$

</details>

---

### Q113. MHT CET 2023 (12 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $\overline{\mathrm{a}}, \overline{\mathrm{b}}, \overline{\mathrm{c}}$ are three vectors, $|\overline{\mathrm{a}}|=2,|\overline{\mathrm{b}}|=4,|\overline{\mathrm{c}}|=1$, $|\overline{\mathrm{b}} \times \overline{\mathrm{c}}|=\sqrt{15}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{b}}=2 \overline{\mathrm{c}}+\lambda \overline{\mathrm{a}}$, then the value of $\lambda$, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2$
- **(B)** $2 \sqrt{2}$
- **(C)** $1$
- **(D)** $4$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $4$**

**Detailed Solution:**

If angle between $\bar{b}$ and $\bar{c}$ is $\alpha$ and
$$
\begin{aligned}
& |\overline{\mathrm{b}} \times \overline{\mathrm{c}}|=\sqrt{15} \\
& \Rightarrow|\overline{\mathrm{b}}||\overrightarrow{\mathrm{c}}| \sin \alpha=\sqrt{15} \\
& \Rightarrow \sin \alpha=\frac{\sqrt{15}}{4} \\
& \Rightarrow \cos \alpha=\frac{1}{4}
\end{aligned}
$$
Now, $\overline{\mathrm{b}}-2 \overline{\mathrm{c}}=\lambda \overline{\mathrm{a}}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \Rightarrow|\overline{\mathrm{b}}-2 \overline{\mathrm{c}}|^2=\lambda^2|\overline{\mathrm{b}}|^2 \\
& \Rightarrow|\overline{\mathrm{b}}|^2+4|\overline{\mathrm{c}}|^2-4 \overline{\mathrm{b}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{c}}=\left.\left.\lambda^2\right|^{-\overline{\mathrm{a}}}\right|^2 \\
& \Rightarrow 16+4-4\{|\overline{\mathrm{b}}||\overline{\mathrm{c}}| \cos \alpha\}=\lambda^2 \\
& \Rightarrow 16+4-4 \times 4 \times 1 \times \frac{1}{4}=\lambda^2 \\
& \Rightarrow \lambda^2=16 \\
& \Rightarrow \lambda= \pm 4
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

If $\bar{a}=\hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k}, \bar{b}=4 \hat{i}+3 \hat{j}+4 \hat{k}$ and $\bar{c}=\hat{i}+\alpha \hat{j}+\beta \hat{k}$ are linearly dependent vectors and $|\overline{\mathrm{c}}|=\sqrt{3}$, then the values of $\alpha$ and $\beta$ are respectively.

### Q115. MHT CET 2023 (11 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $\bar{a}=2 \hat{i}+3 \hat{j}+2 \hat{k}, \bar{b}=2 \hat{i}+\hat{j}-\hat{k}$ and $\bar{c}=3 \hat{i}-\hat{j} \quad$ are such that $\bar{a}+\lambda \bar{b}$ is perpendicular to $\bar{c}$, then the value of $\lambda$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{-1}{5}$
- **(B)** $3$
- **(C)** $\frac{3}{5}$
- **(D)** $\frac{-3}{5}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\frac{-3}{5}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

According to the given condition, we get
$\begin{array}{ll} 
& (\overline{\mathrm{a}}+\lambda \overline{\mathrm{b}}) \cdot \overline{\mathrm{c}}=0 \\
\therefore \quad & {[(2+2 \lambda) \hat{\mathrm{i}}+(3+\lambda) \hat{\mathrm{j}}+(2-\lambda) \hat{\mathrm{k}}] \cdot(3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\hat{\mathrm{j}})=0} \\
\therefore \quad & 3(2+2 \lambda)-(3+\lambda)=0 \\
\therefore \quad & 6+6 \lambda-3-\lambda=0 \\
\therefore \quad & 3+5 \lambda=0 \\
\therefore \quad & \lambda=\frac{-3}{5}
\end{array}$

</details>

### Q116. MHT CET 2023 (11 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the area of the parallelogram with $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$ as two adjacent sides is 16 sq. units, then the area of the parallelogram having $3 \bar{a}+2 \bar{b}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{a}}+3 \overline{\mathrm{b}}$ as two adjacent sides (in sq. units) is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $96$
- **(B)** $112$
- **(C)** $144$
- **(D)** $128$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $112$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Area of the parallelogram with $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$ as two adjacent sides is $|\overrightarrow{\mathrm{a}} \times \overrightarrow{\mathrm{b}}|$
$\therefore \quad|\overrightarrow{\mathrm{a}} \times \overrightarrow{\mathrm{b}}|=16$
$\therefore$ Area of the required parallelogram
$\begin{aligned}
& =|(3 \overline{\mathrm{a}}+2 \overline{\mathrm{b}}) \times(\overline{\mathrm{a}}+3 \overline{\mathrm{b}})| \\
& =|3(\overline{\mathrm{a}} \times \overline{\mathrm{a}})+9(\overline{\mathrm{a}} \times \overline{\mathrm{b}})+2(\overline{\mathrm{b}} \times \overline{\mathrm{a}})+(\overline{\mathrm{b}} \times \overline{\mathrm{b}})| \\
& =0+9|\overline{\mathrm{a}} \times \overline{\mathrm{b}}|-2|\overline{\mathrm{a}} \times \overline{\mathrm{b}}|+0 \\
& =7|\overline{\mathrm{a}} \times \overline{\mathrm{b}}| \\
& =7 \times 16 \\
& =112
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q117. MHT CET 2023 (10 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $\overline{\mathrm{a}}=\mathrm{m} \overline{\mathrm{b}}+\mathrm{n} \overline{\mathrm{c}}$, where $\overline{\mathrm{a}}=4 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+13 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-18 \hat{\mathrm{k}}, \overline{\mathrm{b}}=\hat{\mathrm{i}}-2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$, $\overline{\mathrm{c}}=2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-4 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$, then $\mathrm{m}+\mathrm{n}=$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1$
- **(B)** $2$
- **(C)** $3$
- **(D)** $-1$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $1$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given:
$\begin{aligned}
& \overline{\mathrm{a}}=4 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+13 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-18 \hat{\mathrm{k}} \\
& \overline{\mathrm{b}}=\hat{\mathrm{i}}-2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}} \\
& \overline{\mathrm{c}}=2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-4 \hat{\mathrm{k}}
\end{aligned}$
Also, $\bar{a}=m \bar{b}+n \bar{c}$
$\begin{aligned}
& \Rightarrow 4 \hat{i}+13 \hat{j}-18 \hat{k}=m(\hat{i}-2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})+n(2 \hat{i}+3 \hat{j}-4 \hat{k}) \\
& \Rightarrow 4 \hat{i}+13 \hat{j}-18 \hat{k} \\
& =(m+2 n) \hat{i}+(-2 m+3 n) \hat{j}+(3 m-4 n) \hat{k}
\end{aligned}$
Comparing, we get
$m+2 n=4 \text { and }-2 m+3 n=13$
Solving above equations, we get
$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{m}=-2 \text { and } \mathrm{n}=3 \\
\therefore \quad & \mathrm{m}+\mathrm{n}=-2+3=1
\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q118. MHT CET 2023 (09 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Two adjacent sides of a parallelogram are $2 \hat{i}-4 \hat{j}+5 \hat{k}$ and $\hat{i}-2 \hat{j}-3 \hat{k}$, then the unit vector parallel to its diagonal is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{3}{7} \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\frac{6}{7} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\frac{2}{7} \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
- **(B)** $\frac{2}{7} \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\frac{6}{7} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\frac{3}{7} \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
- **(C)** $\frac{6}{7} \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\frac{2}{7} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\frac{3}{7} \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
- **(D)** $\frac{1}{7} \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\frac{1}{7} \hat{\mathrm{j}}-\frac{3}{7} \hat{\mathrm{k}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{3}{7} \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\frac{6}{7} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\frac{2}{7} \hat{\mathrm{k}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\vec{a}$ and $\vec{b}$ be the adjacent sides of a parallelogram, where
$\begin{aligned}
& \overrightarrow{\mathrm{a}}=2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-4 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+5 \hat{\mathrm{k}} \\
& \overrightarrow{\mathrm{b}}=\hat{\mathrm{i}}-2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-3 \hat{\mathrm{k}}
\end{aligned}$
Let diagonal be $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{c}}$
$\begin{aligned}
\vec{c} & =\vec{a}+\vec{b} \\
\vec{c} & =2 \hat{i}-4 \hat{j}+5 \hat{k}+\hat{i}-2 \hat{j}-3 \hat{k} \\
& =3 \hat{i}-6 \hat{j}+2 \hat{k}
\end{aligned}$
$\text { Magnitude of } \begin{aligned}
\vec{c} & =\sqrt{3^2+(-6)^2+(2)^2} \\
& =\sqrt{49}=7
\end{aligned}$
$\therefore \quad$ Unit vector in direction of diagonal $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{c}}$ is
$\begin{aligned}
& =\frac{\overrightarrow{\mathrm{c}}}{|\overrightarrow{\mathrm{c}}|} \\
& =\frac{1}{7}(3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-6 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{k}}) \\
& =\frac{3}{7} \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\frac{6}{7} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\frac{2}{7} \hat{\mathrm{k}}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q119. MHT CET 2023 (09 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The magnitude of the projection of the vector $2 \hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k}$ on the vector perpendicular to the plane containing the vectors $\hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k}$ and $\hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{2}{\sqrt{6}}$
- **(B)** $\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}$
- **(C)** $\frac{5}{\sqrt{6}}$
- **(D)** $\frac{7}{\sqrt{6}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The vector perpendicular to both vectors containing $(\hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k})$ and $(\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})$ is
$\begin{aligned}
& =(\hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k}) \times(\hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k}) \\
& =\left|\begin{array}{lll}
\hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\
1 & 1 & 1 \\
1 & 2 & 3
\end{array}\right| \\
& =\hat{i}-2 \hat{j}+\hat{k}
\end{aligned}$
Therefore, the magnitude of the projection of vector $(2 \hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k})$ on $(\hat{i}-2 \hat{j}+\hat{k})$ is
$\begin{aligned}
& =\left|\frac{(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}})(\hat{\mathrm{i}}-2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}})}{\sqrt{1^2+(-2)^2+(1)^2}}\right| \\
& =\left|\frac{2-2+1}{\sqrt{6}}\right| \\
& =\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q120. MHT CET 2023 (09 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

$\bar{a}-\hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k}, \bar{b}=\hat{j}-\hat{k}$, then vector $\bar{r}$ satisfying $\overline{\mathrm{a}} \times \overline{\mathrm{r}}=\overline{\mathrm{b}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{r}}=3$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{5}{3} \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\frac{2}{3} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\frac{2}{3} \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
- **(B)** $-\frac{5}{3} \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\frac{2}{3} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\frac{2}{3} \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
- **(C)** $\frac{5}{3} \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\frac{2}{3} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\frac{2}{3} \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
- **(D)** $-\frac{5}{3} \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\frac{2}{3} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\frac{1}{3} \hat{\mathrm{k}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{5}{3} \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\frac{2}{3} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\frac{2}{3} \hat{\mathrm{k}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{r}}=3$
$\overline{\mathrm{a}} \times \overline{\mathrm{r}}=\overline{\mathrm{b}}$
Let $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=x \hat{\mathrm{i}}+y \hat{\mathrm{j}}+z \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
$\begin{aligned}
\overline{\mathrm{a}} \times \overline{\mathrm{r}} & =\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
\hat{\mathrm{i}} & \hat{\mathrm{j}} & \hat{\mathrm{k}} \\
1 & 1 & 1 \\
x & y & \mathrm{z}
\end{array}\right| \\
& =(\mathrm{z}-y) \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\hat{\mathrm{j}}(z-x)+\hat{\mathrm{k}}(y-x)
\end{aligned}$
Given $\overline{\mathrm{a}} \times \overline{\mathrm{r}}=\overline{\mathrm{b}}$
$\therefore \quad(z-y) \hat{\mathrm{i}}-(\mathrm{z}-x) \hat{\mathrm{j}}+(y-x) \hat{\mathrm{k}}=\hat{\mathrm{j}}-\hat{\mathrm{k}}$
Comparing
$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{z}-y=0 ... (i)\\
& \mathrm{z}-x=-1 ... (ii)\\
& y-x=-1 ... (iii)
\end{aligned}$
Also, $\overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{r}}=3$
$\begin{aligned}
& (\hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}})(x \hat{\mathrm{i}}+y \hat{\mathrm{j}}+z \hat{\mathrm{k}})=3 \\
& x+y+z=3
\end{aligned}$
Solving equations (i), (ii), (iii) and (iv), we get
$\begin{aligned}
& x=\frac{5}{3}, y=\frac{2}{3}, \mathrm{z}=\frac{2}{3} \\
\therefore \quad \overline{\mathrm{r}} & =\frac{5}{3} \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\frac{2}{3} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\frac{2}{3} \hat{\mathrm{k}}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q121. MHT CET 2023 (09 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Let two non-collinear vectors $\hat{a}$ and $\hat{b}$ form an acute angle. A point $\mathrm{P}$ moves, so that at any time $t$ the position vector $\overline{\mathrm{OP}}$, where $\mathrm{O}$ is origin, is given by $\hat{a} \sin t+\hat{b} \cos t$, when $P$ is farthest from origin $\mathrm{O}$, let $\mathrm{M}$ be the length of $\overline{\mathrm{OP}}$ and $\hat{\mathrm{u}}$ be the unit vector along $\overline{\mathrm{OP}}$, then

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\hat{\mathrm{u}}=\frac{\hat{\mathrm{a}}+\hat{\mathrm{b}}}{|\hat{\mathrm{a}}+\hat{\mathrm{b}}|}$ and $\mathrm{M}=(1+\hat{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \hat{\mathrm{b}})^{\frac{1}{2}}$
- **(B)** $\hat{\mathrm{u}}=\frac{\hat{\mathrm{a}}-\hat{\mathrm{b}}}{|\hat{\mathrm{a}}-\hat{\mathrm{b}}|}$ and $\mathrm{M}=(1+\hat{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \hat{\mathrm{b}})^{\frac{1}{2}}$
- **(C)** $\hat{\mathrm{u}}=\frac{\hat{\mathrm{a}}+\hat{\mathrm{b}}}{|\hat{\mathrm{a}}+\hat{\mathrm{b}}|}$ and $\mathrm{M}=(1+\hat{\mathrm{2a}} \cdot \hat{\mathrm{b}})^{\frac{1}{2}}$
- **(D)** $\hat{\mathrm{u}}=\frac{\hat{\mathrm{a}}-\hat{\mathrm{b}}}{|\hat{\mathrm{a}}-\hat{\mathrm{b}}|}$ and $\mathrm{M}=(1-\hat{\mathrm{2a}} \cdot \hat{\mathrm{b}})^{\frac{1}{2}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\hat{\mathrm{u}}=\frac{\hat{\mathrm{a}}+\hat{\mathrm{b}}}{|\hat{\mathrm{a}}+\hat{\mathrm{b}}|}$ and $\mathrm{M}=(1+\hat{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \hat{\mathrm{b}})^{\frac{1}{2}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{M}=|\overrightarrow{\mathrm{OP}}| \\
& M=\sqrt{(\hat{a} \sin t+\hat{b} \cos t)^2} \\
& =\sqrt{(\hat{a} \sin t)^2+(\hat{b} \cos t)^2+2(\hat{a} \sin t) \cdot(\hat{b} \cos t)} \\
& =\sqrt{\sin ^2 t+\cos ^2 t+\hat{a} \cdot \hat{b}(2 \sin t \cos t)} \\
& =\sqrt{1+\hat{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \hat{\mathrm{b}}(\sin 2 \mathrm{t})} \\
& \text { Maximum value of } \sin 2 t=1 \\
& \therefore \quad 2 \mathrm{t}=\sin ^{-1}(1) \\
& \therefore \quad \mathrm{t}=\frac{\pi}{4} \\
& \therefore \quad \mathrm{M}=\sqrt{1+\hat{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \hat{\mathrm{b}}(1)} \\
& =(1+\hat{a} \cdot \hat{b})^{\frac{1}{2}} \\
& \text { Now, } \hat{\mathrm{u}}=\frac{\overline{\mathrm{OP}}}{|\overline{\mathrm{OP}}|} \\
& =\frac{\hat{a} \sin t+\hat{b} \cos t}{|\hat{a} \sin t+\hat{b} \cos t|} \\
& =\frac{\hat{a}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\right)+\hat{b}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\right)}{\left|\hat{a}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\right)+\hat{b}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\right)\right|}\\
&
\end{aligned}$
Unit vector of OP is
$\hat{\mathrm{u}}=\frac{\hat{\mathrm{a}}+\hat{\mathrm{b}}}{|(\hat{\mathrm{a}}+\hat{\mathrm{b}})|}$

</details>

### Q122. MHT CET 2022 (11 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The sum of the distinct real values of $\mu$, for which the vector $\mu \hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k}, \hat{i}+\mu \hat{j}+\hat{k}, \hat{i}+\hat{j}+\mu \hat{k}$ are coplanar is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1
- **(B)** -1
- **(C)** 2
- **(D)** 0

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) -1**

**Detailed Solution:**

From the condition for co-planarity
$\begin{aligned}
& \left|\begin{array}{lll}
\mu & 1 & 1 \\
1 & \mu & 1 \\
1 & 1 & \mu
\end{array}\right|=0 \\
& \Rightarrow(2+\mu)(\mu-1)^2=0 \\
& \Rightarrow \mu=-2 \text { or } \mu=1 \\
& \text { sum }=-2+1=-1
\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q123. MHT CET 2022 (10 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the points $A(3,2,1), B(4, x, 5), C(4,2,-2)$ and $D(6,5,-1)$ are coplanar, then $x$ has the value

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-5$
- **(B)** $5$
- **(C)** $-6$
- **(D)** $6$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $5$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For coplanarity
$\begin{aligned} & {\left[\begin{array}{ccc}6-3 & 5-2 & -1-1 \\ 4-3 & x-2 & 5-1 \\ 4-3 & 2-2 & -2-1\end{array}\right]=0} \\ & \Rightarrow\left[\begin{array}{ccc}3 & 3 & -2 \\ 1 & x-2 & 4 \\ 1 & 0 & -3\end{array}\right]=0 \\ & \Rightarrow 3\{-3(x-2)-0\}-3\{1 \times(-3)-1 \times 4\}-2(1 \times 0-1 \times(x-2))=0 \\ & \Rightarrow-7 x+35=0 \\ & \Rightarrow x=5\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q124. MHT CET 2022 (08 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Let $\vec{u}, \vec{v}$ and $\vec{w}$ be vectors such that $|\vec{u}+\vec{v}+\vec{w}=\overline{0}|$. If $|\vec{u}|=3$, $\overrightarrow{|v|}=4$ and $\overrightarrow{|w|}=5$, then the value of $|\vec{u} \cdot \vec{v}+\vec{v} \cdot \vec{w}+\vec{w} \cdot \vec{u}|$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0
- **(B)** -25
- **(C)** 47
- **(D)** 25

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) -25**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & |\vec{u}+\vec{v}+\vec{w}|^2=|\vec{u}|^2+|\vec{v}|^2+|\vec{w}|^2+2(\vec{u} \cdot \vec{v}+\vec{v} \cdot \vec{w}+\vec{w} \cdot \vec{u}) \\ & \Rightarrow 0^2=3^2+4^2+5^2+2(\vec{u} \cdot \vec{v}+\vec{v} \cdot \vec{w}+\vec{w} \cdot \vec{u}) \\ & \Rightarrow \vec{u} \cdot \vec{v}+\vec{v} \cdot \vec{w}+\vec{w} \cdot \vec{u}=-25\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q125. MHT CET 2022 (08 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $\bar{a}=\hat{i}+\hat{j}+\hat{k}, \bar{b}=\hat{i}-\hat{j}+2 \hat{k}, \bar{c}=x \hat{i}+(x-2) \hat{j}-\hat{k}$ and $\bar{c}$ is linear combination of $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$, then $x$ has the value

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1
- **(B)** -2
- **(C)** 0
- **(D)** -4

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) -2**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \vec{c}=\vec{b}+\lambda \vec{a} \\ & \Rightarrow x \hat{i}+(x-2) \hat{j}-\widehat{k}=(\hat{i}-\hat{j}+2 \widehat{k})+\lambda(\hat{i}+\hat{j}+\widehat{k}) \\ & \Rightarrow x=1+\lambda, x-2=-1+\lambda \text { and }-1=2+\lambda \\ & \Rightarrow \lambda=-3 \text { and } x=-2\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q126. MHT CET 2022 (08 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The position vectors of vertices of $\triangle A B C$ are $4 \hat{i}-2 \hat{j}$; $\hat{i}+4 \hat{j}-3 \hat{k}$ and $-\hat{i}+5 \hat{j}+\hat{k}$ respectively, then $m \angle A B C=$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{\pi}{3}$
- **(B)** $\frac{\pi}{4}$
- **(C)** $\frac{\pi}{6}$
- **(D)** $\frac{\pi}{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\frac{\pi}{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \angle A B C=\text { angle between } \overrightarrow{B A} \text { and } \overrightarrow{B C} \\ & =\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{\overrightarrow{B A} \cdot \overrightarrow{B C}}{|\overrightarrow{B A}||\overrightarrow{B C}|}\right)=\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{(3 \hat{i}-6 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k}) \cdot(-2 \hat{i}+\hat{j}+4 \hat{k})}{\sqrt{3^2+(-6)^2+3^2} \sqrt{(-2)^2+1^2+4^2}}\right) \\ & =\cos ^{-1}\left(\frac{6-6+12}{\sqrt{54} \sqrt{21}}\right)=\cos ^{-1}(0)=\frac{\pi}{2}\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q127. MHT CET 2022 (08 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $\overline{A B}=(2 \hat{i}+3 \hat{j}-\hat{k})$ and $A(1,2,-1)$ is the given point, then the coordinates of $B$ are

**Options:**

- **(A)** (2,4,1)
- **(B)** (3,5,2)
- **(C)** (3,5,-2)
- **(D)** (2,4,-1)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) (3,5,-2)**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $B(x, y, z)$ then $\overrightarrow{A B}=(x-1) \hat{i}+(y-2) \hat{j}+(z+1) \widehat{k}$
$\begin{aligned}
& \Rightarrow x-1=2, y-2=3 \text { and } z+1=-1 \\
& \Rightarrow x=3, y=5 \text { and } z=-2 \\
& \Rightarrow B \equiv(3,5,-2)
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q128. MHT CET 2022 (07 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A vector with magnitude of 3 units, which is perpendicular to each of the vectors $\bar{a}=3 \hat{i}+\hat{j}-4 \hat{k}$ and $\bar{b}=6 \hat{i}+5 \hat{j}-2 \hat{k}$, is given by

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\pm(2 \hat{i}-2 \hat{j}+\hat{k})$
- **(B)** $\pm(2 \hat{i}+2 \hat{j}-\hat{k})$
- **(C)** $\pm(2 \hat{i}-2 \hat{j}-\widehat{k})$
- **(D)** $\pm(2 \hat{i}+2 \hat{j}+\hat{k})$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\pm(2 \hat{i}-2 \hat{j}+\hat{k})$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \text { The required vector }= \pm 3\left(\frac{\vec{a} \times \vec{b}}{|\vec{a} \times \vec{b}|}\right)= \pm \frac{3(18 \hat{i}-18 \hat{j}+9 \hat{k})}{\sqrt{18^2+18^2+9^2}} \\ & = \pm \frac{3(18 \hat{i}-18 \hat{j}+9 \hat{k})}{27}= \pm(2 \hat{i}-2 \hat{j}+\hat{k})\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q129. MHT CET 2022 (07 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Let $\mathrm{a}, \mathrm{b}, \mathrm{c}$ be distinct non-negative umbers. If the vectors $a \hat{i}+a \hat{j}+c \hat{k}, \hat{i}+\hat{k}$ and $c \hat{i}+c \hat{j}+b \hat{k}$ lie in a plane, then c is

**Options:**

- **(A)** the arithmetic mean of a and b.
- **(B)** the harmonic mean of a and $b$.
- **(C)** the geometric mean of $a$ and $b$.
- **(D)** not arithmetic mean of a and b.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) the geometric mean of $a$ and $b$.**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \left|\begin{array}{lll}a & a & c \\ 1 & 0 & 1 \\ c & c & b\end{array}\right|=0 \\ & \Rightarrow a b-c^2=0 \\ & \Rightarrow c^2=a b\end{aligned}$
$\Rightarrow$ The geometric mean of a and b is $\mathrm{c}$

</details>

---

### Q130. MHT CET 2022 (07 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $\vec{a}, \vec{b}, \vec{c}$ are position vectors of points $\mathrm{A}, \mathrm{B}, \mathrm{C}$ respectively, with $2 \vec{a}+3 \vec{b}-5 \vec{c}=\overrightarrow{0}$, then the ratio in which point $\mathrm{C}$ divides segment $\mathrm{AB}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2:3 internally
- **(B)** 2:3 externally
- **(C)** 3:2 internally
- **(D)** 3:2 externally

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 3:2 internally**

**Detailed Solution:**

$2 \vec{a}+3 \vec{b}=5 \vec{c} \Rightarrow \vec{c}=\frac{2 \vec{a}+3 \vec{b}}{5}=\frac{2 \vec{a}+3 \vec{b}}{2+3}$
i.e., $\vec{c}$ divides $\vec{a}$ and $\vec{b}$ in the ratio 3:2 internally

</details>

### Q131. MHT CET 2022 (05 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The sum of the lengths of projections of $\mathrm{p} \hat{i}+\mathrm{q} \hat{j}+\mathrm{rk}$ on the co-ordinate axes, where $p=4, q=-5, r=7$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 6 units
- **(B)** 16 units
- **(C)** 20 units
- **(D)** 28 units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 16 units**

**Detailed Solution:**

The projection on the $\mathrm{x}$-axis $=|\mathrm{p}|=4$
The projection on the $\mathrm{y}$-axis $=|\mathrm{q}|=5$
The projection on the $\mathrm{z}$-axis $=|\mathrm{r}|=7$
Sum $=4+5+7=16$

</details>

### Q132. MHT CET 2022 (05 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $\vec{a}, \vec{b}, \vec{c}$ be vectors of lengths $3,4,5$ respectively. Let $\vec{a}$ be perpendicular to $\vec{b}+\vec{c}, \vec{b}$ be perpendicular to $\vec{c}+\vec{a}$ and $\vec{c}$ be perpendicular to $\vec{a}+\vec{b}$, then the length of vector $\vec{a}+\vec{b}+\vec{c}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $5$
- **(B)** $5 \sqrt{3}$
- **(C)** $5 \sqrt{2}$
- **(D)** $5 \sqrt{6}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $5 \sqrt{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/mht_cet/vFG-V9ERq_k2K1jWxJee1648Yp0EPgv3k9nYyuIo3Kc.original.fullsize.png">
$\begin{aligned} & \text { Now }|\vec{a}+\vec{b}+\vec{c}|=\sqrt{|\vec{a}|^2+|\vec{b}|^2+|\vec{c}|^2+2 \vec{a} \cdot \vec{b}+2 \vec{b} \cdot \vec{c}+2 \vec{c} \cdot \vec{a}} \\ & =\sqrt{3^2+4^2+5^2+0} \quad[\because|\vec{a}|=3,|\vec{b}|=4,|\vec{c}|=5 \text { given and from(iv) } \\ & =\sqrt{50} \\ & =5 \sqrt{2}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q133. MHT CET 2022 (05 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The incenter and centroid of the triangle, whose vertices are $A \equiv(0,3,0), B \equiv(0,0,4)$, and $C \equiv(0,3,4)$, are respectively given by

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(0,-2,-3),\left(0,-2, \frac{8}{3}\right)$
- **(B)** $(0,-2,3),\left(0,2,-\frac{8}{3}\right)$
- **(C)** $\left(0,2, \frac{8}{3}\right),(0,2,3)$
- **(D)** $(0,2,3),\left(0,2, \frac{8}{3}\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $(0,2,3),\left(0,2, \frac{8}{3}\right)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$A \equiv(0,3,0), B \equiv(0,0,4)$, and $C \equiv(0,3,4)$
$a=B C=3$
$b=C A=4$
$c=A B=5$.
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/mht_cet/WAbFy0YFEctqpcfBjp6u2vISk11rStODaWPo3ScFbLE.original.fullsize.png">
$\begin{aligned} & \text { Now, Incentre } \equiv\left(\frac{a x_1+b x_2+c x_3}{a+b+c}, \frac{a y_1+b y_2+c y_3}{a+b+c}, \frac{a z_1+b z_2+c z_3}{a+b+c}\right) \\ & \equiv\left(\frac{3 \times 0+4 \times 0+5 \times 0}{3+4+5}, \frac{3 \times 3+4 \times 0+5 \times 3}{3+4+5}, \frac{3 \times 0+4 \times 4+5 \times 4}{3+4+5}\right) \\ & \equiv(0,2,3) \\ & \text { and centroid } \equiv\left(\frac{x_1+x_2+x_3}{3}, \frac{y_1+y_2+y_3}{3}, \frac{z_1+z_2+z_3}{3}\right)\end{aligned}$
$\begin{aligned} & \equiv\left(\frac{0+0+0}{3}, \frac{3+0+3}{3}, \frac{0+4+4}{3}\right) \\ & \equiv\left(0,2, \frac{8}{3}\right)\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q134. MHT CET 2021 (23 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

If $3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}, 4 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$ and $3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+4 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$ are the position vectors of the vertices $\mathrm{A}, \mathrm{B}$, $C$ respectively of $\triangle \mathrm{ABC}$, then the position vector of the point in which the bisector of $\angle \mathrm{A}$ meets $\mathrm{BC}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{5}{3} \hat{\mathrm{j}}-4 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
- **(B)** $5 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-4 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
- **(C)** $5 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+4 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
- **(D)** $\frac{5}{3} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+4 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\frac{5}{3} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+4 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/mht_cet/fBGY4is6lS_QOpCPNI1OmAvJ0MTpcNeOzrswaFECHxk.original.fullsize.png">

We have $\overline{\mathrm{a}}=3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}, \mathrm{b}=4 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{c}}=3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+4 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
Angle bisector $\mathrm{AD}$ divides $\mathrm{BC}$ in the ratio $\mathrm{AB}: \mathrm{AC}$
$$
\mathrm{AB}=\sqrt{9+16}=5 \text { and } \mathrm{AC}=\sqrt{0+16}=4
$$
Thus $\mathrm{D}$ divides $\mathrm{BC}$ in the ratio $5: 4$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \therefore \mathrm{D}=\left[0, \frac{5(3)}{5+4}, \frac{5(4)+4(4)}{5+4}\right] \\
& =\left(0, \frac{15}{9}, \frac{20+16}{9}\right)=\left(0, \frac{5}{3}, 4\right)=\frac{5}{3} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+4 \hat{\mathrm{k}}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q135. MHT CET 2021 (23 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the population grows at the rate of $8 \%$ per year, then the time taken for the population to be doubled is (Given $\log 2=0.6912$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** 6.8 year
- **(B)** 4.3 years
- **(C)** 10.27 years
- **(D)** 8.64 years

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 8.64 years**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the initial population be $\mathrm{P}$ and rate of increase is $8 \%$ per year.
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \therefore \frac{\mathrm{dP}}{\mathrm{dt}}=\frac{8}{100} \mathrm{P} \\
& \therefore \int \frac{\mathrm{dP}}{\mathrm{P}}=\int 0.08 \mathrm{t} \\
& \therefore \log |\mathrm{P}|=0.08 \mathrm{t}+\mathrm{c}
\end{aligned}
$$
When $\mathrm{t}=0$, we get $\mathrm{c}=\log \mathrm{P}$
$$
\therefore \log \mathrm{P}=0.08 \mathrm{t}+\log \mathrm{P}
$$
When ' $\mathrm{P}$ ' doubles, we write
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \log 2 p=0.08 \mathrm{t}+\log \mathrm{P} \\
& \therefore \log \left(\frac{2 \mathrm{P}}{\mathrm{P}}\right)=\log 2=0.6921=0.08 \mathrm{t} \\
& \therefore \mathrm{t}=\frac{0.6912}{0.08}=8.64 \text { years }
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q136. MHT CET 2021 (23 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the vectors $\vec{a}=2 \hat{i}+p \hat{j}+4 \hat{k}$ and $\vec{b}=6 \hat{i}-9 \hat{j}+q \hat{k}$ are collinear, then $\mathrm{p}$ and $\mathrm{q}$ are

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{p}=3, \mathrm{q}=-2$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{p}=3, \mathrm{q}=\mathrm{n} 12$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{p}=-3, \mathrm{q}=12$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{p}=-3, \mathrm{q}=-12$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\mathrm{p}=-3, \mathrm{q}=12$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\vec{a}=x \vec{b}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \therefore 2 \hat{i}+p \hat{j}+4 \hat{k}=6 x \hat{i}-9 x \hat{j}+q x \hat{j} \\
& \therefore 2=6 x \Rightarrow x=\frac{1}{3} \\
& p=-9 x \Rightarrow(-9)\left(\frac{1}{3}\right)=-3 \text { and } 4=q x=q\left(\frac{1}{3}\right) \Rightarrow q=4(3)=12
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q137. MHT CET 2021 (23 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

$\vec{a}=4 \hat{i}+13 \hat{j}-18 \hat{k}, \vec{b}=\hat{i}-2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k}$ and $\vec{c}=2 \hat{i}+3 \hat{j}-4 \hat{k}$ are three vectors such that $\vec{a}=x \vec{b}+y \vec{c}$, then $x+y=$

**Options:**

- **(A)** -1
- **(B)** -2
- **(C)** 5
- **(D)** 1

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 1**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
& \vec{a}=x \vec{b}+y \vec{c} \\
& \therefore 4 \hat{i}+13 \hat{j}-18 \hat{k}=(\hat{i}-2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})(x)+(2 \hat{i}+3 \hat{j}-4 \hat{k})(y) \\
& =(x+2 y) \hat{i}+(-2 x+3 y) \hat{j}+(3 x-4 y) \hat{k} \\
& \therefore x+2 y=4,-2 x+3 y=13 \text { and } 3 x-4 y=-18
\end{aligned}
$$
Solving, we get $x=-2, y=3 \Rightarrow x+y=1$

</details>

---

### Q138. MHT CET 2021 (22 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the points $\mathrm{P}(4,5, \mathrm{x}), \mathrm{Q}(3, \mathrm{y}, 4)$ and $\mathrm{R}(5,8,0)$ are collinear, then the value of $x+y$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 6
- **(B)** 7
- **(C)** 4
- **(D)** 5

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 4**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
& \overline{\mathrm{PQ}}=-\hat{\mathrm{i}}+(\mathrm{y}-5) \hat{\mathrm{j}}+(4-\mathrm{x}) \hat{\mathrm{k}} \\
& \overline{\mathrm{PR}}=\hat{\mathrm{i}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}=\mathrm{x} \hat{\mathrm{k}}
\end{aligned}
$$
Since points $\mathrm{P}$. Q, R are collinear
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \overline{\mathrm{PQ}}=\mathrm{a} \overline{\mathrm{PR}} \\
& \therefore-\hat{\mathrm{i}}+(\mathrm{y}-5) \hat{\mathrm{j}}+(4-\mathrm{x}) \hat{\mathrm{k}}=\mathrm{a}(\hat{\mathrm{i}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}=\mathrm{x} \hat{\mathrm{k}}) \\
& \therefore \mathrm{a}=-1,3 \mathrm{a}=\mathrm{y}-5,-\mathrm{ax}=4-\mathrm{x} \\
& \therefore \mathrm{a}=-1 \Rightarrow-3=\mathrm{y}-5 \quad \text { i.e. } \mathrm{y}=2 \text { and } \mathrm{x}=4-\mathrm{x} \Rightarrow \mathrm{x}=2 \Rightarrow \\
& \mathrm{x}+\mathrm{y}=4
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q139. MHT CET 2021 (22 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the vectors $2 \hat{i}-\hat{j}-\hat{k} ; \hat{i}+2 \hat{j}-3 \hat{k}$ and $3 \hat{i}+\lambda \hat{j}+5 \hat{k}$ are coplanar, then the value of $\lambda$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** -8
- **(B)** -4
- **(C)** -2
- **(D)** -1

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) -8**

**Detailed Solution:**

For given coplanar vectors, we write
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \left|\begin{array}{ccc}
2 & -1 & -1 \\
1 & 2 & -3 \\
3 & \lambda & 5
\end{array}\right|=\Rightarrow 2(10+3 \lambda)+(14)-(\lambda-6)=0 \\
& \Rightarrow 5 \lambda+40=0 \Rightarrow \lambda=-8
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q140. MHT CET 2021 (22 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The vector equation of the line whose Cartesian equations are $y=2$ and $4 x-3 z+5=0$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=(2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+5 \hat{\mathrm{k}})+\lambda(4 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-3 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$
- **(B)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\left(2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-\frac{5}{3} \hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)+\lambda(3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+4 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$
- **(C)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\left(2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-\frac{5}{3} \hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)+\lambda(3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-4 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$
- **(D)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\left(2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\frac{5}{3} \hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)+\lambda(3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+4 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\left(2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\frac{5}{3} \hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)+\lambda(3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+4 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$**

**Detailed Solution:**

We have $4 x-3 z+5=0$ and $y=2$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \therefore 4 x=3 z-5 \Rightarrow 4 x=3\left(z-\frac{5}{3}\right) \\
& \therefore \frac{4 x}{12}=\frac{3\left(z-\frac{5}{3}\right)}{12} \Rightarrow \frac{x}{3}=\frac{3\left(z-\frac{5}{3}\right)}{4}
\end{aligned}
$$
Thus line passes through point $\left(0,2, \frac{5}{3}\right)$, and has direction ratios $3,0,4$.
Hence required equation of line is $\left(2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\frac{5}{3} \hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)+\lambda(3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+4 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$

</details>

---

### Q141. MHT CET 2021 (22 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The position vector of the point of intersection of the medians of a triangle, whose vertices are $\mathrm{A}(1,2,3), \mathrm{B}(1,0,3)$ and $\mathrm{C}(4,1,-3)$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $6 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
- **(B)** $2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}$
- **(C)** $\hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}$
- **(D)** $\hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/mht_cet/w1G3cub6Q9qn3ig2tbtNlYp8UFXF0QbrF7DlISX2Pl0.original.fullsize.png">

Let $\mathrm{M}$ be the mid point of $\mathrm{BC}$ and $\mathrm{N}$ be the mid point of $\mathrm{AC}$.
$$
\therefore \mathrm{M}=\left(\frac{5}{2}, \frac{1}{2}, 0\right)
$$
We know that centroid G divides AM internally in the ratio $2: 1$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \therefore G=\frac{(1)(1)+(2)\left(\frac{5}{2}\right)}{2+1}, \frac{(1)(2)+(2)\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)}{2+1}, \frac{(1)(3)+0}{2+1} \\
& G=(2,1,1)
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q142. MHT CET 2021 (22 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The Cartesian equation of a line is $3 x+1=6 y-2=-z+1$, then its vector equation is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\left(\frac{-1}{3} \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\frac{1}{3} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)+\lambda(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\hat{\mathrm{j}}-6 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$
- **(B)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=(-\hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-\hat{\mathrm{k}})+\lambda(3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+6 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-\hat{\mathrm{k}})$
- **(C)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\left(\frac{-1}{3} \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\frac{1}{3} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)+\lambda(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\hat{\mathrm{j}}+6 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$
- **(D)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\left(\frac{-1}{3} \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\frac{1}{3} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)+\lambda(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+6 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\left(\frac{-1}{3} \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\frac{1}{3} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}\right)+\lambda(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+6 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & 3 x+1=6 y-2=-z+1 \\ & \therefore 3\left(x+\frac{1}{3}\right)=6\left(y-\frac{2}{6}\right)=-(z-1) \\ & \therefore \frac{\left(x+\frac{1}{3}\right)}{\left(\frac{1}{3}\right)}=\frac{\left(y-\frac{1}{3}\right)}{\left(\frac{1}{6}\right)}=\frac{(z-1)}{(-1)} \Rightarrow \text { d.r.s. are } 2,1,-6 \\ & \text { Thus } \bar{r}=\left(-\frac{1}{3} \hat{i}+\frac{1}{3} \hat{j}+\hat{k}\right)+\lambda(2 \hat{i}+\hat{j}-6 \hat{k})\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q143. MHT CET 2021 (21 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

In a quadrilateral $\mathrm{PQRS}, \mathrm{M}$ and $\mathrm{N}$ are mid-points of the sides $P Q$ and RS respectively. If $\overline{\mathrm{PS}}+\overline{\mathrm{QR}}=\mathrm{t} \overline{\mathrm{MN}}$, then $\mathrm{t}=$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{1}{2}$
- **(B)** 4
- **(C)** $\frac{3}{2}$
- **(D)** 2

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/mht_cet/bHEPBMj7s1buFLEVwhtEvduQjgqaYuwqqeU7QXEtfxs.original.fullsize.png">



$\mathrm{p}, \mathrm{q}, \mathrm{r}, \mathrm{s}, \mathrm{m}, \mathrm{n}$ be the position vectors of points $\mathrm{P}, \mathrm{Q}, \mathrm{R}, \mathrm{S}, \mathrm{M}, \mathrm{N}$.
Now $\overline{\mathrm{PS}}+\overline{\mathrm{QR}}$
$$
=\overline{\mathrm{s}}-\overline{\mathrm{p}}+\overline{\mathrm{r}}-\overline{\mathrm{q}}=(\overline{\mathrm{s}}+\overline{\mathrm{r}})-(\overline{\mathrm{p}}+\overline{\mathrm{q}})
$$
Since $\mathrm{M}$ and $\mathrm{N}$ are mid-points of PQ and RS respectively, we write
$\overline{\mathrm{m}}=\frac{\overline{\mathrm{p}}+\overline{\mathrm{q}}}{2}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{n}}=\frac{\overline{\mathrm{r}}+\overline{\mathrm{s}}}{2}$
$\therefore$ eq. (1) becomes
$$
\overline{\mathrm{PS}}+\overline{\mathrm{QR}}=2 \overline{\mathrm{n}}-2 \overline{\mathrm{m}}=2(\overline{\mathrm{n}}-\overline{\mathrm{m}})=2 \overline{\mathrm{MN}}
$$
From given data, $\mathrm{t}=2$

</details>

### Q144. MHT CET 2021 (21 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The vertices of triangle $\mathrm{ABC}$ are $\mathrm{A} \equiv(3,0,0) ; \mathrm{B} \equiv(0,0,4)$; $\mathrm{C} \equiv(0,5,4)$. Find the position vector of the point in which the bisector of angle A meets $\mathrm{BC}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $5 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+12 \hat{\mathrm{j}}$
- **(B)** $\frac{5 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+12 \hat{\mathrm{k}}}{3}$
- **(C)** $\frac{5 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+12 \hat{\mathrm{j}}}{13}$
- **(D)** $\frac{5 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-12 \hat{\mathrm{j}}}{3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{5 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+12 \hat{\mathrm{k}}}{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/mht_cet/HDc9HAwxwrMTbgGdfM19wD7ab6pwBuuucmaoblYvPJk.original.fullsize.png">

Let $\mathrm{AD}$ be the angle bisector of angle $\mathrm{A}$ which divides $\mathrm{BC}$ in the ratio
$\mathrm{AB}: \mathrm{AC}$
Here $\mathrm{AB}=\sqrt{9+16}=\sqrt{25}$ and
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{AC}=\sqrt{9+25+16} \\
& =\sqrt{50}
\end{aligned}
$$
$\therefore \mathrm{D}$ divides $\mathrm{BC}$ in the ratio $\sqrt{25}: \sqrt{50}$ i.e., $1: 2$
$\therefore$ Position vector of $\mathrm{D}=\frac{(4)(2) \hat{\mathrm{k}}+5 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+4 \hat{\mathrm{k}}}{1+2}=\frac{5 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+12 \hat{\mathrm{k}}}{3}$

</details>

### Q145. MHT CET 2021 (20 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

$\overline{\mathrm{r}}=-4 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-6 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-2 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$ is a linear combination of the vector $\bar{a}=-\hat{i}-4 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k}$ and $\bar{b}=-8 \hat{i}-\hat{j}+3 \hat{k}$, then

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\frac{-4}{3} \overline{\mathrm{a}}+\frac{2}{3} \overline{\mathrm{b}}$
- **(B)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\frac{4}{3} \overline{\mathrm{a}}+\frac{2}{3} \overline{\mathrm{b}}$
- **(C)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\frac{-1}{3} \overline{\mathrm{a}}+\frac{2}{3} \overline{\mathrm{b}}$
- **(D)** $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\frac{1}{3} \overline{\mathrm{a}}-\frac{1}{3} \overline{\mathrm{b}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\overline{\mathrm{r}}=\frac{-4}{3} \overline{\mathrm{a}}+\frac{2}{3} \overline{\mathrm{b}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $r=x \bar{a}+y \bar{b}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \therefore-4 \hat{i}-6 \hat{j}-2 \hat{k}=x(-\hat{i}+4 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})+y(-8 \hat{i}-\hat{j}+3 \hat{k}) \\
& =(-x-8 y) \hat{i}+(4 x-y) \hat{j}+(3 x+3 y) \hat{k} \\
& \therefore \quad-x-8 y \quad \ldots(1) \\
& 4 x-y \quad=-4 \quad \ldots(2) \\
& 3 x+3 y \quad=-2 \quad \ldots(3)
\end{aligned}
$$
Solving (1) and (2), we get $\mathrm{y}=\frac{2}{3}$ and $\mathrm{x}=\frac{-4}{3}$

</details>

### Q146. MHT CET 2021 (20 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

$\overline{\mathrm{a}}, \overline{\mathrm{b}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{c}}$ are three vectors such that $\overline{\mathrm{a}}+\overline{\mathrm{b}}+\overline{\mathrm{c}}=\overline{0}$ and $|\overline{\mathrm{a}}|=3,|\overline{\mathrm{b}}|=5,|\overline{\mathrm{c}}|=7$, then the angle between $\overline{\mathrm{a}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{b}}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{\pi}{4}$
- **(B)** $\frac{\pi}{2}$
- **(C)** $\frac{\pi}{3}$
- **(D)** $\frac{\pi}{6}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\frac{\pi}{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\overline{\mathrm{a}}+\overline{\mathrm{b}}+\overline{\mathrm{c}}=0 \Rightarrow \overline{\mathrm{c}}=-(\overline{\mathrm{a}}+\overline{\mathrm{b}})$ and let angle between $\overline{\mathrm{a}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{b}}$ be $\theta$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \therefore|\overline{\mathrm{c}}|^2=(\overline{\mathrm{a}}+\overline{\mathrm{b}})^2=|\overline{\mathrm{a}}|^2+|\overline{\mathrm{b}}|^2+2 \overline{\mathrm{a}} \cdot \overline{\mathrm{b}} \\
& =|\overline{\mathrm{a}}|^2+|\overline{\mathrm{b}}|^2+2|\overline{\mathrm{a}}| \cdot|\overline{\mathrm{b}}| \cdot \cos \theta \\
& \therefore(7)^2=(3)^2+(5)^2+2(3)(5) \cos \theta \\
& \therefore 49=9+25+30 \cos \theta \Rightarrow \cos \theta=\frac{15}{30}=\frac{1}{2} \Rightarrow \theta=\frac{\pi}{3}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q147. MHT CET 2020 (20 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $\bar{a}, \bar{b}, \bar{c}$ are the position vectors of the points $\mathrm{A}(1,3,0), \mathrm{B}(2,5,0), \mathrm{C}(4,2,0)$
respectively and $\bar{c}=t_{1} \bar{a}+t_{2} \bar{b}$, then value of $t_{1} t_{2}=$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-16$
- **(B)** 16
- **(C)** 160
- **(D)** $-160$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $-160$**

**Detailed Solution:**

From given conditions, wo war $\begin{aligned} 4 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}} &=\mathrm{t}_{1}(\hat{\mathrm{i}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{j}})+\mathrm{t}_{2}(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+5 \hat{\mathrm{j}}) \\ \therefore \quad &=\left(\mathrm{t}_{1}+2 \mathrm{t}_{2}\right) \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\left(3 \mathrm{t}_{1}+5 \mathrm{t}_{2}\right) \hat{\mathrm{j}} \end{aligned}$ $\therefore \mathrm{t}_{1}+2 \mathrm{t}_{2}=4 \quad$ and $\quad 3 \mathrm{t}_{1}+5 \mathrm{t}_{2}=2$
Solving, we get $t_{2}=10$ and $t_{1}=-16$ $\therefore \mathrm{t}_{1} \mathrm{t}_{2}=-160$

</details>
