### Q148. MHT CET 2020 (20 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $\bar{a}=\hat{\imath}+\hat{j}+\hat{k}, \bar{b}=2 \hat{\imath}-2 \hat{\jmath}+2 \hat{k}, \bar{c}=2 \hat{\imath}+3 \hat{\jmath}+2 \hat{k}$ are any three co-planar vectors such that $l \bar{a}+m \bar{b}+n \bar{c}=\overline{0}$, then values of $l, m, n$ are respectively

**Options:**

- **(A)** 10, 1, 4
- **(B)** $10,-4,1$
- **(C)** 10, $-1,-4$
- **(D)** $10,1,-4$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 10, $-1,-4$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{array}{l}
\vec{a}=\hat{\imath}+\hat{\jmath}+\hat{k} \\
\vec{b}=2 \hat{\imath}-2 \hat{\jmath}+2 \hat{k} \\
\vec{c}=2 \hat{\imath}+3 \hat{\jmath}+2 \hat{k} \\
x \vec{a}+y \vec{b}=\vec{c}
\end{array}
$$
By solving all eq"s we get $x=+\frac{5}{2} ; y=-\frac{1}{4}$
$$
10 \vec{a}-\vec{b}-4 \vec{c}=0
$$

</details>

### Q149. MHT CET 2020 (20 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $\overline{\mathrm{a}}=2 \hat{\imath}+3 \hat{\jmath}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}, \overline{\mathrm{b}}=4 \hat{\imath}+5 \hat{\jmath}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{c}}=6 \hat{\imath}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+5 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$ are the position vectors of the
vertices of a triangle ABC respectively, then the position vector of the intersection of the medians of the triangle $\mathrm{ABC}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4 \hat{\imath}+3 \hat{\jmath}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
- **(B)** $2 \hat{\imath}+3 \hat{\jmath}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
- **(C)** $5 \hat{\imath}+3 \hat{\jmath}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
- **(D)** $3 \hat{\imath}+3 \hat{\jmath}+4 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $4 \hat{\imath}+3 \hat{\jmath}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Centroid $(\overline{\mathrm{g}})=\frac{\overline{\mathrm{a}}+\overline{\mathrm{b}}+\overline{\mathrm{c}}}{3}=\frac{12 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+9 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+9 \hat{\mathrm{k}}}{3}=4 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$

</details>

### Q150. MHT CET 2020 (20 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the position vectors of the vertices $A, B, C$ of a tringle $A B C$ are $4 \hat{\imath}+7 j+8 \hat{k}, 2 \hat{\imath}+3 \hat{\jmath}+4 \hat{k}$ and $2 \hat{\imath}+5 \hat{\jmath}+7 \hat{k}$ respectively, then the position vector of the point where bisector of angle A meets $B C$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{1}{3}(6 \hat{\imath}+11 \hat{\jmath}+15 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$
- **(B)** $\frac{1}{2}(4 \hat{\imath}+8 \hat{\jmath}+11 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$
- **(C)** $\frac{1}{4}(8 \hat{\imath}+14 \hat{\jmath}+19 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$
- **(D)** $\frac{1}{3}(6 \hat{\imath}+13 \hat{\mathbf{j}}+18 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\frac{1}{3}(6 \hat{\imath}+13 \hat{\mathbf{j}}+18 \hat{\mathrm{k}})$**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/mht_cet/AyUAqUJGIp8BVs11Z6MNESQOOJXzeRSyMIkcT0nhcoY.original.fullsize.png">

Suppose the bisector of angle $\mathrm{A}$ meets $\mathrm{BC}$ at $\mathrm{D}$.
Then $A D$ divides $B C$ in the ratio $A B$ : $A C$
$\therefore$ Position vector of $\mathrm{D}=\frac{(\mathrm{AC})(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+4 \hat{\mathrm{k}})+(\mathrm{AB})(2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+5 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+7 \hat{\mathrm{k}})}{(\mathrm{AB})+(\mathrm{AC})}$
$\overline{\mathrm{AB}}=-2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-4 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-4 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{AC}}=-2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-\hat{\mathrm{k}}$
$|\overline{\mathrm{AB}}|=\mathrm{AB}=6$ and $|\overline{\mathrm{AC}}|=\mathrm{AC}=\sqrt{4+4+1}=3$
Position vector of $D$
$=\frac{6(2 \hat{i}+3 \hat{j}+4 \hat{k})+3(2 \hat{i}+5 \hat{j}+7 \hat{k})}{6+3}=\frac{18 \hat{i}+33 \hat{j}+45 \hat{k}}{9}=\frac{1}{3}(6 \hat{i}+11 \hat{j}+15 \hat{k})$

</details>

### Q151. MHT CET 2020 (19 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

$\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$ are non-collinear vectors. If $\bar{p}=(2 x+1) \bar{a}-\bar{b}$ and $\bar{q}=(x-2) \bar{a}+\bar{b}$
are collinear vectors, then $x=$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-3$
- **(B)** $\frac{1}{3}$
- **(C)** $-\frac{1}{3}$
- **(D)** 3

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{1}{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

(A)
The vector $\bar{q}$ is non-zero since the coefficient in $\bar{b}$ is different from zero and so the vectors $\bar{q}$ and $\bar{p}$ are collinear if for some number $y$ we have
$\overline{\mathrm{p}}=\mathrm{c} \overline{\mathrm{q}}$ that is
$(2 x+1) \bar{a}-\bar{b}=y[(x-2) \bar{a}+\bar{b}]$
$\therefore(2 x+1)=y(x-2)$ and $-1=y \Rightarrow y=-1$
$2 x+1=-(x-2) \Rightarrow 3 x=1 \Rightarrow x=\frac{1}{3}$

</details>

---

### Q152. MHT CET 2020 (19 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The point $\mathrm{P}$ lies on the line $\mathrm{A} \mathrm{B}$, where $\mathrm{A} \equiv(2,4,5)$ and $\mathrm{B} \equiv(1,2,3)$. If $\mathrm{z}$ co-ordinate
of point $\mathrm{P}$ is 3, the its y co-ordinate is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2
- **(B)** $-2$
- **(C)** $-3$
- **(D)** 3

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 2**

**Detailed Solution:**

(C)
Equation of line passing through $\mathrm{A}$ and $\mathrm{B}$ is
$\frac{x-2}{1-2}=\frac{y-4}{2-4}=\frac{z-5}{3-5}=k \quad \text {...(say) } \Rightarrow \frac{x-2}{-1}=\frac{y-4}{-2}=\frac{z-5}{-2}=k$
Hence coordinates of any point onthis line are
$(-k+2,-2 k+4,-2 k+5)$
As per condition given, we have
$-2 k+5=3 \Rightarrow k=1$
Hence $y$ coordinate $=-2+4=2$

</details>

---

### Q153. MHT CET 2020 (19 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $\bar{a}=3 \hat{\imath}+\hat{\jmath}-\hat{k}, \bar{b}=2 \hat{\imath}-\hat{\jmath}+7 \hat{k}$ and $\bar{c}=7 \hat{\imath}-\hat{\jmath}+23 \hat{k}$ are three vectors,
then which of the following statement is true.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\bar{a}, \bar{b}$ and $\bar{c}$ are non-coplanar.
- **(B)** $\bar{a}, \bar{b}$ and $\bar{c}$ are coplanar.
- **(C)** $\bar{a}, \bar{b}, \bar{c}$ are mutually perpendicular.
- **(D)** $\bar{a}$ and $\bar{b}$ are collinear.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\bar{a}, \bar{b}$ and $\bar{c}$ are non-coplanar.**

**Detailed Solution:**

(C)
$\bar{a}=3 \hat{i}+\hat{j}-\hat{k}, \bar{b}=2 \hat{i}-\hat{j}+7 \hat{k}, \bar{c}=7 \hat{i}-\hat{j}+23 \hat{k}$
$\begin{aligned}\left[\begin{array}{ccc}\bar{a} & \bar{b} & \bar{c}\end{array}\right] &=\left|\begin{array}{ccc}3 & 1 & -1 \\ 2 & -1 & 7 \\ 7 & -1 & 23\end{array}\right| \\ &=3(-23+7)-1(46-49)-1(-2+7) \\ &=3(-16)-(-3)-(5)=-50 \neq 0 \end{aligned}$
$\therefore \overline{\mathrm{a}}, \overline{\mathrm{b}}, \overline{\mathrm{c}}$ are non coplanar.

</details>

### Q154. MHT CET 2020 (19 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the points $\mathrm{A}(2,1,-1), \mathrm{B}(0,-1,0), \mathrm{C}(4,0,4)$ and $\mathrm{D}(2,0, x)$ are coplanar, then $x=$

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2
- **(B)** 1
- **(C)** 4
- **(D)** 3

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 1**

**Detailed Solution:**

(B)
$\begin{aligned}
\overline{\mathrm{AB}} &=-2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}} \\
\overline{\mathrm{AC}} &=2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\hat{\mathrm{j}}+5 \hat{\mathrm{k}} \\
\overline{\mathrm{AD}} &=-\hat{\mathrm{j}}+(\mathrm{x}+1) \hat{\mathrm{k}}
\end{aligned}$
Since the points A, B, C, D are coplanar, we write
$\begin{aligned}
&\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
-2 & -2 & 1 \\
2 & -1 & 5 \\
0 & -1 & x+1
\end{array}\right|=0 \\
\therefore &-2[(-1-x)-(-5)]+2[(2 x+2)-0]+1[-2-0]=0 \\
&-2[-1-x+5]+2[2 x+2]+[-2]=0 \Rightarrow-8+2 x+4 x+2=0 \quad \Rightarrow x=1
\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q155. MHT CET 2020 (19 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $a, b, c$ are distinct positive numbers and vectors $a \hat{\imath}+a \hat{\jmath}+c \hat{k}, \hat{\imath}+\hat{k}$
and $c \hat{\imath}+c \hat{\jmath}+b \hat{k}$ lie in a plane, then

**Options:**

- **(A)** $c$ is A.M. of a and b
- **(B)** $\mathrm{c}^{2}=0$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{c}$ is $\mathrm{H} . \mathrm{M}$. of $\mathrm{a}$ and $\mathrm{b}$
- **(D)** $c$ is G.M. of a and b

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $c$ is G.M. of a and b**

**Detailed Solution:**

(B)
since, three vectors are coplanar
$\left|\begin{array}{lll}
\mathrm{a} & \mathrm{a} & \mathrm{c} \\
1 & 0 & 1 \\
\mathrm{c} & \mathrm{c} & \mathrm{b}
\end{array}\right|=0$
Applying $\mathrm{C}_{1} \rightarrow \mathrm{C}_{1}-\mathrm{C}_{2}$
$\left|\begin{array}{lll}
0 & a & c \\
1 & 0 & 1 \\
0 & c & b
\end{array}\right|=0$
Expanding along $C_{1}$, we get
$-1\left(a b-c^{2}\right)=0 \Rightarrow a b=c^{2} \Rightarrow c$ is G.M. of a and b

</details>

### Q156. MHT CET 2020 (16 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $\mathrm{G}$ be the centroid of a triangle $\mathrm{ABC}$ and $0 \mathrm{be}$ any other point in that plane, then $\overline{\mathrm{OA}}+\overline{\mathrm{OB}}+\overline{\mathrm{OC}}+\overline{\mathrm{OG}}=$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4 \overline{\mathrm{OG}}$
- **(B)** $\overline{\mathrm{O}}$
- **(C)** $3 \overline{\mathrm{OG}}$
- **(D)** $2 \overline{\mathrm{OG}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $4 \overline{\mathrm{OG}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

(B)
Let $\mathrm{O}$ be the origin.
Let $\bar{a}, \bar{b}, \bar{c}$ be the position vectors of vertices $A, B, C$ respectively.
$\therefore \overline{\mathrm{OA}}+\overline{\mathrm{OB}}+\overline{\mathrm{OC}}=\overline{\mathrm{a}}+\overline{\mathrm{b}}+\overline{\mathrm{c}}$
Given : $G$ is the centroid of triangle
$\begin{array}{l}
\therefore \overline{\mathrm{OG}}=\frac{\overline{\mathrm{a}}+\overline{\mathrm{b}}+\overline{\mathrm{c}}}{3} \\
\therefore \overline{\mathrm{a}}+\overline{\mathrm{b}}+\overline{\mathrm{c}}=3 \overline{\mathrm{OG}} \\
\therefore \overline{\mathrm{OA}}+\overline{\mathrm{OB}}+\overline{\mathrm{OC}}+\overline{\mathrm{OG}}=3 \overline{\mathrm{OG}}+\overline{\mathrm{OG}}=4 \overline{\mathrm{OG}}
\end{array}$

</details>

### Q157. MHT CET 2020 (16 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

In a quadrilateral $A B C D, M$ and $N$ are the mid-points of the sides $A B$ and $C D$ respectively. If $\overline{A D}+\overline{B C}=t \overline{M N}$, then $t=$

**Options:**

- **(A)** 4
- **(B)** 2
- **(C)** $\frac{1}{2}$
- **(D)** $\frac{3}{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 2**

**Detailed Solution:**

$(\mathrm{C})$
Let $\bar{a}, \bar{b}, \bar{c}, \bar{d}, \bar{m}, \bar{n}$ be the position vectors of $A, B, C$, $\mathrm{D}, \mathrm{M}, \mathrm{N}$ respectively. $\mathrm{M}$ and $\mathrm{N}$ are the midpoints of $\mathrm{AB}$ and CD respectively.
$\overline{\mathrm{m}}=\frac{\overline{\mathrm{a}}+\overline{\mathrm{b}}}{2}$, and $\overline{\mathrm{n}}=\frac{\overline{\mathrm{c}}+\overline{\mathrm{d}}}{2} \Rightarrow \overline{\mathrm{a}}+\overline{\mathrm{b}}=2 \overline{\mathrm{m}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{c}}+\overline{\mathrm{d}}=2 \overline{\mathrm{n}}$
We have $\overline{\mathrm{AD}}+\overline{\mathrm{BC}}=\mathrm{t}(\overline{\mathrm{MN}})$
$(\bar{d}-\bar{a})+(\bar{c}-\bar{b})=t(\bar{n}-\bar{m})$
$\overline{\mathrm{d}}-\overline{\mathrm{a}}+\overline{\mathrm{c}}-\overline{\mathrm{b}}=\mathrm{t}(\overline{\mathrm{n}}-\mathrm{m})$
$(\overline{\mathrm{d}}+\overline{\mathrm{c}})-(\overline{\mathrm{a}}+\overline{\mathrm{b}})=\mathrm{t}(\overline{\mathrm{n}}-\overline{\mathrm{m}})$
$2 \bar{n}-2 \bar{m}=t(\bar{n}-\bar{m}) \Rightarrow t=2$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/mht_cet/8kDNyrOjQXrQ6ySgvVS3I1D4mITClRPxhwbDJzkPI4o.original.fullsize.png">

</details>

### Q158. MHT CET 2020 (15 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If a point $\mathrm{P}$ on the line segment joining the points $(3,5,-1)$ and $(6,3,-2)$ has
its y - coordinate 2, then its z - coordinate is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{2}{15}$
- **(B)** $\frac{17}{3}$
- **(C)** $\frac{15}{2}$
- **(D)** $\frac{3}{17}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\frac{15}{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Equation of line passing through $(3,5,-1)$ and $(6,3,-2)$ is
$$
\frac{x-3}{3}=\frac{y-5}{-2}=\frac{z+1}{-1}=r(\text { say })
$$
Hence coordinates of any point lying on the line are $(3 r+3,-2 r+5,-r-1)$
We have $-2 r+5=2 \quad \Rightarrow \quad r=\frac{3}{2}$
$\therefore \quad z$ coordinate is $\quad-r-1=\frac{-3}{2}-1=\frac{-5}{2}$

</details>

---

### Q159. MHT CET 2020 (15 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $\overline{\mathrm{a}}=2 \hat{\imath}-\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\widehat{\mathrm{k}}, \overline{\mathrm{b}}=\hat{\imath}+2 \hat{\mathrm{\jmath}}-3 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$ and $\overline{\mathrm{c}}=3 \hat{\imath}+\lambda \hat{\mathrm{\jmath}}+5 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$ are coplanar,
then $\lambda$ is the root of the equation

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x^{2}+2 x=6$
- **(B)** $x^{2}+2 x=4$
- **(C)** $x^{2}+3 x=6$
- **(D)** $x^{2}+3 x=4$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $x^{2}+3 x=4$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Since given vectors are coplanar, we write
$$
\begin{aligned}
&\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
2 & -1 & 1 \\
1 & 2 & -3 \\
3 & \lambda & 5
\end{array}\right|=0 \\
\therefore & 2(10+3 \lambda)+1(5+9)+1(\lambda-6)=0 \\
\therefore & 20+6 \lambda+14+\lambda-6=0 \Rightarrow 7 \lambda+28=0 \Rightarrow \lambda=-4
\end{aligned}
$$
Put $x=-4$ in all options.
(A) $8-12=-4 \neq 6$
(B) $16-8=8 \neq 4$
(C) $16-12=4$
(D) $16-8=8 \neq 6$

</details>

---

### Q160. MHT CET 2020 (15 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The perimeter of the triangle whose vertices have the position vectos $\hat{\imath}+\hat{\jmath}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}, 5 \hat{\imath}+3 \hat{\jmath}-3 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$ and $2 \hat{\imath}+5 \hat{\jmath}+9 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(\sqrt{15}-\sqrt{157})$ units
- **(B)** $(15+\sqrt{157})$ units
- **(C)** $(15-\sqrt{157})$ units
- **(D)** $(\sqrt{15}+\sqrt{157})$ units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $(15+\sqrt{157})$ units**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\quad \overline{\mathrm{a}}=\hat{\mathrm{i}}+\hat{\mathrm{j}}+\hat{\mathrm{k}}, \overline{\mathrm{b}}=5 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-3 \hat{\mathrm{k}}, \overline{\mathrm{c}}=2 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+5 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+9 \hat{\mathrm{k}}$
$\therefore \overline{\mathrm{AB}}=4 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}-4 \hat{\mathrm{k}} \quad \Rightarrow \mid \overline{\mathrm{AB}}=\sqrt{16+4+16}=6$
$\overline{\mathrm{BC}}=-3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+12 \hat{\mathrm{k}} \Rightarrow|\overline{\mathrm{BC}}|=\sqrt{9+4+144}=\sqrt{157}$
$\overline{\mathrm{AC}}=\hat{\mathrm{i}}+4 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+8 \hat{\mathrm{k}} \quad \Rightarrow \mid \overline{\mathrm{AC}}=\sqrt{1+16+64}=9$
Perimeter $=|\overline{\mathrm{AB}}|+|\overline{\mathrm{BC}}|+|\overline{\mathrm{AC}}|=15+\sqrt{157}$

</details>

---

### Q161. MHT CET 2020 (15 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $\mathrm{a}, \mathrm{b}, \mathrm{c}$ are non-negative distinct numbers and $\mathrm{a} \hat{\imath}+\mathrm{a} \hat{\jmath}+\mathrm{c} \hat{k}, \hat{\imath}+\hat{k}$ and $\mathrm{c} \hat{\imath}+\mathrm{c} \hat{\jmath}+\mathrm{b} \hat{k}$
are coplanar vectors, then

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{a}, \mathrm{c}, \mathrm{b}$ are in A.P.
- **(B)** $\mathrm{a}, \mathrm{b}, \mathrm{c}$ are in G.P.
- **(C)** $\mathrm{a}, \mathrm{c}, \mathrm{b}$ are in G.P.
- **(D)** $\mathrm{a}, \mathrm{b}, \mathrm{c}$ are in A.P.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\mathrm{a}, \mathrm{c}, \mathrm{b}$ are in G.P.**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given vectors are coplanar.
$$
\begin{array}{l}
\therefore\left|\begin{array}{lll}
\mathrm{a} & \mathrm{a} & \mathrm{c} \\
1 & 0 & 1 \\
\mathrm{c} & \mathrm{c} & \mathrm{b}
\end{array}\right|=0 \\
\therefore \mathrm{a}(0-\mathrm{c})-\mathrm{a}(\mathrm{b}-\mathrm{c})+\mathrm{c}(\mathrm{c}-0)=0 \Rightarrow-\mathrm{ac}-\mathrm{ab}+\mathrm{ac}+\mathrm{c}^{2}=0 \\
\therefore \mathrm{c}^{2}=\mathrm{ab} \Rightarrow \mathrm{a}, \mathrm{c}, \mathrm{b} \text { are in G.P. }
\end{array}
$$

</details>

---

### Q162. MHT CET 2020 (14 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

$\mathrm{ABCD}$ is a parallelogram, $\mathrm{P}$ is the mid-point of $\mathrm{AB}$. If $\mathrm{R}$ is the point of intersection
of $\mathrm{AC}$ and $\mathrm{DP}$, then $\mathrm{R}$ divides $\mathrm{AC}$ internally in the ratio

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3: 1$
- **(B)** $2: 1$
- **(C)** $1: 2$
- **(D)** $2: 3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $1: 2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Draw BS parallel to DP as shown
Let $A P=P B=x \Rightarrow D S=x \Rightarrow S C=x$
$\triangle B A Q \sim \triangle P A R$
$\therefore \frac{\mathrm{AB}}{\mathrm{AP}}=\frac{\mathrm{AQ}}{\mathrm{AR}} \Rightarrow \frac{2 \mathrm{x}}{\mathrm{x}}=\frac{\mathrm{AQ}}{\mathrm{AR}} \Rightarrow \mathrm{AQ}=2 \mathrm{AR}$
Thus $\mathrm{R}$ is mid point of $\mathrm{AQ}$. i.e. $\mathrm{AR}=\mathrm{RQ}$
Similarly $\Delta$ CQS $\Delta$ CRD
$\therefore \mathrm{CQ}=\mathrm{RQ}$
Thus we get $\mathrm{AR}=\mathrm{RQ}=\mathrm{CQ}$
Hence point $R$ divides $A C$ in the ratio $1: 2$

</details>

### Q163. MHT CET 2020 (13 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the vectors $\hat{i}+2 \hat{\jmath}+x \hat{k}$ and $y t+6 \hat{k}+4 \hat{k}$ are collinear, then the values of $x$ and
$y$ are respectively,

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{4}{3}, 3$
- **(B)** 3,4
- **(C)** $\frac{1}{3}, 1$
- **(D)** 4,3

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{4}{3}, 3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\bar{a} \& \bar{b}$ be given collinear vectors
$\begin{array}{l}
\therefore \overline{\mathrm{a}}=\mathrm{m} \overline{\mathrm{b}} \\
\therefore \quad \hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\mathrm{x} \hat{\mathrm{k}}=\mathrm{m}(\mathrm{yi}+6 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+4 \hat{\mathrm{k}}) \\
\therefore \quad \hat{\mathrm{i}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+\mathrm{xk}=\mathrm{my} \hat{\mathrm{i}}+6 \mathrm{~m} \hat{\mathrm{j}}+4 \mathrm{mk} \\
\therefore \quad 1=\mathrm{my}, 2=6 \mathrm{~m}, \mathrm{x}=4 \mathrm{~m} \Rightarrow \mathrm{m}=\frac{1}{3}, \mathrm{y}=3, \mathrm{x}=\frac{4}{3}
\end{array}$

</details>

### Q164. MHT CET 2020 (13 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

$\bar{a}=\hat{i}+j+\hat{k}, \bar{b}=\hat{i}-j+2 \hat{k}$ and $\bar{c}=x|+(x-1)\rangle-\hat{k}$. If the vector $\bar{c}$ lies in the plane
of $\bar{u}$ and $\bar{b}$, then $x=$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{2}{3}$
- **(B)** $\frac{-3}{2}$
- **(C)** $\frac{-2}{3}$
- **(D)** $\frac{3}{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{-3}{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given vectors are coplanar, we write : $\bar{a} \cdot(\bar{b} \times \bar{c})=0$
$\begin{aligned}
&\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
1 & 1 & 1 \\
1 & -1 & 2 \\
x & x-1 & -1
\end{array}\right|=0 \\
\therefore & 1(1-2 x+2)-1(-1-2 x)+1(x-1+x)=0 \\
& 3-2 x+1+2 x+2 x-1=0 \Rightarrow 2 x+3=0 \Rightarrow x=\frac{-3}{2}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q165. MHT CET 2020 (13 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $\overline{A B}=3 \hat{\imath}+5 \hat{\jmath}+4 \hat{k}, \overline{A C}=5 \hat{\imath}-5 \hat{\jmath}+2 \hat{k}$ represent the sides of triangle $\mathrm{ABC}$, then
the length of median through $\mathrm{A}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{6}$ units
- **(B)** 5 units
- **(C)** $\sqrt{5}$ units
- **(D)** 6 units

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 5 units**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given
$\begin{aligned}
\frac{\overline{\mathrm{AB}}}{\mathrm{AC}} &=3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+5 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+4 \hat{\mathrm{k}} \\
&=5 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-5 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{k}}
\end{aligned}$
Let $\overline{\mathrm{AD}}$ is median
position vector of
$\begin{aligned}
\overline{\mathrm{AD}} &=\frac{(3 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+5 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+4 \hat{\mathrm{k}})+(5 \hat{\mathrm{i}}-5 \hat{\mathrm{j}}+2 \hat{\mathrm{k}})}{2} \\
\overline{\mathrm{AD}} &=4 \hat{\mathrm{i}}+3 \hat{\mathrm{k}} \\
\therefore|\overline{\mathrm{AD}}| &=\sqrt{16+9}=\sqrt{25}=5
\end{aligned}$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/mht_cet/5B7amXIty_Psiue0bctk51X8L2_SgtD45Pxn0AEo8KU.original.fullsize.png">

</details>

---

### Q166. MHT CET 2020 (13 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

If $\mathrm{a}, \mathrm{b}, \mathrm{c}$ are lengths of the sides $\mathrm{BC}, \mathrm{CA}, \mathrm{AB}$ respectively of $\triangle \mathrm{ABC}$ and $\mathrm{H}$ is any
point in the plane of $\Delta \mathrm{ABC}$ such that a $\overline{A H}+b \overline{B H}+c \overline{C H}=\overline{0}$, then $\mathrm{H}$ is the

**Options:**

- **(A)** Circumcentre of $\Delta \mathrm{ABC}$
- **(B)** Incentre of $\Delta \mathrm{ABC}$
- **(C)** Centroid of $\Delta \mathrm{ABC}$
- **(D)** Orthocentre of $\Delta \mathrm{ABC}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Incentre of $\Delta \mathrm{ABC}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/mht_cet/Och2pe66KYp16xRV-4dhAXaGTdjUWpOVLJFYwe75iFY.original.fullsize.png">

Consider $\mathrm{H}$ to be origin.
Then position vector of the vertices $A, B, C$ are $\bar{a}, \bar{b}, \bar{c}$ respectively.
We have $\mathrm{a} \overline{\mathrm{AH}}+\mathrm{b} \overline{\mathrm{BH}}+\mathrm{c} \overline{\mathrm{CH}}=\overline{0}$ i.e. $\quad \mathrm{a} \overline{\mathrm{a}}+\mathrm{b} \overline{\mathrm{b}}+\mathrm{c} \overline{\mathrm{c}}=0$
i.e. $\frac{a \bar{a}+b \bar{b}+c \bar{c}}{a+b+c}=0$, which is position vector of incentre.
Hence $\mathrm{H}$ is incentre of triangle.

</details>

### Q167. MHT CET 2020 (13 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the vectors $\bar{a}=\hat{\imath}-2 \hat{\jmath}+\hat{k}, \bar{b}=2 \hat{\imath}-5 \hat{\jmath}+p \hat{k}$ and $\bar{c}=5 \hat{\imath}-9 \hat{\jmath}+4 \hat{k}$ are coplanar,
then the value of $\mathrm{p}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-3$
- **(B)** 3
- **(C)** $\frac{1}{3}$
- **(D)** $-\frac{1}{3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 3**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given vectors are coplanar $\Rightarrow \bar{a} \cdot(\bar{b} \times \bar{c})=0$
$\begin{aligned}
&\left|\begin{array}{rrr}
1 & -2 & 1 \\
2 & -5 & \mathrm{P} \\
5 & -9 & 4
\end{array}\right|=0 \\
\therefore & 1(-20+9 \mathrm{P})+2(8-5 \mathrm{P})+1(-18+25)=0 \\
&-20+9 \mathrm{P}+16-10 \mathrm{P}+7=0 \Rightarrow-\mathrm{P}+3=0 \Rightarrow \quad \mathrm{P}=3
\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q168. MHT CET 2020 (12 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

If $\bar{a}, \bar{b}, \bar{c}, \bar{d}$ are the position vectors of the points $A, B, C, D$ respectively such that
$3 \bar{a}-\bar{b}+2 \bar{c}-4 \bar{d}=\overline{0}$, then the position vector of the point of intersection of the line
segments $A C$ and $B D$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{\bar{b}+3 \bar{d}}{4}$
- **(B)** $\frac{3 \bar{a}+\bar{c}}{4}$
- **(C)** $\frac{\bar{a}+\bar{c}}{2}$
- **(D)** $\frac{\bar{b}+4 \bar{d}}{5}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\frac{\bar{b}+4 \bar{d}}{5}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $3 \bar{a}-\bar{b}+2 \bar{c}-4 \bar{d}$
$\begin{aligned} 3 \bar{a}+2 \bar{c} &=\bar{b}+4 \bar{d} \\ \therefore & \frac{3 \bar{a}+2 \bar{c}}{3+2} \end{aligned}=\frac{\bar{b}+4 \bar{d}}{1+4}$
$\therefore \frac{3 \bar{a}+2 \bar{c}}{5}=\frac{\bar{b}+4 \bar{d}}{5}$

</details>

### Q169. MHT CET 2019 (Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the vectors <math><mi>x</mi><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></math> and <math><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></math> are collinear then the value of <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><msup><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow></mfrac></math> is equal

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>9</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>9</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given, vectors <math><mi>x</mi><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></math> and <math><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></math>  
are collinear 
<math><mo>∴</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi></math>  
<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>y</mi><mi></mi><mi>a</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>d</mi><mi></mi><mn>7</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>z</mi></math>  
Now, <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><msup><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>k</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q170. MHT CET 2018

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<math><mi>L</mi></math> and <math><mi>M</mi></math> are two points with position vectors <math> <mrow> <mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"> <mi>a</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> <mo>−</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>b</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> </mrow> </math> and <math> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>a</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"> <mi>b</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> </mrow> </math> respectively. The position vector of the point <math><mi>N</mi></math> which divides the line segment <math><mi>L</mi><mi>M</mi></math> in the ratio <math><mn>2</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math> externally is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math > <mrow>  <mn>3</mn><mover accent='true'>   <mi>b</mi>   <mo stretchy='true'>&#x2192;</mo>  </mover>   </mrow></math>
- **(B)** <math > <mrow>  <mn>4</mn><mover accent='true'>   <mi>b</mi>   <mo stretchy='true'>&#x2192;</mo>  </mover>   </mrow></math>
- **(C)** <math > <mrow>  <mn>5</mn><mover accent='true'>   <mi>b</mi>   <mo stretchy='true'>&#x2192;</mo>  </mover>   </mrow></math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <mn>3</mn><mover accent="true"> <mi>a</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mover accent="true"> <mi>b</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math > <mrow>  <mn>5</mn><mover accent='true'>   <mi>b</mi>   <mo stretchy='true'>&#x2192;</mo>  </mover>   </mrow></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Since point <math> <mrow> <mtext>N</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>n</mtext> <mo>¯</mo> </mover> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math> divides LM in ratio 2:1 externally
<math> <mrow> <mo>⇒</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>n</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> <mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>m</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>−</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>l</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn><mo>−</mo><mi>l</mi></mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>     (Using section formula)
<math> <mo>⇒</mo><mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>n</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> <mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>a</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"> <mi>b</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>−</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"> <mi>a</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> <mo>−</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>b</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow> </math>
<math> <mo>⇒</mo><mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>n</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> <mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"> <mi>a</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mover accent="true"> <mi>b</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> <mo>−</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"> <mi>a</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>b</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> </mrow> </math>
<math> <mo>⇒</mo><mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>n</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> <mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mover accent="true"> <mi>b</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q171. MHT CET 2017

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If vector <math> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>r</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> </mrow> </math> with direction cosine <math> <mrow> <mi>l</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi> </mrow> </math> is equally inclined to the co-ordinate axes, then the total number of such vectors is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 4
- **(B)** 6
- **(C)** 8
- **(D)** 2

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 8**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>±</mo><mi> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>±</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>±</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mi> </mi></math>
For equally inclined to co-ordinate axes.
<math> <mrow> <mo>⇒</mo><mrow><mo>|</mo> <mi>l</mi> <mo>|</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo>|</mo> <mi>m</mi> <mo>|</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo>|</mo> <mi>n</mi> <mo>|</mo></mrow></mrow> </math>
<math><mo>⇒</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="script"><mi>l</mi></mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
<math> <mo>⇒</mo> <mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="script"><mi>l</mi></mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
<math><mo>⇒</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="script"><mi>l</mi></mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math><mo>⇒</mo> <mi mathvariant="script"><mi>l</mi></mi><mo>=</mo><mo>±</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>√</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>⇒</mo><mi mathvariant="script"><mi>l</mi></mi><mo>,</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi><mi> </mi></math> each has 2 choices.
<math><mo>∴</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi></math> Total lines <math><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let <math> <mrow> <mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>S</mi> </mrow> </math> be a quadrilateral. If <math> <mi>M</mi> </math> and <math> <mi>N</mi> </math> are midpoints of the sides <math> <mrow> <mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi> </mrow> </math> and <math> <mrow> <mi>R</mi><mi>S</mi> </mrow> </math> respectively then <math> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mrow> <mi>P</mi><mi>S</mi> </mrow> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"> <mrow> <mi>Q</mi><mi>R</mi> </mrow> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> <mo>=</mo> </mrow> </math>

- **(A)** <math > <mrow>  <mn>3</mn><mtext>&#x2009;</mtext><mover accent='true'>   <mrow>    <mi>M</mi><mi>N</mi>   </mrow>   <mo stretchy='true'>&#x2192;</mo>  </mover>   </mrow></math>
- **(B)** <math > <mrow>  <mn>4</mn><mtext>&#x2009;</mtext><mover accent='true'>   <mrow>    <mi>M</mi><mi>N</mi>   </mrow>   <mo stretchy='true'>&#x2192;</mo>  </mover>   </mrow></math>
- **(C)** <math > <mrow>  <mn>2</mn><mtext>&#x2009;</mtext><mover accent='true'>   <mrow>    <mi>M</mi><mi>N</mi>   </mrow>   <mo stretchy='true'>&#x2192;</mo>  </mover>   </mrow></math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <mn>5</mn><mtext> </mtext><mover accent="true"> <mrow> <mi>M</mi><mi>N</mi> </mrow> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>s</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mrow> <mi>P</mi><mi>S</mi></mrow> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"> <mrow> <mi>Q</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> <mo>=</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>S</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> <mo>−</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>p</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>+</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>r</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> <mo>−</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>q</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow> </math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>s</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mo>(</mo><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>q</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>)</mo></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>)</mo></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>M</mi><mi>N</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover></math>

</details>

### Q173. MHT CET 2017

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the origin and the points <math> <mrow> <mi>P</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>,</mo><mi>Q</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow> </mrow> </math> and <math> <mrow> <mi>R</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>z</mi> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow> </mrow> </math> are co-planar then

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math > <mrow>  <mi>O</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>R</mi> </mrow></math>are co-planar
<math> <mo>⇒</mo> </math> <math><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi> </mi><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>O</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>O</mi><mi>P</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>O</mi><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mi> </mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mi> </mi></math>
<math> <mo>⇒</mo> </math> <math><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mtable><mtr><mtd><mi>x</mi></mtd><mtd><mi>y</mi></mtd><mtd><mi>z</mi></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>3</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>4</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>3</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
<math> <mo>⇒</mo> </math> <math><mi>x</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>9</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
<math> <mo>⇒</mo> </math> <math><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
<b>Alternative</b>
Points <math > <mrow>  <mi>P</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>Q</mi> </mrow></math> satisfy equations given in option.

</details>

### Q174. MHT CET 2016

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If <math> <mi>G</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>g</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>,</mo><mi>H</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>h</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow> </math> and <math> <mrow> <mi>P</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>p</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow> </mrow> </math> are centroid, orthocenter and circumcenter of a triangle and <math> <mi>x</mi><mover accent="true"> <mi>p</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mover accent="true"> <mi>h</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mover accent="true"> <mi>g</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> <mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn> </math> then <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mi>y</mi><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mi>z</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>= </mo></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>)</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>)</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

We know that, in a triangle centroid (G), orthocenter (H) and circumcenter (P) are always collinear and G divides the line segment PH in ratio 1:2
So using section formula, <math> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>g</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> <mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"> <mi>p</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>h</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> </mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
<math> <mrow> <mo>⇒</mo><mn>3</mn><mover accent="true"> <mi>g</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> <mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"> <mi>p</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>h</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> </mrow> </math>
<math> <mrow> <mo>⇒</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"> <mi>p</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>h</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> <mo>−</mo><mn>3</mn><mover accent="true"> <mi>g</mi> <mo stretchy="true">¯</mo> </mover> <mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q175. MHT CET 2016

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

If line joining points <math><mi>A</mi></math> and <math><mi>B</mi></math> having positive vectors <math><mn>6</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover></math> and <math><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover></math> respectively, and the line joining the points <math><mi>C</mi></math> and <math><mi>D</mi></math> having positive vectors <math><mo>-</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover></math> and <math><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover></math> intersect, then their point of intersection is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math > <mi>B</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math > <mi>C</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math > <mi>D</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math > <mi>A</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math > <mi>B</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>λ</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mn>6</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover> <mo>−</mo><mn>4</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover> <mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>+</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mn>4</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow> <mrow> <mi>λ</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <mi>μ</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mo>−</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover> <mo>−</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover> <mo>−</mo><mn>3</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>+</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover> <mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover> <mo>−</mo><mn>5</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow> <mrow> <mi>μ</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
Comparing coefficients of <math> <mrow> <mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover> <mo>,</mo><mtext> </mtext><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover> <mo>,</mo><mtext> </mtext><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover> </mrow> </math> on both sides, we get
<math> <mrow> <mi>λ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mtext> </mtext><mi>μ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow> </math>
<math> <mrow> <mo>⇒</mo><mi>B</mi></mrow> </math> is the point of intersection

</details>

- **(B)** <math > <mn>2</mn><mover accent='true'>  <mrow>   <mi>N</mi><mi>M</mi>  </mrow>  <mo stretchy='true'>&#x2192;</mo> </mover> </math>

**Detailed Solution:**

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/mht_cet/EM0317805.JPG" style="width: 250px; height: 234px;" />
<math><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>A</mi><mi>D</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>B</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>D</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mo>…</mo></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mo>(</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>M</mi><mi>N</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover></math>

### Q177. MHT CET 2016

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If <math> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>a</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> <mo>=</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>i</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>j</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> <mo>−</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"> <mi>k</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> <mo>,</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>b</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> <mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"> <mi>i</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> <mo>−</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>j</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>k</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> </mrow> </math> and <math> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>c</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> <mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mover accent="true"> <mi>i</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> <mo>−</mo><mover accent="true"> <mi>k</mi> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> </mrow> </math>. If <math> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mi>c</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> <mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mover accent="true"> <mi>a</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo><mi>n</mi><mover accent="true"> <mi>b</mi> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> </mrow> </math> then <math><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math > <mn>0</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math > <mn>1</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math > <mn>2</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math > <mrow>  <mo>&#x2212;</mo><mn>1</mn> </mrow></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math > <mn>2</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>c</mtext> <mo>¯</mo> </mover> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>m</mtext> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>a</mtext> <mo>¯</mo> </mover> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>n</mtext> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>b</mtext> <mo>¯</mo> </mover> </mrow> </math>
<math><mo>⇒</mo><mn>3</mn><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>n</mi><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mi>n</mi><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mi>n</mi><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover></math>
<math><mo>⇒</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mn>3</mn><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>n</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover accent="false"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>¯</mo></mover></math>
<math><mo>∴</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math> ...... (i)
<math><mi>m</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> ...... (ii)
<math><mi>n</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></math> .... (iii)
As <math><mi>m</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
from (ii)
<math><mo>∴</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi></math>
<math><mo>∴</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mn>3</mn><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math>
<math><mo>∴</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi> </mi><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
<math><mo>∴</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math>

</details>

### Q178. MHT CET 2012

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $\mathbf{a}+\mathbf{b}+\mathbf{c}=\mathbf{0}$ and $|\mathbf{a}|=5,|\mathbf{b}|=3$ and $|\mathbf{c}|=7$
then angle between $\mathbf{a}$ and $\mathbf{b}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{\pi}{2}$
- **(B)** $\frac{\pi}{3}$
- **(C)** $\frac{\pi}{4}$
- **(D)** $\frac{\pi}{6}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{\pi}{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given, $\mathbf{a}+\mathbf{b}+\mathbf{c}=\mathbf{0}$
and $|\mathbf{a}|=5,|\mathbf{b}|=3,|\mathbf{c}|=7$
$\Rightarrow \quad \mathbf{a}+\mathbf{b}=-\mathbf{c}$
On squaring both sides, we get $(\mathbf{a}+\mathbf{b})^{2}=(-\mathbf{c})^{2}$
$\Rightarrow$
$|\mathbf{a}+\mathbf{b}|^{2}=|\mathbf{c}|^{2}$
$\Rightarrow|\mathbf{a}|^{2}+|\mathbf{b}|^{2}+2 \mathbf{a} \cdot \mathbf{b}=|\mathbf{c}|^{2}$
$(\because \theta$ be the angle between $\mathbf{a}$ and $\mathbf{b}$ )
$\Rightarrow(5)^{2}+(3)^{2}+2|\mathbf{a}||\mathbf{b}| \cos \theta=(7)^{2}$
$\Rightarrow \quad 25+9+2 \cdot 5 \cdot 3 \cdot \cos \theta=49$
$\Rightarrow \quad 30 \cos \theta=15$
$\Rightarrow \quad \cos \theta=\frac{1}{2}=\cos 60^{\circ}$
$\Rightarrow \quad \theta=\frac{\pi}{3}$

</details>

---

### Q179. MHT CET 2012

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If in a $\Delta A B C, \mathbf{O}$ and $\mathbf{O}^{\prime}$ are the incentre and orthocentre respectively, then $\left(\boldsymbol{O}^{\prime} \mathbf{A}+\boldsymbol{O}^{\prime} \mathbf{B}\right.$
$\left.+\mathbf{O}^{\prime} \mathbf{C}\right)$ is equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2 \mathbf{O}^{\prime} \mathbf{O}$
- **(B)** $0^{\prime} 0$
- **(C)** $00^{\prime}$
- **(D)** 2 OO

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $2 \mathbf{O}^{\prime} \mathbf{O}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

O' $\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{O}^{\prime} \mathbf{O}+\mathbf{O A}$
$\mathbf{O}^{\prime} \mathbf{B}=\mathbf{O}^{\prime} \mathbf{O}+\mathbf{O B}$
$\mathbf{O}^{\prime} \mathbf{C}=\mathbf{O}^{\prime} \mathbf{O}+\mathbf{O C}$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/mht_cet/IK-nfwTpfNQpBGiI03ECR8PPzo9KVp05IFGWmJKiOiI.original.fullsize.png">

$\Rightarrow \mathbf{O}^{\prime} \mathbf{A}+\mathbf{O}^{\prime} \mathbf{B}+\mathbf{O}^{\prime} \mathbf{C}$
$=3 \mathbf{0} \mathbf{0}+(\mathbf{O} \mathbf{A}+\mathbf{O B}+\mathbf{O C}) \ldots(\mathrm{i})$
$\because \quad \mathbf{O A}+\mathbf{O B}+\mathbf{O C}=\mathbf{O O}^{\prime}=-\mathbf{O}^{\prime} \mathbf{O}$
$\therefore \quad \mathbf{O}^{\prime} \mathbf{A}+\mathbf{O}^{\prime} \mathbf{B}+\mathbf{O}^{\prime} \mathbf{C}=3 \mathbf{O}^{\prime} \mathbf{O}-\mathbf{O}^{\prime} \mathbf{O}$
[from Eq. (i)]
$\mathbf{O}^{\prime} \mathbf{A}+\mathbf{O}^{\prime} \mathbf{B}+\mathbf{O}^{\prime} \mathbf{C}=2 \mathbf{O}^{\prime} \mathbf{O}$

</details>

### Q180. MHT CET 2012

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The vector perpendicular to the vectors $4 \mathbf{i}-\mathbf{j}+3 \mathbf{k}$ and $-2 \mathbf{i}+\mathbf{j}-2 \mathbf{k}$ whose magnitude
is 9

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3 \mathbf{i}+6 \mathbf{j}-6 \mathbf{k}$
- **(B)** $3 \mathbf{i}-6 \mathbf{j}+6 \mathbf{k}$
- **(C)** $-3 \mathbf{i}+6 \mathbf{j}+6 \mathbf{k}$
- **(D)** None of the above

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $-3 \mathbf{i}+6 \mathbf{j}+6 \mathbf{k}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\mathbf{a}=4 \mathbf{i}-\mathbf{j}+3 \mathbf{k}, \mathbf{b}=-2 \mathbf{i}+\mathbf{j}-2 \mathbf{k}$
and $\quad \mathbf{c}=x \mathbf{i}+y \mathbf{j}+z \mathbf{k}$
Given, $\quad$ a $\cdot \mathbf{c}=0$
i.e., $\quad 4 x-y+3 z=0$...(i)
and $\quad$ b. $\mathbf{c}=0$
i.e., $-2 x+y-2 z=0$...(ii)
Also, $|\mathbf{c}|=9$
i.e., $x^{2}+y^{2}+z^{2}=81$...(iii)
Now, from Eqs. (i) and (ii), we get
$$
2 x+z=0 \Rightarrow z=-2 x
$$
On putting this value in Eq. (iii), we get
$$
\begin{aligned}
x^{2}+y^{2}+4 x^{2} &=81 \\
\Rightarrow \quad 5 x^{2}+y^{2} &=81...(iv)
\end{aligned}
$$
On multiplying Eq. (i) by 2 and Eq. (ii) by 3 and then adding, we get
$$
\begin{array}{r}
8 x-2 y+6 z=0 \\
\frac{-6 x+3 y-6 z=0}{2 x+y=0} \\
\Rightarrow \quad y=-2 x
\end{array}
$$
On putting this value in Eq. (iv), we get
$$
5 x^{2}+4 x^{2}=81
$$
$$
\begin{array}{lcc}
\Rightarrow & 9 x^{2}=81 \\
\Rightarrow & x^{2}=9 \\
\Rightarrow & x=\pm 3 \\
\therefore & y=\mp 6 & \text { and } z=\mp 6
\end{array}
$$
$\therefore$ Required vector, $\mathbf{c}=x \mathbf{i}+y \mathbf{j}+z \mathbf{k}$
$$
\begin{array}{l}
=\pm 3 \mathbf{i} \mp 6 \mathbf{j} \mp 6 \mathbf{k} \\
=3 \mathbf{i}-6 \mathbf{j}-6 \mathbf{k}
\end{array}
$$
or
$$
=-3 \mathbf{i}+6 \mathbf{j}+6 \mathbf{k}
$$

</details>

---

### Q181. MHT CET 2012

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If three vectors $2 \mathbf{i}-\mathbf{j}-\mathbf{k}, \mathbf{i}+2 \mathbf{j}-3 \mathbf{k}$ and
$3 \mathbf{i}+\lambda \mathbf{j}+5 \mathbf{k}$ are coplanar, then the value of $\lambda$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-4$
- **(B)** $-2$
- **(C)** $-1$
- **(D)** $-8$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $-8$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\quad \mathbf{a}=2 \mathbf{i}-\mathbf{j}-\mathbf{k}$,
$$
\mathbf{b}=\mathbf{i}+2 \mathbf{j}-3 \mathbf{k}
$$
and
$$
\mathbf{c}=3 \mathbf{i}+\lambda \mathbf{j}+5 \mathbf{k}
$$
If these vectors are coplanar, then $[\mathbf{a} \mathbf{b} \mathbf{c}]=0$
$$
\begin{array}{l}
\Rightarrow \quad\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
2 & -1 & -1 \\
1 & 2 & -3 \\
3 & \lambda & 5
\end{array}\right|=0 \\
\Rightarrow \quad 2(10+3 \lambda)+(5+9)-(\lambda-6)=0 \\
\Rightarrow 20+6 \lambda+14-\lambda+6=0 \\
\Rightarrow  5 \lambda+40=0 \\
\Rightarrow  \lambda=-8
\end{array}
$$

</details>

---

### Q182. MHT CET 2011

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A vector $v$ is equally inclined to the $x$ -axis, $y$ -axis and $z$ -axis respectively, its direction cosines are

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left. < \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}, \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}, \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\right\rangle$
- **(B)** $\left. < -\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}},-\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}},-\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\right\rangle$
- **(C)** $ < \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}, \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}, \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}>$ or $ < -\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}},-\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}},-\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}>$
- **(D)** None of the above

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $ < \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}, \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}, \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}>$ or $ < -\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}},-\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}},-\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}>$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the vector $\mathbf{v}$ make an angle $\alpha$ with each of the three axes, then direction cosine of $\mathbf{v}$ are
$$
\begin{array}{l}
\quad < \cos \alpha, \cos \alpha, \cos \alpha> \\
\text { Also, } \quad \cos ^{2} \alpha+\cos ^{2} \alpha+\cos ^{2} \alpha=1 \\
\Rightarrow \quad \cos ^{2} \alpha=1 / 3 \\
\Rightarrow \quad \cos \alpha=\pm \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}
\end{array}
$$
Hence, direction cosine of $\mathbf{v}$ are
$$ < \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}, \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}, \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}>
$$
Or
$$ < -\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}},-\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}},-\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}>
$$

</details>

---

### Q183. MHT CET 2011

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the vectors $\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}$ and $\mathbf{c}$ are coplanar, then $\left|\begin{array}{ccc}a & b & c \\ a \cdot a & a \cdot b & a \cdot c \\ b \cdot a & b \cdot b & b \cdot c\end{array}\right|$ is equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1
- **(B)** $\underline{0}$
- **(C)** $-1$
- **(D)** None of these

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\underline{0}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Since, $\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}$ and $\mathbf{c}$ are coplanar, there must exists three scalars $x, y$ and $z$ are not all zero such that
$$
x \mathbf{a}+y \mathbf{b}+z \mathbf{c}=0
$$
Multiplying both sides of Eq. (i) by a and $\mathbf{b}$ respectively, we get
$$
\begin{array}{l}
x \mathbf{a} \cdot \mathbf{a}+y \mathbf{a} \cdot \mathbf{b}+z \mathbf{a} \cdot \mathbf{c}=0 \\
x \mathbf{b} \cdot \mathbf{a}+y \mathbf{b} \cdot \mathbf{b}+z \mathbf{b} \cdot \mathbf{c}=0
\end{array}
$$
Eliminating $x, y$ and $z$ from Eqs. (i), (ii) and
(iii), we get
$$
\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
\mathbf{a} & \mathbf{b} & \mathbf{c} \\
\mathbf{a} \cdot \mathbf{a} & \mathbf{a} \cdot \mathbf{b} & \mathbf{a} \cdot \mathbf{c} \\
\mathbf{b} \cdot \mathbf{a} & \mathbf{b} \cdot \mathbf{b} & \mathbf{b} \cdot \mathbf{c}
\end{array}\right|=0
$$

</details>

### Q184. MHT CET 2009

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $2 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}+3 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}-5 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}=\overrightarrow{\mathbf{0}}$, then ratio in which $\overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}$
divides $\overrightarrow{\mathbf{A B}}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3: 2$ internally
- **(B)** $3: 2$ externally
- **(C)** $2: 3$ internally
- **(D)** $2: 3$ externally

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $3: 2$ internally**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given, $2 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}+3 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}-5 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}=0$
$\Rightarrow \frac{2 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}+3 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}}{5}=\overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}$
$\Rightarrow \frac{2 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}+3 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}}{2+3}=\overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}$
$\Rightarrow \quad \frac{\overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}+\frac{3}{2} \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}}{1+\frac{3}{2}}=\overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/mht_cet/iuMvNwdfra6ZjTxqVtVIZ0wlRZgpY5eiF-OblK1u1S4.original.fullsize.png">

Let $\overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}$ divide $\overrightarrow{\mathbf{A B}}$ in the ratio $\lambda: 1$
Then, $\overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}=\frac{\overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}+\lambda \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}}{1+\lambda}$
On comparing Eqs. (i) and (ii), we get $\lambda=\frac{3}{2}$
$\therefore$ Required ratio is $3: 2$ internally.

</details>

---

### Q185. MHT CET 2009

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Given $\overrightarrow{\mathbf{p}}=3 \hat{\mathbf{i}}+2 \hat{\mathbf{j}}+4 \hat{\mathbf{k}}, \overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}=\hat{\mathbf{i}}+\hat{\mathbf{j}} \cdot \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}=\hat{\mathbf{j}}+\hat{\mathbf{k}}$
$\overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}=\hat{\mathbf{i}}+\hat{\mathbf{k}}$ and $\overrightarrow{\mathbf{p}}=x \overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}+y \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}+z \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}$, then $x, y, z$
are respectively

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{3}{2}, \frac{1}{2}, \frac{5}{2}$
- **(B)** $\frac{1}{2}, \frac{3}{2}, \frac{5}{2}$
- **(C)** $\frac{5}{2}, \frac{3}{2}, \frac{1}{2}$
- **(D)** $\frac{1}{2}, \frac{5}{2}, \frac{3}{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{1}{2}, \frac{3}{2}, \frac{5}{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\text {  } \begin{aligned}
& \overrightarrow{\mathbf{p}}=x \overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}+y \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}+z \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}} \\
& \Rightarrow 3 \hat{\mathbf{i}}+2 \hat{\mathbf{j}}+4 \hat{\mathbf{k}}=x(\hat{\mathbf{i}}+\hat{\mathbf{j}})+y(\hat{\mathbf{j}}+\hat{\mathbf{k}})+z(\hat{\mathbf{i}}+\hat{\mathbf{k}}) \\
& \Rightarrow 3 \hat{\mathbf{i}}+2 \hat{\mathbf{j}}+4 \hat{\mathbf{k}}=(x+z) \hat{\mathbf{i}}+(x+y) \hat{\mathbf{j}}+(y+z) \hat{\mathbf{k}}
\end{aligned}
$$
On comparing both sides the coefficients of $\hat{\mathbf{i}}, \hat{\mathbf{j}}, \hat{\mathbf{k}}$, we get
$$
x+z=3
$$
$$
x+y=2 \quad \ldots
$$
$$
\text { and } \quad y+z=4 \quad \ldots \text { (iii) }
$$
On solving Eqs. (i), (ii) and (iii), we get
$$
x=\frac{1}{2}, \quad y=\frac{3}{2}, \quad z=\frac{5}{2}
$$

</details>

---

### Q186. MHT CET 2008

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The line joining the points $6 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}-4 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}+4 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}},-4 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}$ and the line joining the
points $-\overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}-2 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}-3 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}, \overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}+2 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}-5 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}$ intersect
at

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-4 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}$
- **(B)** $4 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}-\overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}-\overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}$
- **(C)** $4 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}$
- **(D)** None of these

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) None of these**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equations of the lines joining $6 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}-4 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}+4 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}},-4 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}} \quad$ and
$-\overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}-2 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}-3 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}, \overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}+2 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}-5 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}$ are respectively
$$
\overrightarrow{\mathbf{r}}=6 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}-4 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}+4 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}+m(-6 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}+4 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}-8 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}) ...(i)
$$
and $\overrightarrow{\mathbf{r}}=-\overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}-2 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}-3 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}+n(2 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}+4 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}-2 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}})$ ...(ii)
For the point of intersection, the Eqs. (i) and
(ii) should give the same value of $\overrightarrow{\mathbf{r}}$. Hence equating the coefficients of vectors $\overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}, \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}$ and $\overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}$ in the two expressions for $\overrightarrow{\mathbf{r}}$, we get
$$
\begin{array}{l}
6 m+2 n=7 \\
2 m-2 n=1
\end{array}
$$
and
$$
8 m-2 n=7
$$
On solving Eqs. (iii) and (iv), we get $m=1, n=\frac{1}{2} .$ These values of $m$ and $n$, also satisfy the Eq. (v).
$\therefore$ The lines intersect. Putting the value of $m$ in Eq. (i), we get the position vector of the point of intersection as $-4 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}$.

</details>

---

### Q187. MHT CET 2007

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the vectors $\overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}+\lambda \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}+3 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}},-2 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}+3 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}-4 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}$
and $\overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}-3 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}+5 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}$ are coplanar, then the value of $\lambda$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2
- **(B)** $-1$
- **(C)** 1
- **(D)** $-2$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $-2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Since, the given three vectors are coplanar, therefore one of them should be expressible as a linear combination of the remaining two ie, there exist two scalars $x$ and $y$ such that
$$
\begin{aligned}
\overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}+\lambda \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}+3 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}=x(-2 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}+3&\overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}-4 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}) \\
+& y(\overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}-3 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}+5 \overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}})
\end{aligned}
$$
On comparing the coefficient of $\overrightarrow{\mathbf{a}}, \overrightarrow{\mathbf{b}}$ and $\overrightarrow{\mathbf{c}}$ on both sides, we get
$$
-2 x+y=1 ; 3 x-3 y=\lambda
$$
and $-4 x+5 y=3$
On solving first and third equations, we get
$$
x=-\frac{1}{3}, y=\frac{1}{3}
$$
Since, the vectors are coplanar, therefore these values of $x$ and $y$, also satisfy the second equation ie, $-1-1=\lambda$
$$
\therefore \quad \lambda=-2
$$

</details>

---
